## [AMD acquires Taalas to hardwire AI models into silicon](https://www.theregister.com/systems/2026/08/06/amd-acquires-ai-chip-startup-taalas-to-boost-inference-performance-by-etching-models-into-silicon/5284344) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AMD has agreed to acquire Taalas, an AI inference chip startup co-founded by former Tenstorrent CEO Ljubisa Bajic, for an undisclosed amount. Taalas's accelerators are customized to hardwire a single AI model directly into the silicon, eliminating the need for HBM to store model weights. This acquisition strengthens AMD's position in the rapidly growing AI inference market, potentially offering lower-cost and higher-performance inference solutions. It could intensify competition with NVIDIA and other AI hardware players, and may reshape the AI hardware landscape by enabling more efficient model deployment. Taalas's chips etch model weights directly into silicon, avoiding the need for HBM, which could reduce memory bottlenecks and power consumption. The deal is part of AMD's broader AI strategy, with Taalas team joining under Vamsi Boppana's AI organization. However, the approach may face challenges due to rapid model iteration, as silicon-etched models could become outdated quickly.

hackernews · itvision · Aug 6, 20:23 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49201970)

**Background**: AI inference is the process of running trained models to make predictions, and it is a growing market as AI applications proliferate. Traditional AI accelerators like GPUs store model weights in HBM, which can be a bottleneck. Taalas's approach of hardwiring models into silicon is a radical alternative that could offer performance and efficiency gains. AMD has been competing with NVIDIA in AI hardware, and this acquisition is a strategic move to differentiate its inference offerings.

The article applies the Pareto frontier concept to analyze character trade-offs in Mario Kart, showing how drivers balance speed and acceleration. It demonstrates that optimal choices lie on the frontier, where improving one stat degrades another. This provides a practical framework for game designers to evaluate character balance and for players to make informed choices. It also illustrates a broader decision-making principle applicable to any multi-objective optimization problem. The analysis likely uses data on character stats from Mario Kart games, plotting speed against acceleration to identify the Pareto frontier. Community comments mention speedrun strategies favoring edge characters like Bowser, and similar analyses in WoW item optimization.

**Background**: The Pareto frontier, named after economist Vilfredo Pareto, is a set of solutions where no objective can be improved without worsening another. In multi-objective optimization, it helps designers focus on trade-offs among efficient choices. This concept is widely used in engineering and economics, and here it is applied to game character selection.

The essay 'Taste Is All That's Left' argues that as AI automates technical skills, human taste and judgment become the key differentiators in software development. It has sparked a rich community discussion with 155 points and 120 comments. This matters because it addresses a profound shift in the software engineering profession: as AI handles more coding tasks, the value of human intuition and aesthetic judgment rises. It affects developers, tech leaders, and anyone concerned about the future of work in an AI-augmented industry. The discussion includes philosophical quotes, such as Susan Sontag's 'Notes on Camp,' and practical thought experiments about AI automating aspects of work. Commenters like mdwelsh, who has coded since the 1980s, question whether agent-built software has real intuition or judgment, while boron1006 notes LLMs may not scale well over long projects.

**Background**: The essay is part of a broader discourse on AI's impact on software engineering, where tools like large language models (LLMs) are increasingly capable of generating code. Historically, technical skill was a primary differentiator for developers, but as AI lowers the barrier to coding, human qualities like taste—defined as the ability to make discerning, aesthetic, and ethical judgments—become more valuable. This aligns with philosophical views that taste governs free human responses and is a form of intelligence.

**Discussion**: The community sentiment is largely positive and thoughtful, with many resonating with the essay's thesis. ## [Datasette 1.0a38 fixes SQL injection in mixed public/private setups](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Aug/6/datasette/#atom-everything) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Datasette 1.0a38 fixes a SQL injection vulnerability that could allow users with access to public tables to read private tables in the same database. The fix is also backported to Datasette 0.65.3. This security fix is critical for Datasette instances that serve a mix of public and private tables, as it prevents unauthorized read access to sensitive data. It underscores the importance of promptly patching even in rare configurations. The vulnerability affects instances using the Datasette permissions system with mixed public/private tables. Administrators are advised to disable the execute-sql permission on such databases to mitigate the issue, though the bug is now fixed.

rss · Simon Willison · Aug 6, 18:24

**Background**: Datasette is a tool for publishing data as an interactive website, with a built-in permissions system to control access to databases and tables. The execute-sql permission allows users to run raw SQL queries, which can be restricted to prevent access to private tables. This vulnerability bypassed that restriction via SQL injection.

<a id="item-7"></a>
## [Self-Verifying Agent Architecture Separates Planning from Execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04066) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a self-verifying agent instrument where a deterministic executive owns all belief, and language model proposals are only admitted when pre-registered predictions match observations by code. The architecture structurally separates planning from execution, with self-invalidation on breaches of per-organ write-error, render-size, or salted-canary-echo floors. This addresses a critical trust issue in long-horizon agents by making verification structural rather than post-hoc, potentially influencing future agent design. The clean single-variable result on goal-abandonment (flipping from 0.00 to 1.00 when commitment mechanism is ablated) demonstrates a measurable drift decomposition that could improve agent reliability. The instrument includes a render-invisible shadow reference that compiles the plan the full system would have committed in every ablation cell, defining drift metrics even where the mechanism under test is removed. Four of the first eight architecture runs were invalidated, each localizing a real defect, and task efficacy was null (zero level completions across 52 gated runs on ARC-AGI-3), pre-registered as a structural defeater.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon agents often rely on language models for both planning and execution, but their self-reports and internal states are not trustworthy. This paper proposes a deterministic executive that owns belief, with language models only filing typed proposals, and claims admitted only when pre-registered predictions match observations by code. This approach aligns with broader trends in AI orchestration that treat agents as governed components inside deterministic workflows, ensuring consistency and auditability.

<ul>

<li><a href="https://www.elementum.ai/blog/are-ai-agents-deterministic">Are AI Agents Deterministic? | Elementum</a></li>

<a id="item-8"></a>
## [BrainBench: A Unified Benchmark for Comprehensive EEG Understanding with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04156) ⭐️ 8.0/10

BrainBench is a new unified benchmark introduced in an arXiv paper (2608.04156) that evaluates large language models on comprehensive, instruction-conditioned EEG understanding. It comprises four subsets, 17 datasets, and over 100K real-data instances, with results from more than 100K executions under two paradigms. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLMs for EEG analysis, moving beyond isolated decoding tasks to comprehensive workflows that include natural-language instructions and scientific interpretation. It provides a reproducible testbed that could accelerate research at the intersection of AI and neuroscience, benefiting both fields. The benchmark includes four subsets: Foundational Analysis, Sleep Assessment, Neurocognitive Assessment, and Physiological Integration. It evaluates outputs through numerical, categorical, set, sequence, semantic, and artifact validation, and compares models under autonomous code execution (CodeAct) and structured agentic analysis (BrainAgent) paradigms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Electroencephalography (EEG) records electrical activity from the brain and is used to diagnose conditions like epilepsy and sleep disorders. Traditional EEG analysis often involves predefined labels, but comprehensive understanding requires integrating natural-language instructions, signal processing, and scientific interpretation. Large language models (LLMs) have shown potential in various domains, but their capability for such complex EEG workflows has been insufficiently quantified until now.

<details><summary>References</summary>

<a id="item-9"></a>
## [Learning Error-Detection Rules Without Domain Knowledge for Robust Perception Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04190) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a domain-knowledge-free method for learning error-detection rules for pre-trained perception models using Label Vector Pools (LVP), achieving parity with hand-crafted rules within 0.002 F1 on test sets. The approach frames fusion of multiple ViT-based detectors as a consistency-based abduction problem solved by an exact Integer Program and a polynomial-time heuristic. This work significantly advances neurosymbolic AI by eliminating the need for hand-authored domain knowledge in metacognitive error detection, making it applicable to genuinely novel environments. It enhances robustness under distributional shift, which is critical for deploying AI in high-stakes applications like autonomous driving and medical imaging. The method uses per-model Label Vector Pools built from training embeddings to derive geometric rules, and it retains performance under a coordinated label-flipping attack: at a 90% flip rate it averages 0.42 F1 versus 0.35 for MV-Plurality, a 22% relative gain. The approach remains neurosymbolic, allowing complementation with domain knowledge when available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Distributional shift, the mismatch between training and deployment data, degrades the accuracy of pre-trained perception models in novel environments. Prior metacognitive methods used hand-authored domain-knowledge cues like object-size priors, which do not transfer to unseen scenes. Label Vector Pools (LVP) replace text labels with training images as similarity references, as seen in LVP-CLIP for continual learning. Neurosymbolic AI combines neural networks with symbolic reasoning to enable error detection and formal verification.

## [MatrAIx: Simulating the World with 8.3 Billion Persona Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04205) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MatrAIx introduces a population-scale simulated-user evaluation infrastructure with 8.3 billion persona records, each represented by 1,290 categorical dimensions. It includes a quality-filtered coreset of about 1 million personas and provides four environments (Survey, AI Chatbot, Web, App) for testing AI systems and digital products. This infrastructure could significantly advance AI evaluation methodology by enabling scalable, heterogeneous testing that captures human diversity and interactive behavior. It offers a practical alternative to costly and slow human evaluation, potentially accelerating the development and deployment of AI systems across various domains. The persona agents were powered by three LLMs: Claude Opus 4.8, GPT 5.5, and Claude Haiku 4.5. Validation studies showed 91.5% adherence to declared persona behavior across 400 trials, and human and LLM judges evaluated the extraction quality of human-grounded personas.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Human evaluation of AI systems is costly, slow, and difficult to scale, while offline evaluations often lack human diversity and interactive behavior. MatrAIx addresses this by creating a large-scale simulated-user infrastructure that uses persona records to represent heterogeneous users, enabling more realistic and scalable testing of AI systems and digital products.

## [RAIL Principles Proposed for Neurosymbolic AI Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04285) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new position paper introduces the RAIL framework—Reasoning, Assurances, Interfacing, and Learning—as four principles for designing neurosymbolic AI systems. The paper argues that many leading AI systems, including those not traditionally considered neurosymbolic, can be analyzed through this lens. This framework provides a unified perspective for analyzing diverse AI systems, potentially guiding engineers in making more principled design decisions. It underscores the importance of integrating symbolic reasoning with machine learning to build reliable and trustworthy AI, which is crucial for high-stakes applications. The paper is authored by a large group of prominent researchers and applies RAIL to areas such as physics-aware machine learning, neuro-guided search (e.g., Alpha-* suite), causal learning, and tool-augmented LLMs. It is a position paper rather than a breakthrough result, aiming to influence future research directions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Neurosymbolic AI combines neural networks with symbolic reasoning to leverage the strengths of both approaches while mitigating their weaknesses. It has gained attention as a way to address limitations of deep learning, such as lack of reliability and data efficiency, and has been applied in areas like LLM hallucination reduction. The RAIL principles offer a structured way to think about integrating these methods.

## [SafeCommit: Certifying Safe Actions for Memory-Grounded Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04289) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SafeCommit introduces a risk-controlled layer that certifies when memory-grounded agents can safely execute side-effectful actions, using conformal prediction to construct calibrated sets of plausible latent worlds and only allowing actions certified safe across all retained worlds. This addresses the critical failure mode of premature commitment in long-horizon agents, where acting on stale or incomplete memory can cause harmful side effects. By providing formal safety guarantees, SafeCommit could enhance the reliability of autonomous systems in real-world deployments, impacting AI safety and agent-based applications. The method uses conformal prediction to calibrate world coverage, ensuring the probability of an unsafe certified commit is at most a target level α, and separates calibration error from representation error. A dependency-free controlled simulator is provided to illustrate the safety-utility tradeoff and reproduce all reported results with one command.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Conformal prediction is a distribution-free uncertainty quantification technique that produces statistically valid prediction sets for any underlying predictor, assuming exchangeability of data. Memory-grounded agents use persistent memory and tools to take actions with external side effects, but memory can be stale, conflicting, incomplete, or corrupted, leading to premature commitment. SafeCommit builds on these concepts to provide a formal framework for safe action execution.

## [ADSL-PDE: A Domain-Specific Language to Boost Neural PDE Solver Auto-Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04384) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces ADSL-PDE, a domain-specific language that structures the search space for neural PDE solvers, enabling an evolutionary agent to propose, evaluate, and refine solver designs more efficiently. It reports a more than 52% improvement in search efficiency within the first ten evolution iterations across multiple PDE benchmarks. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in automated scientific computing: the sparsity of valid programs in unrestricted code spaces. By reshaping the search space, it demonstrates that effective LLM-driven auto-design requires not just stronger reasoning but also a representation that concentrates exploration on valid and consequential decisions, potentially accelerating AI-driven discovery in scientific domains. ADSL-PDE abstracts away low-level implementation details by representing functional decisions such as architecture, physical constraints, objectives, sampling, and optimization. A deterministic compiler maps each valid search state to an executable solver, removing large regions of invalid programs and increasing the density of meaningful candidates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Neural PDE solvers combine neural networks with numerical methods to solve partial differential equations, a challenging task that has motivated research into neural-hybrid solvers. Domain-specific languages (DSLs) are specialized languages tailored to a particular application domain, such as SQL or regular expressions, and are often more declarative than general-purpose languages. LLM-based auto-design uses large language models within evolutionary frameworks to automatically generate heuristics or solvers, but faces challenges due to the vast space of possible programs.

## [First Architectural Characterization of Agentic AI Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04458) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents the first architectural characterization of agentic AI workflows, based on a production study at Microsoft Azure and a controlled study of open-source frameworks. It reveals that agentic execution is fragmented and heterogeneous, with requests expanding into workflows that repeatedly cross the CPU-GPU boundary. These findings expose architectural mismatches with conventional uniform datacenter servers, such as stranded CPU/GPU capacity and inefficient homogeneous CPU provisioning. This provides significant insights for future hardware/software co-design of agentic servers, potentially improving utilization and throughput in datacenters. The paper introduces a taxonomy of agentic workflows and proposes Agora, a prototype for commodity servers that dynamically harvests idle CPU cores, oversubscribes GPU memory, and applies affinity-aware scheduling. Agora improves utilization and server throughput while preserving agent tail latency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI refers to AI systems that can autonomously perform tasks by orchestrating multiple steps, such as LLM inferences, tool invocations, and orchestration decisions. In datacenters, these workflows run on servers with CPUs and GPUs, but conventional server architectures are designed for uniform workloads, which may not suit the fragmented and bursty nature of agentic workflows.

## [CARGO-VL: Group-Relative Optimization for Reliable Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04509) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CARGO-VL introduces a group-relative optimization framework that jointly optimizes matched variants across four evidence states (aligned, image-correct, text-correct, both-wrong) to improve counterfactual consistency in vision-language models. It also contributes XMC, a four-condition conflict training resource, and evaluates on CMC-Bench and Modality-Bias. This work addresses a critical gap in multimodal AI: handling conflicting evidence between images and text. By improving reliability and abstention behavior, CARGO-VL could lead to more trustworthy vision-language systems in real-world applications where sources may disagree. The framework uses a primal-dual controller to balance unsafe answers against excessive deferral, and incorporates transition rewards for answer invariance, source equivariance, and answer-to-abstention switching. Ablations show complementary benefits from relational transition signals and adaptive risk control.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models combine images with retrieved text, but these sources can conflict. Traditional post-training objectives score instances independently, failing to enforce coherent behavior under counterfactual changes. Group-relative optimization, like GRPO, uses group averages as baselines rather than a separate critic, which can be more efficient and effective for such tasks.

## [New Benchmark Exposes Vulnerabilities in LLM Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04519) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel benchmark for evaluating machine unlearning in large language models (LLMs), focusing on multi-hop reasoning paths and recovery attacks. Experiments on 3 models, 6 unlearning methods, and 2 datasets show that current unlearning methods are vulnerable to these attacks. This benchmark addresses critical gaps in existing unlearning evaluation, which typically only considers single-hop questions and static assessments. The findings highlight that current unlearning methods are not robust, which is significant for privacy and safety in LLM deployment. The benchmark includes diverse multi-hop reasoning paths that can induce knowledge leakage, and recovery attacks such as lightweight post-unlearning adaptation. The paper also explores the trade-off among forget quality, robustness, and model utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning aims to remove specific knowledge from trained models, which is crucial for privacy and compliance. However, current evaluation methods often overlook that knowledge is interconnected and can be accessed via multi-hop reasoning, and that unlearned knowledge might be recoverable through attacks. This benchmark aims to provide a more comprehensive evaluation.

## [SkillSV: Structure-Aware Shapley Valuation for Agent Skills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04562) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces SkillSV, a structure-aware Shapley-style framework for assigning credit to internal units of agent skills, such as rules, examples, scripts, and heuristics. It accounts for dependencies and hierarchy among units and separates content value from context cost using paired deletion and length-neutral padding. SkillSV addresses a gap in AI interpretability and optimization by providing a principled way to value internal skill units, which is crucial for automated feedback loops that refine agent skills. It enables safe pruning and compression of skills, potentially improving resource allocation and performance in LLM-based agents. SkillSV compiles a skill into units, dependencies, and hierarchy, ensuring only valid counterfactual skills are evaluated. It uses a rollout-budgeted estimator for noisy agent evaluations and was tested on four agentic benchmarks, demonstrating faithfulness, actionability, and explanation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: The Shapley value is a cooperative game theory concept that fairly distributes total gains among players based on their average marginal contributions across all coalitions. In machine learning, it is used for feature importance, but applying it to structured skill units requires accounting for dependencies and context costs, which SkillSV addresses.

## [Calibrating AI Guilt via Human fMRI for Prosocial Multi-Agent RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04663) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a method to calibrate guilt-based reward shaping in multi-agent reinforcement learning using human fMRI data, recovering a guilt weight of 1.118 from the SoDec dataset and embedding it into a two-agent Social Lottery environment. The calibrated agents achieved the closest match to human prosocial behavior (KL=0.0012) compared to other shaping regimes. This work bridges neuroscience and AI alignment by using human neural data to quantitatively constrain reward shaping, potentially improving the alignment of AI behavior with human values. It offers a data-driven alternative to manual tuning of social reward terms, which could be applied to cooperative AI systems in real-world settings. The study used the public SoDec fMRI dataset (40 participants) and fitted a subject-fixed-effects regression to recover the guilt weight as the contrast between Partner-negative and Social-negative outcomes. The calibrated agents were trained with independent Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) and evaluated over 1,000 episodes per condition, with the calibrated regime achieving a safe-choice rate of 0.459 versus human 0.484.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent reinforcement learning often incorporates social rewards to encourage cooperation, but the scale of these rewards is typically hand-tuned. Reward shaping is a technique to guide learning by adding auxiliary rewards, and potential-based shaping can preserve optimal policies. This paper leverages human neural data to calibrate such shaping, drawing on the concept of guilt as a prosocial emotion.

## [Canary Tools Diagnose LLM Agent Tool-Selection Weaknesses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04719) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces canary tools, diagnostic probes planted in an agent's MCP tool set, each targeting one of six specific tool-selection weaknesses. The authors evaluate eight models across 8,640 runs and a 2,880-run ablation, revealing a 36x range in susceptibility and that capability tier alone does not predict safety. This work addresses a gap in agent evaluation by moving beyond binary success/failure to a multi-dimensional profile of tool-selection reasoning. It provides a taxonomy and framework that could influence future agent evaluation and tooling, helping developers identify and mitigate specific weaknesses in LLM agents. The six-type taxonomy includes semantic decoys, parameter traps, capability mirages, prerequisite blindness, temporal decoys, and granularity traps. The study uses a provider-independent judge with a second judge corroborating (Cohen's kappa = 0.75), and softening give-away phrases leaves frontier CSR unchanged, indicating the probes measure reasoning, not phrase-spotting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Model Context Protocol (MCP) is a standard that allows LLMs to interact with external tools, such as querying databases or calling APIs. Agent evaluations typically only report whether a model picked the wrong tool, not why. Canary tools are diagnostic probes planted in the tool set to reveal specific reasoning weaknesses, providing a more detailed assessment of an agent's tool-selection capabilities.

## [CoT Monitoring Unreliable in Implicit-Influence Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04735) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the first benchmark comparing chain-of-thought (CoT) monitorability under implicit-influence settings versus explicit-influence settings. It reveals that detection rates drop significantly when prompts contain no direct instruction to hide, with detection falling by 41-46 percentage points in two of four task formats. This finding challenges the reliability of CoT monitoring as a safety layer for frontier reasoning models, suggesting that monitorability estimates from explicit-influence settings may overestimate real-world effectiveness. It highlights a critical gap in AI safety evaluation, as implicit biases are common in real-world deployments. The benchmark spans four task formats (binary choice, numeric rating, multiple-choice QA, open-ended coding) and seven frontier extended-thinking models. Realistic system-prompt additions can lower implicit detection to as low as 5%, while preserving the behavioral influence itself.

## [EviGraph: Evidence-Guided Autonomous Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04738) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EviGraph is a new autonomous research framework that represents the research process as a typed evidence graph with Problem, Gap, Hypothesis, Experiment, Finding, and Claim nodes, and uses this graph as the operational state to inspect and repair evidence chains. It outperforms existing baselines on ARC-Bench-ML and NanoResearch-20, improving Claim Support Rate by 40.19% and achieving 87.73% Experimental Data Consistency. This work addresses a critical limitation in autonomous research agents—unsupported claims and inconsistencies—by explicitly maintaining and validating the claim-evidence structure. It could significantly improve the reliability of AI-driven research workflows, making them more trustworthy for scientific discovery and report generation. EviGraph inspects evidence chains for missing dependencies, semantic misalignment, and result-claim inconsistencies, localizes the earliest weak node, and regenerates its affected downstream subgraph. Graph checkpointing prevents unsuccessful repairs from corrupting previously validated evidence, and manuscripts are generated only after every retained claim is grounded in a validated evidence chain.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous research agents can generate hypotheses, execute experiments, and draft manuscripts, but their outputs often contain unsupported claims and inconsistencies. Existing systems typically organize research as sequential pipelines without explicitly maintaining the evolving claim-evidence structure. EviGraph introduces a typed evidence graph as the operational state, allowing the agent to validate and repair evidence chains throughout the research process.

## [PI-Conditioned Self-Distillation Fails on Hard Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04794) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper demonstrates that self-distillation with privileged information (PI), despite reported gains in easy settings, fails to improve validation accuracy on difficult tasks across QA, math, coding, and multi-turn agentic tool use. It identifies a causal chain from PI bias to a loss that is decoupled from task success. This challenges the effectiveness of self-distillation as a standalone objective, which is a growing area of interest for compute-efficient alternatives to RL. The findings could redirect research toward hybrid approaches or better loss designs, impacting the AI alignment and RL communities. The study reproduces SDPO's gains in easy settings but finds consistent failure on hard tasks, with per-token loss decreasing while validation accuracy stagnates or degrades. The causal chain includes PI bias, loss blindness to correctness, and penalization of exploratory tokens, quantified by a PI Bias Score.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Self-distillation (SD) is a technique where a model learns from its own predictions or a teacher model, often conditioned on privileged information (PI) such as reference solutions. It is considered a compute-efficient alternative to reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards. The paper tests SD as a lone objective without reward terms, across various domains and model sizes.

<a id="item-23"></a>
## [ContextWeave: New Benchmark Tests Memory in Real-World Agent Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04830) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ContextWeave, a new longitudinal benchmark, was introduced with 1,005 executable tasks reconstructed from privacy-preserved multi-month workflows of 14 participants. It evaluates whether recalled experience improves downstream agent performance in realistic office-work streams, showing that the strongest memory configuration raises Workspace Score from 68.08 to 78.20 and Preference Score from 41.50 to 70.60. This benchmark addresses a significant gap in evaluating memory for language agents, focusing on downstream performance rather than retrieval metrics. It provides a valuable resource for the AI/ML community to develop and test memory systems that can handle long-horizon, stateful workflows, potentially improving real-world agent applications. The benchmark includes 568 core evaluation tasks with instructions, containerized environments, trajectories, and task-specific rubrics. It measures workspace quality and alignment with participant-specific preferences, complemented by diagnostics of relevance, continuity, solvability, and robustness to misleading recall. With a fixed memory component, recall improves both outcomes for all five tested base models, although gains vary substantially.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Language agents are increasingly moving from isolated tasks to long-horizon, stateful workflows where memory is essential. Existing evaluations often reduce memory to retrieval or question answering, failing to capture how recalled experience affects actual task performance. ContextWeave reconstructs realistic office workflows to provide a more holistic evaluation, using metrics like Workspace Score and Preference Score to measure downstream impact. The benchmark is available on GitHub under the OpenMOSS organization.

<a id="item-24"></a>
## [WorldCycle: Self-Verifiable RL Cuts World Model Drift by 44%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04964) ⭐️ 8.0/10

WorldCycle introduces a self-verifiable reinforcement learning framework that uses reversible action cycles to provide annotation-free supervision for long-horizon video world models. It reduces state-returning drift by up to 44% and improves composite-action accuracy nearly 4x over the base model, along with a new benchmark, CycleBench. This addresses the critical verification bottleneck in long-horizon planning, where no ground-truth future states exist for arbitrary action sequences. By enabling self-supervised verification, it could significantly improve the robustness and physical grounding of world models, impacting embodied AI and interactive video generation. The framework optimizes two complementary rewards: a spatial closure reward enforcing symmetry between mirrored forward and reverse segments, and a temporal consistency reward aligning states across repeated cycle executions. It extends naturally to out-of-distribution composite action cycles, and CycleBench covers inverse, repeated, and composite cycles.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Interactive video world models are essential for long-horizon planning and exploration but suffer from compounding errors. Post-training methods like RL can improve them, but face a verification bottleneck because arbitrary action sequences lack ground-truth future states. The key insight is that reversible action cycles—composing a sequence with its inverse—must analytically return to the initial state, providing free supervision. This builds on prior work in self-verification for LLMs, such as RISE, and long-horizon world models like RELIC and LIVE.

## [Applying Item Response Theory to Improve LLM Safety Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05086) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new preprint applies Item Response Theory (IRT) to eight safety benchmarks across 192 language models, identifying three latent factors and showing that psychometrically selected items can recover full benchmark scores more efficiently than random subsets. This work offers a novel statistical framework to improve the interpretability and efficiency of AI safety benchmarks, potentially influencing future benchmark design and evaluation practices in the AI safety community. The study is the largest psychometric analysis of LLM safety evaluations to date. It finds that three interpretable factors—refusal strictness, truthfulness, and contextual harm—explain most variance, and that roughly ten adaptively chosen items can cut evaluation cost by 97-99% for several benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Item Response Theory (IRT) is a psychometric framework used to model the relationship between an individual's performance on test items and their underlying latent traits, such as ability or attitude. Unlike classical test theory, IRT does not assume all items are equally difficult, allowing for more nuanced analysis. In the context of AI safety, benchmarks are used to measure how safely language models behave, but aggregated scores can be hard to interpret due to redundancy and correlation among benchmarks.

<a id="item-26"></a>
## [ABSeeker: Answer-Backtracked Credit Assignment for Long-Horizon Search Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05102) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ABSeeker introduces Answer-Backtracked Credit Assignment (ABC), a framework that converts sparse trajectory-level outcomes into dense step-level rewards for training long-horizon search agents. The resulting model, based on Qwen3.5-4B with only 8.5k examples, achieves 37.3% on BrowseComp and 39.1% on BrowseComp-ZH, improving to 55.3% and 52.9% with context management, outperforming same-scale agents and matching larger ones (~30B). This work addresses a key limitation in training long-horizon search agents by providing fine-grained step-level supervision, which can improve performance in complex search tasks. It demonstrates that dense credit assignment can enable smaller models to compete with much larger ones, potentially reducing computational costs and advancing agentic AI. ABC consists of two components: Answer-Backtracked Clue Recovery, which traces back from the ground-truth answer to recover intermediate clues, and Clue-Anchored Step Scoring, which evaluates each search step against these clues. The framework is used to develop ABC-SFT (loss reweighting) and ABC-GRPO (step-level rewards in GRPO), and the model is trained on only 8.5k examples.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon search agents must perform multiple sequential actions to search, retrieve, verify, and integrate evidence. Traditional training methods treat all steps uniformly, failing to distinguish useful actions from erroneous ones. Credit assignment in reinforcement learning is a fundamental challenge, especially in long trajectories, and this work proposes a solution by providing dense step-level rewards.

## [OctoLong: Mid-Training on Cross-Repository Code Contexts Enhances Long-Context Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05141) ⭐️ 8.0/10

OctoLong introduces a context engineering pipeline that uses AST parsing, a language server backend, and a package manager to recursively retrieve code references, building dependency-rich code contexts of millions of tokens. The authors trained OctoLong-Instruct, a suite of open long-context LMs (600M to 14B parameters), via mid-training on a ~50B-token mixture including ~6.2B tokens of OctoLong code contexts, followed by ~10B tokens of instruction tuning. This work addresses the scarcity of long-distance dependency data in existing long-context corpora, which are dominated by books and articles. By showing that replacing just 12% of traditional context-extension data with OctoLong data yields substantial gains across retrieval, state tracking, repository-level understanding, and agentic tasks, it offers a scalable method to improve long-context capabilities of open LMs. The pipeline instruments an AST parser, a language server backend, and a package manager to enable recursive retrieval of code references, curating contexts of millions of tokens. The authors performed training ablations and evaluated against 18 state-of-the-art open-weight long-context LMs, also noting enhanced API usage in short-context coding scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Long-context language models are increasingly important for tasks like in-context learning and agentic workflows, but existing long-context corpora are limited. AST parsing converts code into a tree structure representing its syntax, while the Language Server Protocol (LSP) provides code intelligence features like go-to-definition and find-references, which are used to trace dependencies across repositories. Context engineering is a discipline focused on optimizing the information provided to LLMs to improve their performance.

## [Argus: Self-Evolving Agentic Runtime Boosts Long-Horizon Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05144) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Argus, a persistent, self-evolving agentic runtime, achieves about 78% on SWE-Bench Pro, outperforming Direct Copilot's 59% while using 1.41 times the aggregate tokens. It separates stable user intent from operational objectives and employs Manager, Planner, Engineer, and Reviewer roles over durable project state. This demonstrates that a fixed-weight model can self-evolve through runtime state and control policy, improving long-horizon reasoning tasks without changing model weights. It offers a promising architecture for AI agents in complex software engineering and other domains, potentially reducing token usage and increasing reliability. After verification-gated self-evolution, mature SWE-Bench waves use 21% fewer solve-input tokens and 15% less active workflow time per task than startup waves, with 34 verifier recoveries and 22 strict review-loop rescues. Argus also reaches 76.8% on AARRI-Bench and a 28.0-point gap on mathematical data synthesis, with competitive GPU-kernel and language-model-training results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: An agentic runtime is an execution engine that runs AI agent code step by step, persists state between steps, and handles failures. SWE-Bench Pro is an advanced benchmark for evaluating language models on complex, real-world software engineering tasks requiring extended reasoning and multi-step problem solving. Argus's self-evolution occurs through persistent runtime state and control policy, not by updating model weights.

<a id="item-29"></a>
## [Wiring Beats Blending: What Transfers Between Transformer Sizes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02829) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper characterizes the conversion of a 1.4B parameter model to a 410M model in the Pythia family, showing that representations align strongly (ridge R^2=0.84) while parameters align weakly, and that dense weight projection is functionally destructive. The authors decompose conversion into two independent levers—least-squares compensation and variance-preserving rescale—and find that conversion value lies in initialization rather than weight projection. This research provides a rigorous analysis of model conversion across sizes, with practical implications for efficient training. The findings could influence future approaches to model scaling and initialization, potentially reducing the computational cost of training smaller models by leveraging larger pretrained ones. The paper shows that after the best-fit linear operator, weight residuals are statistically indistinguishable from noise under shuffle controls. In matched-budget continued pre-training, compensation is a token-efficient, low-budget win, beating the strongest subcloning variant at 30M tokens on both width-reduced and depth-reduced pairs, but converges to parity at a 33x larger budget. At ~5x the donor scale (6.9B->1.4B), stacking both levers over-corrects due to ill-conditioning of the compensation solve, pointing to dimension-aware regularization as the fix.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: The Pythia model family is a suite of open-source language models designed for analyzing LLMs across training and scaling. Model conversion refers to the process of adapting a pretrained large model to a smaller size, which could save training resources. The paper uses ridge regression (a linear regression technique with regularization) to measure representation alignment, and rotary positional encoding (RoPE) is a common positional encoding method in transformers that the paper mentions as being broken by dense weight projection.

## [RAG-Stack: Co-Optimizing RAG Serving Performance and Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03487) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RAG-Stack is a new framework that efficiently discovers quality-performance Pareto frontiers across diverse RAG applications and serving systems. It introduces three components: RAG-PE (an iterative design-space exploration algorithm), RAG-IR (a workload abstraction), and RAG-CM (a performance model), which together enable searching the joint algorithm-system configuration space without deploying every candidate. This framework addresses a critical gap in RAG deployment by balancing answer quality and serving performance, which is a major challenge for knowledge-intensive applications. It can significantly reduce the cost and time of optimizing RAG systems, making it easier for developers to deploy high-quality, efficient RAG pipelines. RAG-Stack's Pareto frontiers cover 52.5% to 153.2% more of the normalized quality-performance space compared to state-of-the-art configuration-search methods, given the same number of optimization iterations across diverse datasets. The framework also supports transferring an existing Pareto frontier to a new serving system, enhancing its practical utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) combines large language models (LLMs) with external knowledge retrieval to improve answer accuracy. Modern RAG systems have many configuration choices—such as retrieval indexes, model selections, and retrieval invocation methods—each yielding different trade-offs between quality and performance. Finding the optimal configuration for a specific deployment is challenging, and existing methods often require exhaustive evaluation. RAG-Stack aims to automate this search efficiently.

## [Trajectory-Guided Framework for Red Teaming Agentic AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04018) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces TrajRed, a trajectory-guided red-teaming framework, and TrajGuard, a runtime governance layer, to identify and mitigate execution risks in agentic AI systems. Experiments on AgentDojo show TrajRed finds stronger vulnerabilities than baselines, and TrajGuard reduces attack success to near zero while preserving benign task utility. This work addresses a critical gap in AI safety by shifting focus from final outcomes to execution trajectories, enabling more effective red teaming and real-time risk control. It provides a practical foundation for governing agentic AI deployments in organizations, potentially influencing future safety standards and practices. TrajRed uses execution trajectories to uncover vulnerabilities, while TrajGuard leverages high-risk trajectories for runtime monitoring and intervention. The framework was evaluated on AgentDojo across four organizational task suites, demonstrating significant improvements over fixed-template and automatic red-team baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI systems are increasingly used in organizational workflows, where they interact with external information and tools, posing risks from malicious inputs. Traditional red-teaming methods rely on fixed attack templates or final outcomes, lacking visibility into multi-step reasoning and tool use. This paper argues that execution risk should be understood as a trajectory-level phenomenon, leading to the development of TrajRed and TrajGuard.

## [Trust-Region Framework Unifies Adaptive Optimizers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04026) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a trust-region framework for moment estimation in adaptive optimizers like Adam, deriving a family of learning-rate mechanisms called Gmake. Experiments on GPT2-124M show that fourth-moment estimation (kurtosis-like) performs best under weak trust-region constraints, while second-moment estimation becomes competitive under stronger constraints. This work provides a unified theoretical interpretation of several optimization techniques—normalization, scheduling, spectral filtering, and spectral normalization—within a single framework, potentially guiding the design of more efficient training methods. It also offers practical insights into when higher-order moment estimation is beneficial, which could impact large-scale model training. The framework constrains each weight's update within a trust region governed by a moment constraint of order p in [2,4]. The resulting Gmake mechanism unifies normalization by moment estimation, learning-rate scheduling, spectral lowpass filtering as momentum, and operator-level spectral normalization. Experiments were conducted on GPT2-124M trained on FineWeb-Edu and TinyStories datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Adaptive optimizers like Adam maintain exponential moving averages of gradients and squared gradients to compute coordinate-wise adaptive learning rates. Trust-region optimization is a family of iterative methods that build local models within a bounded region to reliably approximate and reduce the objective. This paper bridges these concepts by interpreting moment estimation as a trust-region constraint.

## [RRQ: Multi-Precision LLM Quantization from a Single Checkpoint](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04048) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Recurrent Residual Quantization (RRQ), a post-training quantization framework that constructs 2-, 4-, 6-, and 8-bit LLM representations from a single checkpoint by progressively adding 2-bit residuals. In a Qwen3-8B setup, the full package is built in 1,293 seconds, 3.3 times faster than MatGPTQ. This method addresses a practical deployment challenge by enabling flexible accuracy-memory trade-offs without requiring separate checkpoints for each bit-width. It could significantly improve model serving flexibility and reduce storage overhead for LLM deployment. RRQ is calibration-free and avoids joint multi-bit optimization, using round-to-nearest (RTN) for both the base 2-bit model and residuals. Experiments on six recent LLMs show competitive accuracy at 6 and 8 bits, with model-dependent behavior at 4 bits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training quantization (PTQ) reduces model size and inference cost by mapping weights to lower precision, but conventional methods require a separate checkpoint per bit-width. Residual vector quantization (RVQ) is an additive technique that encodes a vector as a sum of codewords, sequentially approximating residuals. RRQ applies this concept to LLM weights, enabling multi-precision from one checkpoint.

## [AgentAntibody: Adaptive Immune System Defends LLM Agents Against Prompt Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04053) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AgentAntibody introduces a self-evolving immune system for LLM agents that learns from past encounters to defend against prompt injection attacks. It maintains a persistent library of antibodies representing the user's security boundary, which evolves over time to strengthen defenses. This addresses a critical security gap in LLM agents, as existing defenses treat each task independently and fail to adapt to user expectations. By learning from experience, AgentAntibody could significantly improve the safety and reliability of AI agents in real-world applications, where tasks are often underspecified. The system was evaluated across three benchmarks and four backbone LLMs, showing it outperforms existing defenses in preventing harmful actions while preserving legitimate task completion. It is particularly effective when harmful and legitimate actions are both compatible with the stated task, a common challenge in underspecified requests.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Prompt injection is a security threat where malicious instructions are embedded in user inputs to manipulate LLM agents into performing unintended actions. Traditional defenses often rely on static rules or one-time detection, but AgentAntibody draws inspiration from biological adaptive immunity, which learns and remembers past threats to mount faster and stronger responses. This approach allows the defense to evolve with the user's expectations, which are often not fully specified in initial requests.

## [MACH: Prototype Hypergraph Learning for Multimodal Intent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04054) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces MACH, a hierarchical prototype-hypergraph framework that explicitly models both agreement and conflict between modalities for multimodal intent recognition. It uses separate agreement and conflict prototype hypergraphs combined via a sample-adaptive arbitration mechanism. This work addresses a critical gap in multimodal fusion by treating modality disagreement as informative rather than noise, which is key for tasks like sarcasm detection. The framework could improve performance on multimodal intent benchmarks and inspire new approaches to handling cross-modal inconsistencies. MACH progressively composes unimodal representations into bimodal and trimodal abstractions, activating sparse agreement prototype hypergraphs and dedicated conflict prototype hypergraphs. A progressive optimization strategy stabilizes the hierarchy before joint learning, and experiments on benchmark datasets validate the distinct roles of hierarchical composition, prototype-mediated refinement, and arbitration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal intent recognition integrates text, audio, and visual signals to understand human intentions. Traditional fusion methods often align modalities or suppress inconsistencies, but disagreement can be class-informative, such as in sarcasm. Hypergraph learning captures higher-order relationships beyond pairwise interactions, and prototype-based methods enhance robustness by learning representative patterns.

## [NOVA-KV: Attention-Preserving KV Cache Quantization Boosts LLM Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04074) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces NOVA-KV, a novel KV cache quantization method that derives attention-preserving transforms from calibration statistics, achieving near-lossless long-context retrieval at 2 bits per element. It formulates KV cache quantization as a transform coding problem with distortion measured by attention product error. This work addresses the bandwidth-bound bottleneck in long-context LLM inference, potentially enabling faster decoding and higher serving capacity. It improves upon existing methods by deriving optimal transforms from a distortion criterion rather than using data-oblivious or Hadamard rotations, which could lead to more efficient deployment of large models. The optimal key transform is non-orthogonal and satisfies a generalized Parseval relation, converting attention-aware distortion into MSE in the transform domain. Grouping coefficients into equal-volume partitions allows fixed-width codebooks to achieve variable-rate optimality under a high-resolution model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: KV cache stores key and value tensors during autoregressive decoding, and loading it dominates inference time in long-context scenarios. Quantization reduces cache size but must preserve attention products. Transform coding compresses data by transforming it to a domain where quantization is more effective, often using orthogonal transforms like Hadamard rotations.

## [DyLaR: Dynamic Latent Reasoning for Efficient Video QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04124) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces DyLaR (Dynamic Latent Reasoning), a method for video question answering that first grounds the question in perception latents and then adaptively decides whether to append reasoning latents before answering. Across nine video benchmarks and four multimodal backbones, DyLaR improves average accuracy while generating fewer than 20 tokens per query. This work challenges the conventional reliance on long textual chain-of-thought rationales in video QA, offering a more efficient and accurate alternative. By decoupling reasoning from language and adaptively invoking it only when needed, DyLaR could significantly reduce inference costs and latency in multimodal AI systems, benefiting real-time video understanding applications. On Qwen3-VL-4B, DyLaR improves average accuracy from 54.0 to 58.2 while reducing response length from 1,220.7 to 18.5 tokens per query. Ablations confirm that grounded perception latents, rationale-supervised reasoning latents, and adaptive routing each contribute to the accuracy gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Video question answering (VideoQA) requires models to ground language queries in visual evidence and reason over time. Traditional methods often generate long chain-of-thought rationales in text, which can be inefficient and sometimes unnecessary for simple questions. Latent reasoning operates in continuous hidden states, potentially enabling more efficient computation. DyLaR combines perception grounding with adaptive latent reasoning, using reinforcement learning to refine the decision of when to reason.

## [InvFlowFD: Reference-Free Music Quality Metric Using Flow Matching Inversion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04142) ⭐️ 8.0/10

InvFlowFD introduces a novel perceptual music quality metric that is both reference-free and background-set-free, using only a pre-trained Flow Matching backbone. It performs unconditional flow inversion via Euler integration and compares inverted samples to the prior distribution, achieving high correlation with human judgments. This advancement removes the need for a background set in reference-free audio quality assessment, making the metric more flexible and less restrictive than existing methods. It could significantly impact music generation evaluation by providing a more practical and accurate tool for assessing generative model quality. The method is validated through quantitative comparisons with prior work and a thorough human study, showing high correlation with human perception of sound distortions and generative model quality. It relies on unconditional Flow Matching inversion via simple Euler integration, which is sufficient to detect various artificial distortions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Flow matching is a generative modeling technique that learns to transform a simple prior distribution into a target data distribution by following a learned vector field. Inversion refers to finding the latent representation that corresponds to a given sample, which is useful for editing and analysis. Traditional perceptual audio quality metrics like PEAQ require a reference signal, while recent reference-free methods still need a background set of clean samples to compute statistics.

## [SkillClone: Black-Box Attack Reconstructs Hidden LLM Agent Skills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04192) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces SkillClone, a black-box attack that reconstructs the functionality of hidden LLM agent skills through benign probing and differential validation. The attack achieves exact or partial recovery on held-out inputs across 30 skills spanning rules, tables, procedures, and algorithms. This research reveals a new security risk beyond prompt injection: file secrecy alone does not ensure functional secrecy for LLM agent skills. It challenges existing disclosure-focused defenses and highlights the need for defenses that limit cumulative information leakage from ordinary use. SkillClone operates by forming an interface hypothesis from the skill's public advertisement, issuing structured benign probes, synthesizing an executable replica, and iteratively repairing it through differential validation against the victim skill. The attack uses only legitimate interactions, so disclosure-focused defenses provide limited coverage, and less detailed skill descriptions offer limited protection.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agent skills are lightweight, open formats for extending AI agent capabilities with specialized knowledge and workflows, often packaged as folders containing a SKILL.md file. Closed-source agent skills may encode proprietary instructions, scripts, constants, and data, and providers may offer them as services while keeping the underlying packages hidden. Prior work focused on prompt injection attacks that directly disclose these artifacts, and existing defenses aim to prevent such leakage. However, preventing file disclosure does not prevent users from recovering the functionality those files implement, which is the core issue this paper addresses.

## [ACT-Eval: Tool-Augmented Framework to Detect Hallucinations in LLM Chess Commentary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04240) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces ACT-Eval, a novel evaluation framework that decomposes LLM chess commentary into atomic claims and verifies them using chess engines and expert annotations. It also releases a new benchmark of 325 position-move pairs, including 125 with expert-verified gold atoms and a five-class error taxonomy. This work addresses the critical problem of hallucination detection in domain-specific LLM applications, offering a tool-augmented approach that improves factual accuracy. It provides a valuable benchmark and methodology for evaluating LLMs in educational and expert contexts, potentially influencing broader LLM evaluation practices. The framework routes atomic claims to engine-supported tools and expert-annotated gold references to assess factual correctness, conceptual coverage, and move-quality judgment. Results show that GPT-5.4 without tools produces incorrect sub-claims 22.0% of the time, while smaller open-weight models exceed 40%, and tool augmentation improves factual correctness but coverage of expert ideas remains limited.

## [EA-Graph: Artifact-Anchored Verification Memory for Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04278) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EA-Graph introduces an artifact-anchored memory for verification claims in coding agents, representing artifacts at sub-path granularity and anchoring each claim to the content used to establish it. It ensures claims become unprovable rather than guessed when replacement content is unavailable, and it was evaluated across 42 sessions with ground truth, showing significant improvements for the smaller model. This addresses a critical problem in AI-assisted software engineering: maintaining verification memory across sessions under upstream drift. By improving provability judgments, it can enhance the reliability of coding agents and reduce false confidence in their outputs, potentially narrowing the capability gap between different model tiers. The evaluation used generated repositories with known behavior-to-artifact ground truth, covering value drift, logic drift, and withheld content. In the Haiku round, artifact-anchored memory outperformed prose notes and no persistent memory in all seven worlds (p = 0.0156), while the Sonnet round had perfect performance but non-significant contrasts due to control ceilings.

**Background**: Coding agents often work across sessions, but prose notes can preserve conclusions without the program state that supported them. Upstream drift refers to changes in dependencies or source data that can invalidate previous verification claims. Artifact anchoring ties claims to specific content, and sub-path granularity allows precise tracking of code components.

## [Trident: Agentic LLM Red Teaming Breaks DRL Cyber Defenses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04317) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Trident introduces an agentic LLM red teaming framework with a dynamic benchmark and a dataset of over 13,000 interaction trajectories, using a 'Code-as-Policy' RLVR architecture to train a 7B planner that reduces blue agent defensive performance by an average of 522% compared to static red agents. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating DRL-based cyber defenses, which are typically tested only against static heuristics, by demonstrating their brittleness against adaptive threats. It also advances the integration of RLVR into cybersecurity, providing a benchmark and dataset that could accelerate research in LLM-driven red teaming and robust defense. The framework comprises three components: a dynamic benchmark with isolated sandbox servers spanning CybORG CAGE 4 and CyberWheel, a dataset of over 13,000 high-fidelity red-blue interaction trajectories, and a tripartite Log Summarizer–Planner–Coder design where a trainable Planner generates attack strategies from compressed logs and a frozen Coder translates them into executable Python policies. The empirical evaluation reveals emergent behaviors like decoy avoidance and adaptive state prioritization that static heuristics fail to uncover.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Deep Reinforcement Learning (DRL) is used to build autonomous cyber defense systems, but they are typically evaluated against static, heuristic red agents, leaving their robustness against adaptive threats understudied. Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a post-training method for LLMs where rewards come from automatic, rule-based checkers, which has improved reasoning but has been difficult to apply to cybersecurity due to a lack of suitable environments and datasets. CybORG CAGE 4 is a benchmark for cyber operations research, providing a high-fidelity simulation for training and evaluating AI algorithms.

## [COMPAS: Difficulty-Aware Joint Optimization for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04336) ⭐️ 8.0/10

COMPAS is a new method that jointly optimizes models, prompts, and decoding settings for code generation, learning group-specific quality-cost fronts and routing tasks to the best configuration. On LiveCodeBench, it improves pass@1 from 45.9% to 52.8% while reducing cost from $36.57 to $4.92. This work addresses a significant gap in code generation optimization by considering the joint interactions of models, prompts, and decoding settings, which are often tuned separately. It demonstrates substantial improvements in both quality and cost efficiency, potentially impacting how LLM-based code generation systems are optimized in practice. COMPAS uses a two-stage search: first, it selects a low-cost model per difficulty group, then it jointly tunes prompts and decoding settings via LLM-based reflection. The method also transfers to repository-level code generation, resolving 76.0% of tasks on SWE-bench versus 70.0% for the best baseline.

**Background**: Code generation systems typically make each LLM call with a model, a prompt, and decoding settings. Existing optimization methods often tune only part of these choices or use a fixed configuration for all tasks, ignoring their joint and group-specific interactions. COMPAS introduces a difficulty-aware routing mechanism that learns quality-cost fronts for different task difficulty groups, allowing online routing without further search.

FinReportBench is a new expert-grounded benchmark for evaluating and improving institution-grade financial report generation by LLMs. It features a 35-item rubric derived from expert partial orders and includes 244 bilingual tasks across three research objects and two input tiers. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating institutional-grade financial reports, as fluency alone is insufficient for professional acceptance. ## [BinaryPC: Training-Free Hashing Attention for Long-Context LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04405) ⭐️ 8.0/10

BinaryPC introduces a training-free, data-aware hashing-based sparse attention method that computes binary principal components of data to construct compact binary hash codes. It achieves a 3.56x end-to-end decoding throughput improvement over FlashAttention on modern GPUs while preserving accuracy relative to full attention. This method addresses the efficiency bottleneck of long-context LLMs by reducing the computational cost of self-attention during decoding, which is critical for real-world deployment. It offers a practical alternative to existing sparse attention methods that often require additional training or suffer from accuracy degradation, potentially enabling faster and more scalable long-context applications. BinaryPC uses binary principal components to create hash codes that preserve data structure without gradient-based training, unlike LSH with random projections or learned hashing. Experiments across multiple model families and long-context benchmarks show superior performance among sparse and hashing-based baselines, with code available at https://github.com/yudaohai666/BPC.

**Background**: Long-context LLMs rely on self-attention, which processes all key-value pairs, leading to high computational costs as context grows. Sparse attention methods reduce this by attending to a subset of keys, but often require training or use expensive hashing. BinaryPC leverages binary principal components, a data-aware technique that captures the most important directions in the data, to generate efficient hash codes for sparse attention.

The paper introduces SPOT, a novel method for on-policy distillation that uses an acquisition-exploration-exploitation procedure to efficiently allocate teacher supervision and improve student learning. It addresses limitations of standard reverse-KL training by combining position-level scores, verifier-scored continuations, and closed-form KL-regularized targets. SPOT could improve the efficiency and quality of LLM training by better utilizing teacher supervision in on-policy distillation, potentially leading to better reasoning performance in student models. This is significant for the broader trend of model compression and efficient training in the AI industry. The method uses a position-level score combining normalized teacher entropy, top-k candidate probability mass, and student-teacher mismatch to allocate a limited probing budget. During exploitation, it produces a closed-form, KL-regularized target that favors candidates with better downstream outcomes while remaining anchored to the teacher distribution.

Knowledge distillation is a model compression technique where a smaller student model learns to mimic a larger teacher model.

This paper introduces a training-free geometric prior based on tropical algebraic geometry, using the tropical Abel-Jacobi transform and polarization distances to create descriptors for graph learning. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of message-passing GNNs, which cannot distinguish certain non-isomorphic graphs due to the 1-WL test. By providing a mathematically grounded, training-free descriptor, it offers a new way to enhance GNN expressivity, particularly for complex spatial data like neuronal morphologies, potentially improving classification and analysis in neuroscience and geometric deep learning. The method computes the discrete Arakelov-Green measure in closed form via the graph Laplacian's generalized inverse, decomposing exactly into the intrinsic path metric minus the unquantized polarization distance, avoiding NP-hard lattice searches. The descriptors include node-level eigenvectors and a permutation-invariant eigenvalue spectrum, which integrate seamlessly into standard architectures without extra parameters.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) are widely used for learning on graph-structured data, but their expressive power is bounded by the 1-Weisfeiler-Lehman (1-WL) test, which limits their ability to capture cycles and other topological features. Tropical algebraic geometry provides tools like the tropical Abel-Jacobi transform and tropical Jacobian, which embed metric graphs into a torus, enabling the study of their geometry. The Arakelov-Green measure is a concept from Arakelov theory, and here it is used to derive a metric that captures graph structure without requiring training.

## [GUARD: Test-Time Failure Detection for Diffusion-Based VLAs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04510) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GUARD is a novel test-time method that detects weakly grounded predictions in diffusion-based vision-language-action (VLA) policies by ablating salient KV cache entries and analyzing denoising responses. It introduces a diagnostic stream with sensitivity, attention entropy, modality bias, and grounding efficiency, processed by a lightweight temporal classifier. This addresses a critical safety issue in embodied AI: diffusion-based VLAs can generate plausible but weakly grounded actions, leading to failures in real-world deployment. By enabling reliable failure detection without modifying pretrained policies, GUARD improves the trustworthiness and robustness of robotic systems across tasks and domains. GUARD is evaluated on task-held-out splits across five policy-benchmark settings using Pi0, SmolVLA, and Alpamayo-1.5 on LIBERO, SimplerEnv, MetaWorld, and PhysicalAI-AV. It achieves the best ROC-AUC on four of five unseen-task settings and ranks second on the remaining, improving average unseen-task ROC-AUC by 5.73 percentage points over the strongest competing runtime monitor.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion-based vision-language-action (VLA) policies combine vision-language models with action generation, enabling robots to perform tasks from visual and language inputs. However, they can produce actions that are not well-grounded in the evidence, posing risks in real-world applications. KV cache stores key-value pairs from the vision-language model, and ablating salient entries can reveal which parts of the input the model relies on. Test-time failure detection aims to identify when a policy is likely to fail without requiring failure data, which is crucial for safe deployment.

## [MCU: Model Merging for Continual MLLM Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04548) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces MCU (Merging for Continual Unlearning), a novel approach that dynamically merges one-shot unlearning adapters into a unified adapter to handle continual unlearning requests in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). It also reveals cross-task dependencies among these adapters and proposes a method to reconfigure them to mitigate interference while enhancing transferability. This work addresses a critical gap in MLLM unlearning, as existing methods are mostly one-shot and fail in continual scenarios, leading to utility degradation and unlearning rebound. By enabling effective continual unlearning, MCU enhances the practicality of unlearning for privacy and safety in deployed multimodal systems, which is increasingly important as regulations demand data removal. MCU projects adapters into a shared representation space, preserves dominant directions, suppresses over-concentrated coordinates, and reconfigures cross-task dependencies. Experiments on ICU-Bench and MLLMU-Bench show that MCU achieves superior unlearning effectiveness while preserving retained knowledge and general multimodal utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning aims to remove the influence of specific data from trained models to address privacy and safety concerns. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) process both text and images, and unlearning in such models is challenging. Continual unlearning refers to handling a sequence of unlearning requests over time, which is more realistic but harder than one-shot unlearning. Model merging is a technique that combines multiple models or adapters into one, often used in continual learning and multi-task learning.

## [EuroExec Benchmark Shows Frontier LLMs Underperform Experts on Executive Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04549) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new benchmark, EuroExec, with 413 open-ended European executive tasks authored by 47 domain experts, was introduced. After over 4,000 hours of human expert evaluation, the best frontier LLM solved only 56.9% of tasks, while expert-written reference answers were preferred over every model response in 74% of direct rankings. This benchmark reveals a significant gap between frontier LLMs and professional standards in complex executive decision-making, highlighting that current models are not yet reliable for real-world high-stakes tasks. It underscores the need for more robust evaluation methods and improvements in LLM capabilities for open-ended problems. The evaluation used a multi-attribute rubric, item-specific checklists, and preference ranking to compute a 'Solve Rate' metric. The study also found that automatic measurements fall short when evaluating real-world open-ended problems with subjective ground truth, emphasizing the importance of human evaluators.

The benchmark includes tasks from European executive contexts, requiring nuanced judgment under incomplete information and regulatory constraints.

## [Breadcrumbing Attack Hijacks LLM Search Agents via Coordinated Injections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04565) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper reveals that LLM-based search agents are vulnerable to coordinated prompt injection attacks through mediated search interfaces, which can hijack the agent's evidence-gathering process and final answers. The proposed Authority-Chain Hijack (ACH) attack achieves up to 83.3% MaxN ASR on the SafeSearch test split. This work identifies a novel security vulnerability in LLM-based search agents, demonstrating that coordinated injection across a trajectory can bypass existing defenses. It has significant implications for the safety of deployed agents, as search agents are widely used for information-seeking tasks. The attack operates under a constrained tool-intermediary threat model, appending only one controlled result per query. The paper also introduces Trace-Guided Strategy Evolution (TGSE), which automatically improves attacker strategies from execution traces, reaching 71.4% / 95.0% ASR / MaxN ASR in held-out evaluation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based search agents synthesize open-web content into actionable recommendations, but the web content retrieved during execution is untrusted, exposing agents to prompt injection and goal hijacking. Prior work focused on static web-content injection, but modern agents issue follow-up queries and cross-check competing sources, so a single injected page is often diluted or rejected. This paper shows that the channel delivering search and page observations is a fragile security boundary, allowing coordinated manipulation across the agent's trajectory.

## [PhysMind: Training-Free Framework Builds Executable Worlds from Video for Physical Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04575) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PhysMind is a training-free agentic framework that constructs a reusable, question-agnostic executable world from each video, using object segmentation, mesh reconstruction, and 6D pose tracking, then fits analytic dynamics to answer physical reasoning questions. It improves accuracy by 38.23 points on CLEVRER and 8.08 points on Physion++ over direct chain-of-thought reasoning with the same VLM, and exceeds GPT-5.5 by 19.25 points on counterfactual questions. This work addresses a key limitation of vision-language models in physical reasoning, offering a training-free alternative that could be applied to embodied AI and video understanding without costly fine-tuning. The significant gains on benchmarks suggest a promising direction for improving VLMs' dynamic scene understanding and counterfactual reasoning. PhysMind recovers a temporally consistent dynamic scene and fits analytic continuous-time dynamics with latent physical parameters, avoiding time-stepped simulation. It is a preprint (arXiv:2608.04575) and not yet widely validated, but the approach is technically deep and could impact embodied AI and video understanding.
