**Background**: Neural network verification aims to formally prove properties of neural networks, such as robustness to adversarial perturbations. Abstract interpretation techniques like CROWN and alpha-CROWN compute bounds on network outputs to verify these properties. alpha-CROWN is a parameterized version of CROWN that optimizes bound tightness, but its Python implementation limits integration. Luna addresses this by providing a C++ implementation that is both efficient and easy to integrate.

## [Terminal Agents Suffice for Enterprise Automation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00073) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper argues that coding agents equipped only with a terminal and filesystem can effectively automate enterprise tasks by directly using APIs, often outperforming more complex agent architectures at lower cost. The authors implement this as StarShell and evaluate it across diverse real-world systems. This finding challenges the prevailing trend toward complex agent frameworks like MCP and GUI-based web agents, suggesting that simpler, programmatic interfaces combined with strong foundation models could be the backbone of enterprise automation. It has significant implications for cost, operational overhead, and system design in the AI agent ecosystem. The paper compares three paradigms: GUI-driven agents, tool-augmented agents (e.g., MCP-based), and minimal terminal-based coding agents. The terminal agent, StarShell, operates through a terminal for executing commands and a filesystem for storing artifacts, and it matches or outperforms more complex architectures at a fraction of the cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Enterprise automation often involves agents that interact with digital platforms to perform tasks autonomously. Current approaches include tool-augmented agents built on standards like the Model Context Protocol (MCP), which standardizes how LLMs integrate with external tools, and web agents that operate through graphical interfaces. This paper questions whether such complexity is necessary, proposing that direct API interaction via a terminal and filesystem may be sufficient.

## [GUIDE: Generative Experiences for Digital Mental Health Interventions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07558) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A preregistered randomized study with N=237 participants introduces GUIDE, a system that generates personalized intervention content and multimodal interaction structures at runtime, significantly reducing stress (p=.02) and improving user experience (p=.04) compared to an LLM-based cognitive restructuring control. This work shifts digital mental health personalization from what support is provided to how it is experienced, potentially improving engagement and outcomes. It also demonstrates a novel application of generative AI in human-computer interaction, with implications for both mental health technology and personalized intervention design. GUIDE uses rubric-guided generation of modular components to compose intervention experiences at runtime, supporting diverse reflection and action flows. The study revealed tensions around personalization across the interaction sequence, indicating areas for future refinement.

## [VLM Reasoning Dynamics Reveal Answer Inertia and Modality Reliance Limits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14888) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper analyzes reasoning dynamics in 18 vision-language models (VLMs), revealing that models exhibit 'answer inertia' where early predictions are reinforced rather than revised during chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning. It also shows that models are influenced by misleading textual cues even when visual evidence is sufficient, and that this influence is only partially detectable in CoT traces. These findings highlight that CoT provides only a partial view of how modalities drive VLM decisions, with important implications for transparency and safety of multimodal systems. Understanding answer inertia and modality reliance is crucial for improving VLM reasoning and ensuring reliable deployment in real-world applications. The study covers instruction-tuned and reasoning-trained models from two model families, tracking confidence over CoT and measuring the corrective effect of reasoning. Controlled interventions with misleading textual cues show that reasoning-trained models explicitly refer to cues but may produce fluent CoTs that obscure modality reliance, while instruction-tuned models show shorter traces with inconsistencies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) integrate visual and textual information to perform tasks like visual question answering and image captioning. Chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning is a technique where models generate intermediate reasoning steps to improve interpretability and accuracy. However, how VLMs dynamically integrate modalities during reasoning remains unclear, and prior work has not fully explored the phenomenon of answer inertia or the limits of monitoring modality reliance through CoT.

## [JuRe: Minimal Denoising Network Achieves SOTA Time Series Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17388) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces JuRe (Just Repair), a minimal time series anomaly detector consisting of a single depthwise-separable convolutional residual block trained to repair Gaussian-corrupted, channel-masked windows. JuRe ranks second on the TSB-AD multivariate benchmark (AUC-PR 0.404) and second on the UCR univariate archive (AUC-PR 0.201), while running roughly 20x faster than AxonAD on TSB-AD. This work challenges the trend of increasing complexity in time series anomaly detectors, showing that a minimal architecture can achieve competitive or superior performance with significant computational savings. It could encourage the community to revisit simpler designs and prioritize efficiency, potentially making anomaly detection more accessible for real-time and resource-constrained applications. Ablation studies show that removing Gaussian corruption reduces AUC-PR by 0.046, while architecture variants span at most 0.017, indicating the training signal is more critical than architectural complexity. A synthetic linear-manifold experiment shows anomaly scores correlate with true off-manifold distance (Pearson r=0.725), supporting a geometric interpretation. Wilcoxon signed-rank tests with Holm correction find significant differences against 20 of 25 baselines, though dependence among series limits dataset-level interpretation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Time series anomaly detection is crucial for monitoring systems like servers, industrial equipment, and financial transactions. Modern detectors often use complex architectures such as attention mechanisms, adversarial training, and stochastic latent variables, but their necessity is unclear. JuRe uses a simple denoising approach: it learns to repair corrupted windows, and at inference time, the discrepancy between the original and repaired window serves as the anomaly score. Benchmarks like TSB-AD and UCR provide standardized datasets for evaluating and comparing methods.

## [DRUM: Robust Transfer Learning for Missing Covariates in Cardiac Arrest Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24212) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces DRUM, a distributionally robust unsupervised transfer learning framework that handles structurally missing covariates and unavailable labels in the target domain. It is demonstrated on cross-national out-of-hospital cardiac arrest (OHCA) prediction, transferring models from a US registry to Asian registries. This work addresses a critical barrier in deploying clinical prediction models across healthcare systems, where key covariates are often missing and labels are scarce. By improving transferability and calibration, DRUM could enhance the reliability and equity of healthcare AI in low-resource settings. DRUM partitions covariates into shared (X) and missing (A) components, and optimizes worst-case performance over the unknown target distribution of A|X using a neural network generator with a robustness parameter. A bias correction procedure reduces sensitivity to nuisance estimation error, and simulations show improvements in mean and worst-case prediction error.

## [VibeSearchBench: Benchmarking Long-Horizon Proactive Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27882) ⭐️ 8.0/10

VibeSearchBench introduces a new benchmark with 200 manually curated bilingual tasks across 20 domains, designed to evaluate long-horizon proactive search in LLM agents. It uses a progressive-disclosure user simulator and graph-matching evaluation, and tests seven frontier models under ReAct and OpenClaw frameworks. This benchmark addresses the evaluation-experience gap in LLM-based search, where current benchmarks fail to reflect real-world collaborative refinement. By highlighting that all tested models achieve only a best F1 of 30.30, it underscores the need for fundamental advances in long-context reasoning and proactive intent elicitation, impacting AI agent and search system development. The benchmark splits tasks into VibeSearch-Pro (professional) and VibeSearch-Daily (daily-life) subsets, each pairing a user persona with a schema-free ground-truth knowledge graph. Evaluation uses a progressive-disclosure user simulator and graph-matching, and results show all models remain substantially inadequate for VibeSearch.

## [Deep Learning Training as Hamilton-Jacobi Initial-Value Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28983) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper establishes an exact correspondence between neural network training and Hamilton-Jacobi initial-value problems, showing that each gradient step selects initial data for a viscous Hamilton-Jacobi equation whose Hopf-Cole propagator fits the observations. The correspondence is exact for log-sum-exp layers, with ReLU, sigmoid, SiLU, and GELU as exact limits, gradients, or moments, and it is structural for residual networks, transformers, and recurrent networks. This framework unifies various architectures and activation functions under a single theoretical umbrella, potentially leading to new insights into generalization, adversarial robustness, and scaling laws. It could influence deep learning theory and inspire new algorithms based on PDE principles. A single deformation parameter ε unifies network, tropical algebra, viscous PDE, and convex optimization perspectives in a commutative diagram. The paper derives quantitative results including minimax optimal generalization rate O(n^{-1/(d+2)}), adversarial robustness controlled by ε, backpropagation as the co-state equation for residual networks, and a closed-form O(N) influence function with softmax attribution weights.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Hamilton-Jacobi equations are a classical formulation of mechanics that describe wave propagation and are closely related to optimal control and dynamic programming. The Hopf-Cole transformation converts certain nonlinear PDEs, like Burgers' equation, into linear heat equations, which is a key tool in this paper. Log-sum-exp is a smooth approximation to the maximum function widely used in machine learning, and it serves as the exact correspondence point for common activation functions.

## [Order-Equivariant Neural Networks Unify Graph and Sheaf Deep Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03798) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces order-equivariant neural networks (OENN), which generalize graph message passing and sheaf neural networks via equivariant vector bundles over face posets, and proves universal approximation theorems for continuous order-equivariant maps, a new result even for sheaf neural networks. This work provides a theoretical unification of geometric and topological deep learning, potentially leading to more powerful and general architectures. It extends the known universal approximation theorem for graph neural networks to a broader setting that includes sheaf neural networks, impacting researchers and practitioners in these fields. The authors characterize all linear order-equivariant maps, build OENN layers, and prove universal approximation theorems. They also connect OENN to category-equivariant neural networks (CENN) via the action groupoid Grothendieck construction, enabling categorical symmetry in data.

**Background**: Geometric deep learning builds architectures that respect group symmetries, while topological deep learning organizes computation through cells and incidence relations. Sheaf neural networks extend graph neural networks by assigning vector spaces to nodes and edges, using a sheaf Laplacian for diffusion. Equivariant vector bundles over face posets provide a mathematical framework to unify these approaches.

## [TerraZero: Fast Procedural Driving Simulator for Zero-Demonstration Self-Play](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13028) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TerraZero is a new procedural driving simulator and self-play training stack that achieves 1.3 million agent-steps per second on a single GPU, far faster than existing object-level simulators. It trains driving policies from scratch using reinforcement learning alone, with zero human demonstrations, no imitation, and no fallback planner at inference. This matters because it addresses key bottlenecks in autonomous driving RL training: scalability, realism, and diversity. The ability to train policies that generalize zero-shot across cities and datasets, including emergent left-hand-traffic driving, could significantly accelerate the development of robust autonomous driving systems. The simulator uses a configurable C engine for CPU simulation and GPU policy inference over a zero-copy path, sustaining high throughput while maintaining fidelity with heterogeneous agents, multiple dynamics models, and full traffic-rule enforcement. Logged data is used only for map geometry, with randomized road users, signal controllers, dynamics, rewards, and sizes per episode, yielding an unbounded set of scenarios from a single map.

## [Diagnosing Inter-Column Dependency Gap in Tabular Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21636) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel method to decompose a gradient-boosted classifier two-sample test (C2ST) score into marginal, dependency, and numerical-categorical cross terms, revealing a persistent dependency gap in state-of-the-art flow-matching (TabbyFlow/EF-VFM) and diffusion (TabDiff) models. The authors show that standard metrics like linear C2ST and pairwise Trend scores are largely blind to inter-column dependency destruction. This work addresses a critical blind spot in evaluating tabular generative models, as inter-column dependencies carry minority-class signals crucial for high-stakes domains like fraud detection and clinical risk. By providing a diagnostic tool that quantifies dependency fidelity, it enables better model selection and motivates direct supervision of dependency in training objectives. The decomposition reads each term against a zero-dependency reference and a real-data oracle, applied to four benchmarks. The study finds that the dependency gap is not closed by a 16x capacity increase under clean training, and that residual gaps in minority-class utility do not track the measured dependency gap, suggesting the need for explicit dependency supervision in the objective.

**Background**: Tabular generative models aim to synthesize realistic data while preserving statistical properties, including inter-column dependencies. Standard evaluation often relies on metrics like classifier two-sample tests (C2ST) and pairwise correlation scores, but these can be insensitive to dependency destruction. Flow matching and diffusion models are recent generative paradigms that learn to transform noise into data distributions, and they have been adapted for tabular data synthesis.

CORF-GS introduces a real-time wireless radiance field (WRF) reconstruction framework that processes sequential optical and RF keyframes using coupled optical-RF Gaussian splatting, achieving state-of-the-art RF spectrum synthesis quality and a 6.4x reduction in reconstruction time compared to existing methods. CORF-GS constructs a unified Gaussian representation with shared geometry and modality-specific appearance, using optical-guided Gaussian sampling to densify under-represented regions and coupled optical-RF optimization to refine shared Gaussians. This prevents passive adaptation to frozen optical geometry, allowing the model to adapt to both optical structures and RF power distributions.

SE(3)-MeanFlow extends the MeanFlow framework to the Lie group SE(3)^N, enabling few-step protein backbone generation with simulation-free training. It introduces closed-form average-velocity identities for rotations and translations, along with an SE(3) alpha-Flow objective for warm-up and a rectification-based post-training. This work addresses a major computational bottleneck in protein diffusion models, which typically require hundreds of network evaluations during inference. By enabling few-step generation, SE(3)-MeanFlow could significantly accelerate high-throughput protein design campaigns, potentially impacting drug discovery and enzyme engineering. The method works natively in the Lie algebra so(3) and R^3, deriving closed-form average-velocity identities. The SE(3) alpha-Flow objective removes the Jacobian-vector product from the rotation branch, and training switches to a small-t stabilized MeanFlow loss for the remainder of pretraining and rectification-based post-training.

ORCA-bench introduces a new benchmark with 1,079 tasks that evaluate LLM agents' ability to perform root cause analysis on a live OpenTelemetry-instrumented microservice system. The benchmark reveals that even frontier agents achieve only 25.3% accuracy on Medium-difficulty tasks and 10.0% on Hard tasks. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLM agents for production-like oncall scenarios, highlighting significant limitations in current agents' ability to reason over noisy telemetry data. The low accuracy rates underscore the need for further research and development before agents can be safely entrusted with production reliability tasks. The benchmark pairs a live microservice system with six days of metrics, logs, and traces exposed through real telemetry interfaces (Prometheus, Jaeger, OpenSearch via Grafana) and full source-code access. Ground-truth symptoms are curated and validated by expert SREs, and the LLM-as-judge evaluation is independently re-scored by humans with a Cohen's kappa of 0.90.

**Background**: Root cause analysis (RCA) in oncall scenarios requires reasoning over noisy metrics, logs, traces, and source code, often starting from ambiguous user reports. OpenTelemetry is a standard for instrumenting microservices to provide observability data, and LLM-as-a-judge is a common technique for evaluating LLM outputs. This benchmark builds on prior work exploring LLM-based agents for RCA, such as the ReAct agent evaluated on production incidents at Microsoft.

<a id="item-131"></a>
## [DragonCrawl: LLM-Driven Mobile E2E Testing Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28750) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DragonCrawl is an AI-driven mobile testing system that evolved from embedding-based similarity matching to generative intent-based reasoning using large language models. It achieves 91.6% pass rate on iOS and 92.2% on Android across 1,013 automated tests running continuously in CI/CD pipelines. This framework significantly reduces test onboarding time from 96-120 hours to under 4 hours and saves an estimated 27 developer years in maintenance effort, addressing the brittleness and high maintenance cost of traditional mobile E2E testing. It demonstrates a practical application of LLMs in testing that can scale across platforms and integrate with CI/CD, potentially transforming how mobile regression testing is conducted. DragonCrawl leverages GPT-4o's multimodal capabilities for end-state detection and tool calling for backend state transitions, enabling comprehensive regression testing that bridges UI interactions with system state. The paper discusses implementation challenges including token explosion and memory constraints, and reports operational experience from production deployment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional mobile E2E testing frameworks rely on brittle UI selectors and scripted steps, which break frequently with UI changes and require high maintenance. Intent-based testing, powered by LLMs, shifts the focus from 'how' to 'what' the system should do, making tests more resilient. DragonCrawl builds on this concept, using generative reasoning to validate user flows on every code change, blocking commits that break critical functionality.

<a id="item-132"></a>
## [CompanionBench: A Real-World-Grounded Benchmark for AI Emotional Companionship](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02046) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CompanionBench is a new interactive bilingual benchmark for evaluating AI emotional companionship, grounded in de-identified real-world data and derived from 25 psychological theories. It assesses ten capabilities, including four not explicitly graded by prior work, and uses a hidden disclosure gate to control interaction state space. This benchmark addresses significant gaps in existing evaluations by grounding scenarios in real data and incorporating theory-derived capabilities, which could improve the development and evaluation of LLM-based companions. It reveals capability-level differences obscured by aggregate scores, highlighting common weaknesses like emotion regulation and calibrated challenge, and shows that role-play agents often lack relational competence. The benchmark uses a cross-family panel to dilute same-family favoritism and an Item Response Theory model to separate agent quality from judge severity. Rankings are reproducible in both languages (rho = 0.996 ZH / 0.953 EN), and the authors will release 500 Chinese-English parallel pairs and evaluation code.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: LLM companions are deployed at scale in personally consequential settings, yet existing benchmarks often use hand-authored scenarios and prompted simulators, aggregate empathy into one score, and overlook judge biases. CompanionBench grounds both scenarios and a trained user simulator in real-world data, and operationalizes capabilities from theories in psychology and counseling, such as selfobject responsiveness and calibrated challenge.

## [PhyCheck: New Dataset to Test Video-LLMs' Physical Law Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02150) ⭐️ 8.0/10

PhyCheck introduces a video QA dataset with coarse- and fine-grained subsets to evaluate and improve Video-LLMs' understanding of physical laws. It also includes a diagnostic subset with external causal context to test models' ability to recalibrate judgments. This dataset addresses an underexplored area in video understanding, crucial for embodied intelligence and world models. It provides a structured benchmark that could drive progress in making Video-LLMs more physically aware, impacting fields like robotics and autonomous systems. The coarse-grained subset asks whether a video conforms to or violates physical laws, while the fine-grained subset requires identifying specific physical details causing the violation or compliance. Experiments with fine-tuned Qwen2.5-VL show improved physical-consistency understanding, but diagnostic results reveal difficulty in incorporating additional causal conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs) extend LLMs to video understanding, but they often lack deep physical reasoning. Embodied intelligence and world models require systems to understand physical regularities beyond recognizing objects and actions. Existing benchmarks focus on generated video quality, not systematic evaluation of physical-law understanding.

## [GROVE: Training-Free Framework for Wearable Video Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02392) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GROVE is a training-free framework that grows a temporally stratified memory from streaming video, supporting both reactive question answering and proactive assistance. It achieves state-of-the-art results on benchmarks like MM-lifelong and EgoServe. This addresses a significant gap in wearable assistant systems by unifying reactive and proactive behaviors in a single memory architecture, potentially advancing video understanding and embodied AI applications. The memory consists of fine-grained perceptual evidence, time-stamped moments, coherent episodes, and recurring cross-day patterns, each paired with scale-native retrieval skills. Controlled ablations show these strata are complementary, with patterns being most beneficial for multi-day evidence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Wearable assistants need to recall past visual experiences and proactively use them in current situations. Existing video-memory systems typically support only question-conditioned recall, while proactive assistants use separate memory and control mechanisms. GROVE introduces a unified, training-free approach that grows memory causally from continuous video streams.

## [SPIKE-Bench: Quantifying Biosecurity Risks in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02684) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced SPIKE-Bench, a benchmark with 631 toxin-design prompts across seven functional categories, and the SPIKE funnel, a three-stage protocol to evaluate LLMs' ability to generate biologically plausible and functionally harmful sequences. An audit of 32 LLMs found that most comply with toxin-design requests, with a Functional Harmfulness Rate (FHR) reaching 50.7%. This work addresses a critical blind spot in AI safety evaluation by moving beyond natural-language refusal checks to assess actual biological risk. It provides a standardized benchmark and metric (FHR) that can guide the development of safer LLMs in biotechnology, potentially influencing future alignment and safety research. The SPIKE funnel filters outputs through compliance (SORRY-Bench judge), biological plausibility (ESM-2 pseudo-perplexity and ESMFold pLDDT), and predicted toxicity (ToxinPred2), producing stage-level diagnostics. The study also introduces BioSafe-Guard, a domain-specialized classifier that reduces functional risk while preserving benign utility, and the benchmark is publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly used in biological research, but they can also be misused to generate harmful sequences like toxins. Traditional safety evaluations only check if a model refuses to answer, not whether the output is biologically plausible or harmful. SPIKE-Bench fills this gap by providing a curated benchmark and a multi-stage evaluation protocol that measures functional harmfulness.

## [Sieve: Structure-Aware Boolean Retrieval Boosts Deep-Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02751) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Sieve, a search-inspect-fetch strategy that uses a Boolean Query Language (BQL) to exploit webpage structure, improving accuracy and reducing token usage in deep-research agents. Across three QA collections, Sieve outperformed the strongest conventional Search-Visit configuration while using 20.7%–50.6% fewer tokens. This work addresses a key inefficiency in current deep-research agents, which often retrieve whole webpages without leveraging structural cues, leading to wasted tokens and reduced accuracy. By demonstrating consistent gains across rankers and backbones, Sieve offers a practical, model-agnostic improvement that could enhance the efficiency and reliability of AI-powered research tools. Sieve filters webpage fields via Boolean queries, uses an interchangeable ranker, presents structure-rich result cards, and fetches only selected sections. The implementation is included in the SkimSearchAgent library on GitHub, and Boolean filtering improved every tested ranker.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional deep-research agents follow a Search-Visit workflow, retrieving entire webpages without considering their structure (e.g., titles, headings, sections, metadata). Boolean Query Language (BQL) is a classic information retrieval technique that uses operators like AND, OR, NOT to match documents against logical expressions. By combining BQL with structure-aware retrieval, Sieve allows agents to target specific parts of a webpage, reducing irrelevant content in the context.

## [GPTKB 2.0: Building Million-Scale Disambiguated Knowledge Bases from LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03729) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GPTKB 2.0 introduces a methodology for constructing disambiguated knowledge bases directly from large language models, achieving over 1 million disambiguated entities and 38.4 million triples with explicit canonicalization of entities, relations, and classes. This is the first million-scale LLM-native knowledge base with internal canonicalization, available at https://gptkb.org/. This work addresses the critical challenge of entity disambiguation in automated knowledge base construction from LLMs, which has been a major bottleneck. The achievement of a million-scale, disambiguated KB marks a significant milestone that could influence future research and applications in NLP and knowledge engineering, offering a scalable alternative to traditional Wikimedia-centric KBs. GPTKB 2.0 incorporates on-the-fly disambiguation of entities, relations, and classes, and is designed to balance scalability and disambiguation accuracy. The materialized KB contains over 1M disambiguated entities and 38.4M triples, with the browser exposing the disambiguation chain (surface form → canonical entity → provenance batch) on every triple.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Automated Knowledge Base Construction (AKBC) is a core NLP task, and recent work has explored generating knowledge bases directly from LLMs, treating the model as the knowledge source. However, LLMs lack native entity representations, leading to duplicate entries and conflations. Entity disambiguation is the process of mapping mentions to unique entities in a knowledge base, which is crucial for maintaining accurate and non-redundant KBs. Traditional KBs like DBpedia are built from curated or textual sources, whereas GPTKB 2.0 leverages LLM parameters directly.

## [MCTS-Report: Monte Carlo Tree Search for Multimodal Report Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04071) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MCTS-Report, a novel framework that applies Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) to generate multimodal reports from tabular data, decomposing the process into atomic actions and using LLMs for dynamic reasoning. It also presents MMRBench, a new benchmark with real-world tables from six domains, and reports a 77.9 overall score, outperforming strong baselines. This work addresses limitations of existing linear pipelines in table-to-report generation, enabling joint optimization of factual accuracy, visual quality, and narrative coherence. It could advance automated data intelligence and report generation, benefiting fields like business intelligence and data journalism. The framework uses a multi-dimensional reward function that evaluates numerical fact consistency via SQL, chart quality, chart-text alignment, and structural completeness, along with a diversity penalty and precondition check. Experiments on MMRBench show significant improvements in structural completeness, numerical accuracy, chart-text alignment, and insight novelty.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) is a heuristic search algorithm used in decision processes, notably in board games like Go, where it combines tree search with random sampling. ## [FinPerMA: New Benchmark Reveals LLM Agents' Weak Personalized Memory in Finance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04095) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced FinPerMA, an event-grounded benchmark for evaluating LLM agents' personalized memory in financial advising, using 2,994 questions from 276 personas. Testing seven frontier LLMs with up to seven memory configurations, they found no configuration exceeded approximately 47% overall accuracy or 39% on multiple-choice questions. This benchmark addresses an underexplored area—event-driven preference adaptation in personalized memory—which is critical for high-stakes domains like financial advising. The results show that current LLM agents are far from saturated, highlighting a significant gap that must be bridged for reliable deployment as personalized assistants. The benchmark uses a generation pipeline combining deterministic, theory-informed impact rules, controlled LLM narration, and automated quality screening. A Post-Shock checkpoint isolates whether an agent has integrated a material event into its persistent user model, and attribution analysis shows summary-based memory often loses preference signals, with simple retrieval sometimes outperforming purpose-built memory systems.

This paper introduces a dynamic precision allocation mechanism for interoceptive attention in an active inference foraging agent, which significantly improves survival compared to uniform precision allocation. This work demonstrates a novel mechanism for dynamic interoceptive attention that could influence future embodied AI and cognitive modeling, showing that selective precision allocation can substantially improve performance in resource-constrained agents. ## [NeuMoSync: Neuromodulatory Control for Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04358) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NeuMoSync introduces a novel architecture that integrates dynamic, neuron-specific modulation into deep neural networks to enhance plasticity and adaptability in continual learning. It is evaluated on diverse benchmarks, showing improvements in forward and backward adaptation. This work addresses the critical challenges of plasticity loss and poor knowledge transfer in continual learning, which are major obstacles for deploying deep learning models in dynamic environments. By drawing inspiration from neuromodulation, it offers a new direction for building more robust and adaptive AI systems. NeuMoSync extends standard neural networks with learnable feature vectors per neuron and a higher-level module that synthesizes neuron-specific signals conditioned on current inputs and network state. Ablation studies confirm the necessity of each component, and analysis reveals interpretable coordination patterns across tasks.

**Background**: Continual learning (CL) requires models to learn tasks sequentially without forgetting previous knowledge, but deep neural networks often suffer from plasticity loss, where they lose the ability to learn new tasks. This approach aims to improve both forward transfer (learning new tasks) and backward transfer (improving performance on old tasks).

## [JUROR: Joint UAV Flight and Opportunistic Routing via PPO for DTNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04590) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces JUROR, a PPO-based framework that jointly optimizes UAV flight headings and decentralized opportunistic routing in delay-tolerant networks (DTNs). It formulates the problem as a factored partially observable Markov decision process with centralized training and decentralized execution (CTDE), and demonstrates performance gains over PRoPHET and MaxProp in simulations. This work addresses a key challenge in DTNs—sparse connectivity and congestion—by actively controlling UAV movement to create future contacts, rather than passively relying on opportunistic encounters. It offers a scalable, decentralized solution that could improve delivery performance in disaster response, remote sensing, and other infrastructure-limited scenarios. The framework uses a factored POMDP with sequential motion-routing coupling and a per-step team reward, where decentralized actors use local observations and a training-time critic leverages global statistics. An optional multi-horizon hotspot predictor provides auxiliary supervision, and simulations cover four traffic modes, showing gains over PRoPHET and MaxProp while retaining contact-limited decentralized execution.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Delay-tolerant networks (DTNs) rely on store-carry-forward (SCF) communication, where nodes store messages and forward them upon opportunistic encounters, making them suitable for sparse or intermittent connectivity. However, limited buffers and message time-to-live (TTL) can cause congestion and delivery failures. Centralized training with decentralized execution (CTDE) is a multi-agent reinforcement learning paradigm that allows agents to learn with global information during training but act on local observations during execution. Proximal policy optimization (PPO) is a policy gradient RL algorithm known for its stability and sample efficiency.

## [Agreement-Before-Diversity: A Label-Free Rule for Heterogeneous LLM Ensembles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04618) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Agreement-Before-Diversity (ABD), a frozen, label-free decision rule for heterogeneous language-model ensembles, which decides whether to retain an anchor answer or replace it with a heterogeneous synthesis. It proves two exact identities for the accuracy gap and demonstrates strong empirical results on LiveCodeBench-v6 and GPQA-Diamond. This work addresses a critical gap in heterogeneous LLM ensembles by providing a principled, auditable criterion for answer replacement without requiring labels or calibrated confidence. It could improve the reliability and transparency of multi-model coordination, benefiting applications that rely on diverse model outputs. ABD retains an anchor answer if two additional trusted samples corroborate it under a fixed equivalence relation; otherwise, it replaces it with a heterogeneous synthesis. The paper proves two exact identities that do not assume independence or calibrated confidence, and the expected inference cost is approximately eight minus five times the coverage in number of calls. On LiveCodeBench-v6, ABD achieves 59.43% vs. 52.57% for Single9 and 52.00% for HAC (n=175); on GPQA-Diamond, it achieves 75.00% vs. 72.78% for both controls (n=180).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Heterogeneous language-model ensembles combine outputs from models with different architectures or training to expand the space of candidate responses, but they lack a principled criterion for when a new answer should replace an existing one. Existing ensemble methods often rely on training a reward or fusion model, which can generalize poorly to unseen data. ABD offers a label-free, frozen decision rule that decouples candidate headroom from replacement authority, making the decision process explicit and auditable.

## [A/B Agent: Self-Evolving Agent for Industrial A/B Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04625) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces A/B Agent, a closed-loop framework that organizes historical strategies into a hierarchical experience tree, uses multi-path Tree-RAG for retrieval, and incorporates sequential A/B feedback for autonomous tuning and self-evolution. In a real-world short-video e-commerce recommendation system, it achieved a 4.829% improvement in GMV while maintaining positive gains across all guardrail metrics. This work addresses the labor-intensive and time-consuming nature of traditional A/B testing in industrial recommendation systems, where experts manually design and tune strategies. By automating strategy iteration and enabling cross-scenario knowledge transfer, it has the potential to significantly reduce costs and improve efficiency for companies relying on large-scale experimentation. The framework consists of three core components: Historical Strategy Knowledge Organization, Autonomous Target-Aware Strategy Generation, and Experiment-Guided Strategy Self-Evolution. It leverages a hierarchical experience tree and multi-path Tree-RAG to retrieve transferable evidence, and uses online A/B feedback to guide tuning and update the tree, enabling continuous self-evolution.

Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) agents have been explored to assist, but they typically organize experience in a flat manner, overlooking hierarchical relationships among business scenarios, stages, and objectives. ## [Prompt-Region Grounding Bridges Semantic Channel Gap in Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04726) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Visualized Task Semantics (VTS), a controlled intervention that moves questions into images, and reveals a 'semantic channel gap' in multimodal LLMs. It proposes prompt-region grounding, which improves VTS accuracy from 58.0 to 66.3 across four benchmarks without requiring OCR or region metadata at inference. This work highlights a critical limitation in multimodal LLMs: they often fail to use instructions embedded in images as effectively as text instructions, which is crucial for real-world applications like document analysis and UI understanding. The proposed method offers a training-efficient solution to close this gap, potentially improving the reliability of multimodal reasoning systems. The study evaluates six MLLMs across four benchmarks, finding accuracy drops in all 24 model-task pairs, with an average drop of 17.8 points. Prompt-region grounding aligns the question region with typed semantics and recovers its clean representation from a masked view, preserving accuracy on the original interface at matched training cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

The 'semantic channel gap' refers to the discrepancy in how well models use instructions from different modalities. Prompt-region grounding is a technique that helps models better associate visual regions with their semantic meaning, improving their ability to follow visual instructions.

## [ReCo: Reward-Coordinated KV-Cache Compression for Efficient Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04771) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces ReCo, a step-wise framework that uses a process-reward estimator to coordinate KV-cache compression, reflection-token penalty, and early stopping in large reasoning models. It reduces generated tokens by 37%-65% and end-to-end latency by 2.08x-2.35x over full chain-of-thought across three models and six benchmarks, while largely preserving accuracy. ReCo addresses the practical problem of high inference cost in large reasoning models caused by overthinking. By adapting compression to process rewards, it offers a more efficient way to deploy LRMs in real-world applications, potentially reducing operational costs and improving response times. ReCo comprises three components: reward-adaptive KV-cache compression, a reward-banded penalty on reflection tokens, and confidence-based early stopping. The method leverages the observation that high-reward reasoning steps are more tolerant to token deletion, and that smaller caches can lead to more generated tokens, so it coordinates both sides under a single process reward.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) generate long chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning, which can be inefficient due to overthinking. KV-cache compression is a common technique to reduce memory and compute, but existing methods apply a uniform policy and ignore the generation-side effects. Process Reward Models (PRMs) score intermediate reasoning steps, which can guide more adaptive compression.

A new audit of NAVSIM v2.2 reveals that its scoring can incorrectly reward agents that ignore surrounding actors due to shared rollout failures. The paper shows that under certain conditions, blind probes outrank human replay and PDM-Closed on the navtest split. This finding undermines the validity of NAVSIM as a benchmark for defensive driving, potentially leading to incorrect rankings of autonomous driving policies. It highlights the need for rigorous audit protocols in re-simulation benchmarks to ensure reliable evaluation. The audit identifies reference-conditioned forgiveness as a key mechanism that propagates shared reference failures into compliance credit. Replacing only the solver on a 450-token control pool eliminates rollout divergence and restores blind-last ordering, indicating numerical instability as the direct trigger.

**Background**: Short-term load forecasting (STLF) is crucial for power grid operations, and under the EU AI Act, systems used in critical infrastructure may be classified as high-risk, requiring strict compliance with determinism, reproducibility, and auditability. The spotforecast2-safe library is designed for safety-critical environments, implementing these requirements by design. Traditional large foundation models, while accurate, are often energy-intensive and less transparent, whereas local models like macl2l offer a cost-effective alternative.

## [HiGram: Hierarchical Graph Memory with Path-level Localization and Rewrite for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05095) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HiGram introduces an evolving hierarchical graph memory framework for LLM agents, featuring path-level localization and coordinated rewriting to improve long-term reasoning. It organizes memory into a coarse-to-fine architecture with upper-level nodes and MemoryUnits, reducing irrelevant context during retrieval. This addresses limitations of flat graph memory, which can accumulate irrelevant contexts and require inefficient unit-wise updates. By improving memory efficiency and update effectiveness, HiGram could enhance long-term reasoning and question answering in AI agents, benefiting applications like conversational agents and dynamic knowledge bases. The framework uses MicroGraph-based path-level localization to identify support subgraphs and evidence paths before rewriting. It also proposes a coordinated rewriting method that jointly revises intra-unit memory and inter-unit dependencies, ensuring valid dependency structures are updated. Experiments show improvements in answer quality and token efficiency on long-term conversational QA and conflict-aware memory benchmarks.

**Background**: LLM agents often need memory to retain and reason over long-term information. Graph memory provides structural organization for multi-hop retrieval, but flat graphs can become cluttered. Hierarchical memory organizes information at multiple levels, and path-level localization focuses updates on relevant subgraphs, improving efficiency and accuracy.

## [CoPlan: Contestable AI Care Planning via Role-Based Argument Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05107) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoPlan is a new co-intelligent interface for care planning that uses a multi-agent workflow to generate candidate interventions and supporting or challenging arguments, allowing human stakeholders to accept, reject, modify, or add arguments before final plan generation. It is demonstrated in an aging-in-place care planning scenario. This work addresses the critical issue of contestability in AI-supported healthcare, where recommendations are often presented as fixed outputs. By enabling human oversight and revision, CoPlan preserves human agency and clinical accountability, which is essential for trustworthy AI in high-stakes domains like care planning. CoPlan integrates adaptive care team recruitment, role-based argument review, final care plan generation, and practical follow-up through scheduling agents. The system combines co-intelligence, where humans and AI contribute complementary expertise, with contestability, keeping recommendations open to inspection and justification.

It enables biologists to interact with deep learning models using natural language, automating model selection, hyperparameter tuning, and data retrieval from protein databases. ## [Temporal Context Awareness Framework Defends LLMs Against Multi-Turn Manipulation Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15560) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Temporal Context Awareness (TCA) framework, a novel defense mechanism that continuously analyzes semantic drift, cross-turn intention consistency, and evolving conversational patterns to detect and mitigate multi-turn manipulation attacks on large language models (LLMs). Preliminary evaluations on simulated adversarial scenarios show TCA can identify subtle manipulation patterns often missed by traditional single-turn detection methods. Multi-turn manipulation attacks exploit the temporal nature of dialogue to bypass safety measures, posing a critical security vulnerability for real-world LLM deployments. The TCA framework offers a promising context-aware defense that could enhance the security of conversational AI systems without sacrificing their utility, addressing a pressing need in AI safety. The TCA framework integrates dynamic context embedding analysis, cross-turn consistency verification, and progressive risk scoring. The paper also analyzes diverse attack vectors and their progression across multi-turn conversations, and the implementation is made available to support further research.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in conversational AI, but they are vulnerable to adversarial attacks. Multi-turn manipulation attacks involve adversaries gradually building context through seemingly benign conversational turns to eventually elicit harmful or unauthorized responses, which single-turn detection methods fail to catch. The TCA framework addresses this by analyzing the entire conversation's temporal dynamics.

## [C2MOE: Consistency and Complementarity-guided Mixture of Experts for Incomplete Multimodal Emotion Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04013) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces C2MOE, a novel framework that unifies representation learning and missing modality imputation for multimodal emotion recognition in conversations. It factorizes multimodal knowledge into consistency and complementarity components using interaction-aware experts and a dual-branch prediction mechanism. This work addresses a critical real-world problem of missing modalities in emotion recognition, which often degrades model performance. By explicitly modeling both consistency and complementarity, C2MOE could improve robustness and generalization of multimodal systems, benefiting applications like human-computer interaction and affective computing. C2MOE uses information-theoretic principles: maximizing cross-modal predictability for consistency and conditional entropy for complementarity. It employs a learnable reweighting module to dynamically assign importance to expert outputs, and experiments on multiple MERC benchmarks show it surpasses state-of-the-art methods across various missing-modality settings.

Existing methods focus on cross-modal consistency but ignore complementarity, leading to biased reconstructions. Mixture of Experts (MoE) is a scalable architecture that uses specialized experts to handle different modalities or tasks.

## [New Stability Theory for Subdominant Ultrametric Under Sparse Edits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04014) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an l0-type stability theory for the subdominant ultrametric, showing that sparse edits propagate only through the minimum spanning tree and providing sharp Hamming-Lipschitz bounds on the number of changed ultrametric entries. This work fills a gap in stability analysis for ultrametrics under sparse perturbations, which is relevant to single-linkage clustering and hierarchical representations. The sharp bounds and simple proofs could improve vulnerability diagnostics in clustering and computational geometry applications. The analysis shows that a pairwise ultrametric value changes only if its tree path crosses an edited edge or a cut exposed by an edited off-tree edge, leading to a per-edit exposed-cut score and a tree-only global envelope. Sharpness results demonstrate that the dependence on tree geometry is unavoidable, with explicit families where one edited distance changes Θ(n²) ultrametric entries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: The subdominant ultrametric is the largest ultrametric bounded above by a given dissimilarity matrix and is equivalent to the ultrametric induced by single-linkage clustering. Classical stability theory for ultrametrics typically uses l∞ or Gromov-Hausdorff bounds, which are not well-suited for sparse perturbations that affect only a few pairwise distances. The minimum spanning tree plays a central role in single-linkage clustering, as the dendrogram is derived from the MST.

## [Lindblad-Inspired Reservoir Computing with Separable Rotation and Dissipation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04028) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel reservoir computing architecture inspired by Lindblad dynamics, where the recurrent operator is assembled from exactly discretized damped rotational modes, allowing rotation and decay to be independently controlled. The method provides a direct global stability guarantee without post-hoc spectral-radius rescaling. This work bridges open-system dynamical principles with structured state-space modeling, offering an interpretable recurrent architecture where mixing, memory, and stability are explicit and independently tunable. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on benchmarks like NARMA-20 and Lorenz-63, potentially impacting efficient sequence processing in temporal learning. The method is evaluated against standard, leaky, deep, orthogonal, cycle, and next-generation reservoirs, as well as a compact trained GRU, across benchmarks including linear memory, nonlinear recurrence, chaotic forecasting, delayed logic, and sensor calibration. Ablation studies show that rotation increases state diversity, while dissipation provides controlled forgetting and improves predictive conditioning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Echo-state networks (ESNs) are a type of reservoir computing where the recurrent dynamics are fixed and only a linear readout is trained. Conventional reservoirs typically use a single random recurrent matrix, which couples signal mixing, memory retention, and stability. Lindblad dynamics, originally from open quantum systems, describe the evolution of a system coupled to a Markovian reservoir, providing a framework for separating reversible (Hamiltonian) and irreversible (dissipative) processes.

The paper introduces NuclearDiffusion, a study that fine-tunes three open-source diffusion models (SDXL, SD-v3.5-Medium, and Flux.1) on a curated dataset of 1,000 captioned nuclear energy images. It evaluates their performance against zero-shot baselines and commercial systems, showing that fine-tuning significantly improves SDXL but yields limited gains for SD-v3.5-Medium and no measurable improvement for Flux.1. This work addresses a critical gap in domain-specific text-to-image generation, particularly for specialized engineering fields like nuclear energy where general-purpose models often produce inaccurate or inconsistent images. By demonstrating that fine-tuning open-source models can outperform commercial systems on specialized prompts, it provides a practical pathway for developing trustworthy generative AI tools for domain-specific applications. The dataset covers reactors, fuel cycles, radiation, and related concepts. The study uses both quantitative image-similarity metrics and qualitative expert assessment, and compares fine-tuned models against GPT-Image-2, Gemini-3.1-Flash-Image, and Midjourney, finding that fine-tuned open-source models produce more accurate outputs on specialized engineering prompts.

EDATracer is a new agentic framework that organizes EDA artifacts into a knowledge graph and semantic vector index, enabling LLM agents to perform evidence-grounded analysis. It introduces an 18.9 GB dataset of 2,787 open-source chip designs and a 90-question benchmark, achieving best pass@1 accuracy over Cursor and Claude Code while using fewer tokens. It demonstrates that grounding LLM agents with structured knowledge can significantly improve accuracy and efficiency, potentially accelerating chip design workflows. The framework combines a knowledge graph with a semantic vector index to retrieve evidence across source files, logs, netlists, and reports. In evaluations, EDATracer outperformed Cursor and Claude Code by 6.4 and 7.2 percentage points on average in pass@1 accuracy, while using 2.0-3.2x fewer tokens.

A new preprint (arXiv:2608.04045) evaluates personalized federated learning for aircraft engine remaining-useful-life (RUL) prediction under both benign and adversarial client heterogeneity, showing that shared-representation personalization closes about 70% of the local-to-centralized root-mean-square-error gap. This work addresses a critical intersection of federated learning and safety-critical aerospace prognostics, demonstrating that personalization can significantly improve model performance while robust aggregation is essential to defend against adversarial attacks. The findings have implications for collaborative maintenance across aircraft fleets, where data privacy and model safety are paramount. The study uses a multi-task 1D CNN on a structurally non-IID partition of the C-MAPSS benchmark, comparing four remedies for benign heterogeneity and five attacks against four aggregation methods. A physically motivated sensor-value backdoor achieves a 94.9% attack success rate against standard averaging, while Krum reduces attack success by an order of magnitude; combining personalization with robust aggregation restores robustness (2.8% attack success) with minimal accuracy cost.

## [Temporal QBER ML Framework Detects Stealthy Eavesdropping in BB84 QKD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04047) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a temporal QBER-based machine learning framework for detecting and classifying eavesdropping attacks in BB84 QKD systems, achieving 88.01% accuracy with XGBoost, compared to only 25.82% for a fixed 11% QBER threshold. This addresses a critical security gap in QKD systems, as stealthy attacks can evade conventional threshold-based monitoring. The framework significantly reduces the false negative rate from 0.8477 to 0.0198, enhancing the practical security of quantum key distribution. The framework extracts 63 physics-informed temporal features, including burst behavior, temporal instability, basis-dependent asymmetry, and QBER loss interactions. It evaluates Random Forest, XGBoost, and SVM-RBF classifiers on seven attack types and a normal channel scenario under noisy and lossy conditions, with SHAP explainability confirming the discriminative power of the features.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: BB84 is a quantum key distribution protocol proposed by Bennett and Brassard in 1984, where Alice and Bob exchange qubits to generate a secure key. QBER (Quantum Bit Error Rate) is a key metric in QKD, and a fixed 11% threshold is commonly used to detect eavesdropping. However, sophisticated attacks can stay below this threshold, motivating the need for more advanced detection methods.

<a id="item-171"></a>
## [LaPrune: Exact-Budget Differentiable Sparsity with Theoretical Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04057) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LaPrune introduces a differentiable layer that enforces exact-budget sparsity by controlling the normalized second moment while preserving the selected mass, using a LapSum barrier. It provides theoretical guarantees on the saturated fraction and near-zero fraction, and the hardness parameter is invariant to score scale. This work addresses a key challenge in sparse model training: enabling gradient flow through hard top-k selection without sacrificing exact sparsity budgets. It could improve the efficiency and effectiveness of sparse autoencoders and other large-scale models, potentially impacting deployment in resource-constrained environments. The method uses a LapSum barrier to preserve selection mass and a normalized second-moment constraint to transition from dense to hard top-k. It derives a population prediction of the saturated fraction, a near-binary limiting law, and a tight worst-case guarantee on the near-zero fraction, with the hardness parameter being scale-invariant.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Top-k selection is a common technique for inducing sparsity in neural networks, but hard selection blocks gradients, while continuous relaxations often couple mask hardness to the selected mass. Differentiable masking methods approximate hard operators with smooth surrogates to enable gradient-based optimization. LaPrune builds on these ideas by providing an exact-budget differentiable layer with theoretical guarantees.

## [SJEPA: Hybrid Symbolic-Neural JEPA Learns Compact Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04060) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SJEPA introduces a reconstruction-free JEPA framework that combines symbolic regression with neural corrections to learn compact symbolic dynamics, addressing representation collapse and non-identifiability. In pendulum experiments, joint learning discovers simpler symbolic dynamics with lower long-horizon rollout error than post-hoc fitting. This work bridges interpretable symbolic models with powerful neural representations, potentially enabling more transparent and efficient world models for AI systems. It addresses critical challenges in representation learning, such as collapse and non-identifiability, which are relevant to self-supervised learning and model-based reinforcement learning. The framework formalizes induced-dynamics complexity and shows that unconstrained operator compression can shortcut to representation collapse. It supports both alternating representation-equation learning and symbolic dynamics fitted to fixed representations, with correction regularization handling grammar misspecification.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Joint-embedding predictive architectures (JEPA) learn by predicting abstract representations rather than reconstructing raw inputs, as proposed by Yann LeCun. Symbolic regression searches for mathematical expressions that fit data, offering interpretable models. Representation collapse is a failure mode where different inputs map to similar embeddings, losing information.

This paper introduces Guarded-V2X, an inline semantic guardrail architecture designed to secure LLM-enabled V2X services under real-time constraints. It integrates rule-based ingress filtering, a lightweight safety classifier, policy-constrained structured generation, trusted-only retrieval, and post-decision adjudication to enforce machine-checkable safety boundaries. This work addresses a novel and timely problem: securing LLM-enabled V2X systems against prompt-level attacks, which are not covered by traditional V2X security mechanisms. The proposed architecture could enhance the safety and reliability of intelligent transportation systems, benefiting researchers and practitioners in AI safety and transportation. The evaluation uses a four-stage pipeline including intrusion vulnerability analysis, calibration and latency benchmarking, guardrail validation, and robustness under adversarial stress. Results show that Guarded-V2X reduces intrusion acceptance success rates and eliminates observed unsafe completions in two-turn settings without exceeding latency budgets for V2X semantic advisory paths.

## [Leveraging Historical Gold Standard Data to Improve Multi-Fidelity Bayesian Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04113) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a method to incorporate out-of-the-loop gold standard data, i.e., historical high-fidelity observations, into multi-fidelity Bayesian optimization (MF-BO), addressing the suboptimality of standard MF-BO when the highest-fidelity function is too expensive to query. The approach uses task descriptors to transfer information from previous experiments to the current optimization task. This work addresses a practical limitation of MF-BO in real-world scenarios where high-fidelity evaluations are prohibitively expensive, such as molecular optimization and hyperparameter tuning. By leveraging existing gold standard data, it can improve optimization efficiency and reduce costs, benefiting fields that rely on expensive black-box optimization. The method requires task descriptors, which can be explicitly provided or extracted from unstructured metadata, to relate historical data to the current task. The authors demonstrate effectiveness on synthetic functions, chemistry problems, and hyperparameter optimization, showing improvements over standard MF-BO baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-fidelity Bayesian optimization (MF-BO) is a technique for optimizing expensive black-box functions by leveraging cheaper lower-fidelity proxies. Standard MF-BO assumes the highest-fidelity function can be queried during optimization, but in many real-world tasks, this is too costly. This paper introduces a way to use historical high-fidelity data, obtained from previous experiments, to improve the optimization process.

## [LiNC: Lightweight Label Noise Correction for Medical Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04147) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LiNC introduces a per-sample trainable trust parameter and a 3-component Gaussian Mixture Model to correct label noise in medical imaging datasets, achieving consistent accuracy gains on MedMNISTv2 under up to 50% label noise. Label noise is a critical issue in medical imaging, where annotation errors and inter-rater variability can undermine model reliability. LiNC offers a lightweight, effective solution that can improve clinical AI robustness without significant computational overhead. The method uses a convex combination of the observed label and the model's predictive distribution, controlled by the trust parameter. It employs a short soft-correction phase followed by a hard correction phase, and adds memory that grows linearly with training set size.

## [AgentForge: Role-Playing Platform for Learning Agentic Software Engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04148) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AgentForge is an immersive learning platform where novices adopt one of four software engineering roles—Task Planner, Patch Author, Code Reviewer, or Test Runner—within a multi-agent code-repair workflow, with AI agents handling the other roles. A study with 37 novice developers showed high task completion rates and significant learning gains, though the Code Reviewer role was the most challenging. This platform addresses a critical gap in education for novices learning to work with agentic AI in software development, which is increasingly prevalent in industry. By providing role-based scaffolding and metacognitive support, AgentForge helps novices develop both software engineering skills and the ability to critically evaluate AI outputs, preparing them for the evolving landscape of AI-assisted development. In the study, the Code Reviewer practice required significantly more interaction turns, reroutes, and completion time (p_adj = .004) and was perceived as the most challenging. Participants reported significant gains in understanding software repair and agent collaboration (p_adj < .001), indicating the platform's effectiveness despite varying interaction demands.

**Background**: Agentic AI refers to AI systems that can autonomously plan, execute, and coordinate tasks, often in multi-agent setups, to achieve complex goals. Educational scaffolding is a teaching method that provides temporary support to learners, gradually fading as they gain competence, and is central to AgentForge's design.

## [Visualizing Graph-to-Answer Mechanism Recovery in Materials-Science Hypothesis Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04170) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a visual diagnostic workflow for tracing and analyzing mechanism recovery in Graph-PRefLexOR-8B, a Qwen3-8B model adapted for graph-native scientific reasoning. The workflow combines semantic backtracking, graph corruption, activation-based recovery measurements, and layer-by-token-region grids to inspect the graph-to-answer pathway. This work addresses the critical issue of interpretability in AI co-scientists, ensuring that generated hypotheses preserve scientifically meaningful mechanisms rather than just fluency. It provides a tool for scientists and model developers to identify where mechanism support is lost or regained, enhancing reliability before downstream experimental planning. The study uses 100 open-ended materials-science questions and performs a full sweep over 37 residual-stream checkpoints (embedding output and 36 transformer blocks). Results show little mechanism recovery in the early transition region (layers 7-10), with recovery concentrating in late synthesis and answer-start regions around layers 30 and 36.

The residual stream in transformers is a latent vector per position that evolves through layers, with each attention head and feed-forward sublayer performing read-then-write operations on it. ## [Patients-like-me: Variational LM-GNN Framework for Explainable Clinical Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04193) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Patients-like-me (PLM), a unified framework that combines language models and graph neural networks to integrate local patient semantics with global cohort structure for clinical prediction. It proposes a Variational Expectation-Maximization algorithm for efficient training and demonstrates consistent performance improvements over state-of-the-art methods on MIMIC-III and MIMIC-IV. This framework addresses two key limitations in clinical prediction: the isolation of patient sequences in language models and the reliance on high-quality representations in graph neural networks. By integrating both local and global information, PLM improves prediction accuracy and provides reference-patient explanations, which could enhance trust and adoption of AI in healthcare. PLM consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods on MIMIC-III and MIMIC-IV, with improvements generalizing across both encoder-only and decoder-only LM backbones. The gains are achieved with only modest additional computational overhead, and edge-masking experiments confirm that the highest-ranked reference patients have the greatest impact on predictions.

MIMIC-III and MIMIC-IV are publicly available critical care databases that provide deidentified clinical data for research. The Variational Expectation-Maximization algorithm is an extension of the EM algorithm that incorporates variational inference for approximate posterior estimation.

This paper proposes using model merging techniques to create a unified cross-domain code clone detector, overcoming the fragmentation of specialized models. The approach achieves a combined F1 of 0.865 on UniXcoder with TIES merging, reaching 93% of multi-task performance without any training data at the merging step. This work addresses a critical problem in clone detection where specialized models degrade significantly outside their training distribution, with F1 drops exceeding 70%. By enabling effective cross-domain detection without retraining, it offers a practical and cost-efficient solution for code analysis tools, potentially improving software maintenance and security. The study evaluates parameter merging with five task-vector methods, architecture merging via greedy layer stitching, and cross-tokenizer alignment across four code models, three benchmarks, and twelve configurations. TIES merging is recommended for better generalization to unseen AI-generated clones, while cross-base merging yields only marginal gains, indicating that a shared pre-trained base is crucial.

This paper introduces a physics-grounded benchmark for evaluating LLM planning strategies in cyber-physical systems, using a smart-grid demand-response scenario with 40 heterogeneous prosumers. It employs paired forced-mode counterfactuals and event-level deadline feasibility to assess planning architectures beyond simple task success. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in LLM planning evaluation by considering how autonomous participants and physical constraints affect outcomes, which is essential for real-world deployment in critical infrastructure. It provides a rigorous method to compare planning architectures, potentially guiding the design of safer and more reliable LLM-based control systems. The benchmark implements four executors (predefined, sequential, hierarchical, search) and finds that forced search is the oracle in all five baseline seeds. ## [Lexi-LowGLM: Efficient Online Algorithm for Lexicographic Low-Rank Matrix Bandits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04324) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Lexi-LowGLM, an online algorithm for generalized low-rank matrix bandits with lexicographic multi-objective preferences. It updates each objective-specific estimator via an online Newton step, reducing the estimator-update complexity from O(T^2) to O(T) over T rounds. This work fills a gap in multi-objective online learning by addressing lexicographic preferences in low-rank matrix bandits, which is common in real-world sequential decision-making. The computational efficiency improvement makes it practical for large-scale applications, potentially impacting fields like recommendation systems and clinical trials. The regret bound for each objective i is O~(W_i^lex * sqrt(m) * (d1+d2) * r * sqrt(T)), depending on the effective low-rank dimension (d1+d2)r rather than the ambient dimension d1*d2. Numerical experiments validate the effectiveness and computational efficiency of the proposed method.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Generalized low-rank matrix bandits extend multi-armed bandits to matrix-valued arms with low-rank structure, often modeled under the generalized linear model (GLM) framework. Lexicographic optimization is a multi-objective optimization approach where objectives are prioritized in a strict order, optimizing higher-priority objectives first. The online Newton step is an online optimization technique that updates parameters using second-order information, offering faster convergence than first-order methods.

## [Constrained Mixed-Strategy GroupDRO for Equitable System-Prompt Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04339) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a constrained mixed-strategy GroupDRO framework that assigns weights to existing system prompts to minimize worst-case information-quality loss across different question phrasings, while keeping average performance close to baseline. Across five LLMs on two bilingual benchmarks, it reduces Overall Mean, Worst 25% Mean, and Worst by 13.1%, 13.2%, and 13.7% on average relative to no mitigation. This work addresses a critical fairness issue in LLM-based information seeking, where semantically equivalent questions phrased differently can receive unequal answer quality. By improving worst-case performance without sacrificing average quality, it offers a practical method to enhance equity in LLM deployments, benefiting users who may otherwise receive subpar responses. The framework decouples pool generation from selection, making it applicable to any system-prompt pool and enabling an ensemble of complementary prompts. Experiments were conducted on two bilingual medical and consumer-finance benchmarks across five LLMs, and the multi-prompt weights reveal complementarity across metric-group pairs. Code and data are available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Group Distributionally Robust Optimization (GroupDRO) is a robust learning framework that minimizes worst-case loss across predefined groups to ensure fair performance. System prompts are instructions given to LLMs to steer their behavior, but they are typically optimized for average-case quality, leading to disparities across different question phrasings. This paper applies GroupDRO principles to system-prompt selection, using a mixed-strategy approach to assign weights to multiple prompts rather than selecting a single one.

## [iStructTab: Graph-Enhanced Descriptor Sequencing for Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04348) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces iStructTab, a method that uses Graph-Enhanced Descriptor Sequencing (GEDS) to improve multimodal learning with images and tabular data. GEDS refines statistical descriptors via similarity graph computations and integrates order-aware memory tokens in a transformer framework, reducing feature dispersion and enhancing predictive performance. This work addresses a known problem in multimodal learning—ineffective representations causing redundancy and dispersion—with a principled, graph-based approach. It demonstrates empirical improvements across benchmarks, highlighting the importance of structured feature sequencing, which could influence future multimodal model designs. GEDS is grounded in the Column Permutation Problem (CPP) and uses similarity graph-based computations to determine feature sequencing. The method is incorporated into an order-aware efficient transformer with memory tokens and a dedicated loss function, and experiments show reduced feature dispersion and improved robustness.

**Background**: Multimodal learning combines data from different modalities, such as images and tabular data, but often suffers from ineffective representations. The Column Permutation Problem (CPP) involves finding an optimal ordering of columns in a matrix, which inspires GEDS to sequence features effectively. Transformers are a popular architecture for handling sequential data, and order-aware memory tokens help the model adhere to the derived feature ordering.

## [HyPASE: Hyperbolic PEFT for Speech Emotion Recognition in LALMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04351) ⭐️ 7.0/10

HyPASE introduces a hyperbolic parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) framework for speech emotion recognition (SER) in large audio-language models (LALMs), leveraging the Poincaré ball model to capture multi-granularity emotion cues. It comprises a Hyperbolic Geometric Adapter (HGA) for layer-adaptive weight modulation and an Emotion-aware Multi-capacity Cross-modal Aggregator (EMCA) for compressing multi-scale features into audio prefixes. This work addresses a key limitation of Euclidean PEFT methods in capturing the hierarchical nature of emotion cues, potentially improving SER performance in resource-constrained settings. It offers a more efficient and effective path for adapting LALMs to fine-grained affective tasks, which could benefit applications in human-computer interaction and affective computing. HyPASE outperforms Euclidean PEFT baselines on MELD across all metrics and achieves notable Unweighted Accuracy gains on IEMOCAP, especially for class-imbalanced emotion recognition, with a slight Weighted Accuracy trade-off. It also demonstrates robust zero-shot cross-dataset generalization within a constrained parameter budget.

**Background**: Large Audio-Language Models (LALMs) excel at general speech understanding but struggle with fine-grained tasks like Speech Emotion Recognition (SER). Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT) methods adapt pre-trained models with minimal parameters, but they typically operate in Euclidean space, which may not capture the multi-granularity nature of emotion cues. Hyperbolic geometry, such as the Poincaré ball model, is well-suited for representing hierarchical data, making it a promising alternative for emotion modeling.
