## [HyperTrust: Robust Hypergraph Learning Under Label Noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04377) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a systematic study of hypergraph node classification under label noise and proposes a novel robust framework, HyperTrust, which estimates hyperedge trustworthiness and adjusts the hypergraph structure to mitigate noisy labels. Label noise is a common issue in real-world data, and hypergraph neural networks (HGNNs) are vulnerable to it. This work addresses an underexplored problem, providing a unified benchmark and an effective solution that could improve the reliability of HGNNs in practical applications. HyperTrust consists of two modules: HyperedgeBoost, which connects unlabeled nodes to trustworthy hyperedges, and HyperedgePrune, which removes untrustworthy node-hyperedge incidences. The framework is evaluated on multiple hypergraph datasets under various noisy settings, with extensive experiments and theoretical analysis demonstrating its effectiveness.

Learning with label noise (LLN) and graph learning with label noise (GLN) have been studied, but noisy-label learning on hypergraphs remains underexplored.

## [CNN-QNN Hybrid Model Optimizes Correlated Features for Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04379) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a novel method to optimize the correlation among CNN features used as inputs to a QNN, intentionally introducing correlated features rather than using orthogonal decomposition. Monte Carlo simulations and experiments on CIFAR-10, Fashion-MNIST, and radar micro-Doppler data show that an average correlation of 0.5 yields optimal classification accuracy. This work challenges the common assumption that uncorrelated or orthogonal features are best for QNN inputs, showing that moderate correlation aligns with QNN's entanglement structure and improves accuracy and stability. It highlights the potential of QNNs to surpass classical classifiers as qubit availability grows, impacting the field of quantum machine learning. The method introduces a correlation-regularization term on CNN outputs to drive off-diagonal entries of the feature correlation matrix toward a target constant. Across all datasets, intermediate correlation consistently improved accuracy and reduced variance compared to low, high, or unregulated correlations, without modifying the quantum circuit.

**Background**: Quantum neural networks (QNNs) are parameterized quantum models that exploit superposition and entanglement, potentially offering advantages over classical neural networks. In hybrid CNN-QNN models, features extracted by a classical CNN are fed into a QNN for classification. Prior approaches often used orthogonal decomposition to decorrelate features, but this paper argues that correlated features are more physically compatible with QNNs due to entanglement. Datasets like CIFAR-10 and Fashion-MNIST are standard benchmarks for image classification.

## [NodeJEPA: Structure-Conditioned Latent Prediction for Node-Level Graph SSL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04381) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NodeJEPA introduces a joint-embedding predictive architecture (JEPA) for node-level graph self-supervised learning, which masks structure-aware k-hop ego-subgraphs and predicts latent representations of masked nodes using a structure-conditioned predictor. This approach avoids input reconstruction and hand-crafted augmentations, instead operating entirely in latent space. This work addresses a gap in graph self-supervised learning by extending JEPA-style methods to node-level tasks, potentially improving representation quality by focusing on relational structure rather than low-level input statistics. It offers a practical recipe that could influence future graph SSL research and applications. NodeJEPA uses an EMA-updated target encoder with stop-gradient, and integrates spectral and centrality descriptors through cross-attention in the predictor. It also employs variance, covariance, and Laplacian spectral regularizers, along with an optional curriculum that gradually increases masking difficulty. The model is evaluated on node classification benchmarks under linear probing and fine-tuning protocols.

**Background**: Joint-embedding predictive architectures (JEPA) are a self-supervised learning paradigm that predicts latent representations of target blocks from context blocks, avoiding input reconstruction. In graph learning, ego-subgraphs are local neighborhoods centered at a node, and spectral and centrality descriptors capture structural properties. EMA (exponential moving average) updates are commonly used in student-teacher frameworks to stabilize training.

## [RA*pex: Efficient Approximate Multi-Objective Search Under Rulebooks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04398) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces RA*pex, a best-first search algorithm that computes a compact set of epsilon-approximate rulebook-optimal solutions for multi-objective robotic planning. It also formalizes the concept of epsilon-rule-dominance, a principled notion of approximate dominance under rulebooks. This work addresses the computational expense of exact rulebook-optimal planning, offering a principled approximation that is over two orders of magnitude faster than existing methods. It bridges the gap between exact rulebook-based planning and approximate multi-objective search, potentially benefiting robotics and AI planning applications that require balancing safety, efficiency, and compliance. RA*pex leverages dimensionality reduction to speed up search while respecting rule hierarchies by maintaining separate closed sets and performing dominance checks over truncated and residual rule sets. The paper provides a formal proof that every rulebook-optimal solution is epsilon-rule-dominated by at least one solution in the returned set.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Robotic planning often involves multiple objectives with complex priority relationships, such as safety, efficiency, and regulatory compliance. Rulebooks formalize these relationships, allowing partial ordering of objectives that generalizes both Pareto and lexicographic dominance. Computing the full set of rulebook-optimal solutions is computationally expensive, motivating the need for approximation techniques.

## [MESH: Memory-Efficient Sinkhorn Optimization for MoE Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04407) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MESH, a hidden-momentum Sinkhorn update for MoE experts, which reduces optimizer memory by 62.5% and peak CUDA allocation by about 12.6% relative to AdamW, while maintaining a modest evaluation-loss gap in a 110M-parameter DeepSeek-style MoE pretraining setup. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in MoE training—memory-efficient optimization—by showing that temporal smoothing, not full coordinate-wise state, is the key for expert matrices. It offers a practical path to reduce memory footprint in large-scale MoE models, which are increasingly used in modern LLMs like DeepSeek and Mixtral. The study uses a controlled 110M-parameter nanowhale DeepSeek-style MoE pretraining setup. A SAGE/Sinkhorn hybrid reduces optimizer state from 0.883GB to 0.331GB but degrades evaluation loss to 3.8265, while AdamW baselines achieve 3.58–3.64. MESH restores a temporal first-moment signal through the gradient-buffer lifecycle without storing expert first moment as optimizer state, and an optional block-preconditioned variant (MESH-B) adds a coarse neuron/block inverse-RMS multiplier.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) is an architecture that activates only a subset of parameters per token, enabling efficient scaling of large language models. Memory-efficient optimizers like Sinkhorn gradient descent reduce optimizer state for dense matrices, but their direct application to MoE training is unreliable because expert gradients are conditional and temporally varying. MESH addresses this by introducing hidden momentum, which provides temporal smoothing without storing full optimizer state.

## [Counterfactual Recoverability Improves On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04408) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a counterfactual recoverability framework for on-policy distillation, which determines whether student errors are correctable by replaying error states through teacher-continuation and rollback branches. The method achieves an AUC of 1.000 for recoverability prediction, far surpassing divergence-based methods at 0.392, and improves performance on AIME and GPQA benchmarks. This work addresses a fundamental limitation of divergence-based supervision in on-policy distillation, which cannot distinguish correctable errors from irreversible ones. By introducing recoverability as a decision variable, it enables more selective and effective trajectory supervision, potentially improving the efficiency and performance of RL and knowledge distillation for large language models. The framework categorizes states as recoverable, irreversible-but-avoidable, or ambiguous based on the relative success of teacher-continuation versus rollback branches. On AIME branch diagnostics, the mean continuation-minus-rollback effect is 0.185 for recoverable states and -1.000 for irreversible-but-avoidable states, demonstrating opposite intervention preferences. Recoverability-aware control achieves 0.578 success on held-out AIME2025, compared to 0.517 for the best baseline, and improves average@32 on AIME2024-2025 and GPQA-Diamond.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) is a technique where a student model learns from a teacher by sampling its own trajectories and matching the teacher's outputs at those states. Traditional divergence-based methods measure the difference between student and teacher distributions but cannot determine if an error is correctable. Counterfactual reasoning involves considering alternative outcomes, and here it is used to evaluate whether a different action could have led to a better result. The paper is a preprint and has not yet been widely validated.

## [GOAL: Generative Framework for Constrained Incentivized Advertising](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04421) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces GOAL, a constraint-aware generative framework that formulates incentive allocation as a conditional sequence generation problem, and proposes Safe Constrained Policy Optimization (SCPO) to learn a single generative policy that generalizes across various ROI constraints without retraining. Experiments on large-scale real-world data and a synthetic fatigue-aware environment show that GOAL improves long-term revenue and user retention while substantially reducing ROI violation rates compared to strong baselines. This work addresses a significant challenge in incentivized advertising—optimizing continuous incentive magnitudes under strict global constraints—which existing uplift modeling and constrained reinforcement learning approaches struggle with. By introducing a generative paradigm and a flexible constraint-control method, it could improve the efficiency and effectiveness of advertising systems, benefiting advertisers and platforms alike. GOAL integrates a hierarchical causal state encoder to capture both local behavioral dynamics and long-range dependencies, and SCPO enables flexible constraint control by learning a single generative policy that generalizes across a spectrum of ROI constraints. The framework is evaluated on large-scale real-world data and a synthetic fatigue-aware environment, demonstrating improvements in long-term revenue and user retention while reducing ROI violation rates.

## [D2F-ReAG: Dynamic Decomposition and Filtering for Multi-Hop RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04444) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces D2F-ReAG, a novel paradigm that adaptively controls reasoning depth in multi-hop retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) by judging the reliability of root-level reasoning. If the root reasoning is reliable, it directly generates the answer; otherwise, it decomposes the question into sub-questions and uses verified reasoning to refine the root reasoning. This approach addresses a known limitation in multi-hop RAG—the lack of dynamic decomposition and effective filtering—which often leads to lower efficiency and accuracy. By improving accuracy and efficiency on multi-hop benchmarks, D2F-ReAG could enhance the reliability of RAG systems in complex question-answering tasks, benefiting applications that require cross-document reasoning. The method is evaluated on three multi-hop benchmarks, demonstrating its effectiveness in handling complex multi-hop questions. The paper is available on arXiv (arXiv:2608.04444) and was announced as a cross-type submission, indicating it may have been submitted to a conference.

Existing methods, such as graph-structured RAG or question decomposition, often lack dynamic decomposition and effective filtering, leading to suboptimal performance. D2F-ReAG builds on these ideas by introducing a dynamic mechanism that decides whether to decompose a question based on the reliability of the initial reasoning.

## [When Does Downscaled Training Preserve Diffusion Gradients?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04448) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper analyzes when training on downscaled images preserves gradient signal in diffusion transformers, revealing a noise-dependent term and a sigma-independent floor that limits downscaling benefits. This work addresses a significant open problem in diffusion model training efficiency by providing a theoretical decomposition and empirical validation. The findings could inform practical training strategies, potentially reducing training time by 14.6% while maintaining near-native performance. The gradient change under downscaling is decomposed into a noise-dependent term governed by the downscale ratio and a sigma-independent floor determined by the target grid's absolute token count. The measured route-sigma map reveals a window (0.65 < sigma < 0.95) on the 1024->768 route where downscaled gradients stay close to native, and training LoRA adapters with downscaled steps reduces training time by 14.6% at a fixed step budget.

This paper extends that analysis to the gradient signal, which is crucial for training efficiency.

## [Eigenius: Typed Knowledge-Graph DBMS with Epistemic Stratification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04457) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Eigenius is an open-source typed knowledge-graph DBMS that integrates dependent type theory, institutions, and content-addressed storage to enforce epistemic status as a structural invariant. It was introduced in a preprint (arXiv:2608.04457) and demonstrated by recomputing a published Nature study, where all 52 derived conclusions held and four machine-checked discrepancies were surfaced. This architecture addresses critical challenges in AI-driven research and data provenance by making auditability a built-in property rather than an afterthought. It could significantly improve trust and reproducibility in scientific workflows, especially as AI scientists increasingly rely on stateful, interconnected evidence. The kernel rests on three pillars: dependent type theory, institutions as strongly typed integration boundaries, and content-addressed immutable storage. Cross-system translations (comorphisms) are checked at commit and materialized as first-class resources, and shared on-chain intermediate representations (IRs) eliminate O(N^2) polystore bottlenecks. It also embeds a fast, in-process term checker for Lean 4 to safely evaluate formal mathematical proofs without IPC overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Dependent type theory is a type system where types can depend on values, enabling precise specifications and reducing bugs. Institutions, introduced by Goguen and Burstall, provide a formal framework for specifying logical systems and their translations. Content-addressed storage (CAS) uses cryptographic hashes to create unique keys for data, ensuring integrity and deduplication. These concepts are combined in Eigenius to create a unified kernel for knowledge graphs with strong provenance guarantees.

## [Neural Bilinear Dynamical Models for Nonlinear Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04471) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Neural Bilinear Dynamical Model (NBDM), which uses Koopman theory to lift nonlinear dynamics into a higher-dimensional latent space and models state evolution with a bilinear dynamical system, incorporating a parameterized error compensation term. It also proposes a memory-enhanced controller to handle missing control inputs. NBDM addresses limitations of linear and Koopman-linearized models in capturing complex nonlinear behaviors, potentially improving long-horizon forecasting accuracy across various real-world applications. This could benefit fields like finance, weather prediction, and autonomous systems where accurate multi-step forecasting is critical. The model integrates control inputs explicitly, using auxiliary variables when available and learned feedback signals otherwise. Experiments on five real-world datasets show consistent outperformance over baselines in both given-control and missing-control settings, especially for multi-step and long-horizon forecasting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Time series forecasting often deals with nonlinear dynamical systems, which are challenging to model. Koopman theory provides a way to represent nonlinear dynamics as linear operators in a higher-dimensional space, but linear approximations can be insufficient. Bilinear dynamical systems offer a middle ground by allowing state-dependent control, and neural networks can learn these representations from data.

## [EndoVLM: Anatomy-Guided Vision-Language Model for Endoscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04472) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EndoVLM is a novel vision-language pretraining model for endoscopy, trained on over 348,000 endoscopic examinations with paired clinical reports and images. It introduces an Anatomy-Guided Sparse Pooling mechanism and a Progressive Semantic-Aware Alignment strategy to bridge the modality gap between textual descriptions and redundant visual data. This work addresses a critical gap in endoscopy foundation models by leveraging rich clinical report semantics, potentially improving downstream tasks like lesion detection and classification. Its robust zero-shot generalization suggests broad clinical applicability, advancing medical AI toward more efficient and accurate endoscopic analysis. The model uses Anatomy-Guided Sparse Pooling to aggregate semantically salient frames into anatomy-specific representations, and Progressive Semantic-Aware Alignment models clinical taxonomy via structured soft targets. A Semantic-Concentrated Masked Autoencoder is applied to semantic-rich frames, integrating low-level visual precision with high-level semantics. Experiments show EndoVLM outperforms existing foundation models and is competitive with task-specific methods.

AudioScape-TTA introduces a structured, complexity-aware benchmark for fine-grained text-to-audio (TTA) evaluation, featuring 2,258 audio-text pairs and 25,707 binary QA rubrics. It proposes a rubric-based, audio-grounded evaluation framework that verifies event realization, acoustic attributes, and speech content. This benchmark addresses a known limitation in existing TTA evaluation metrics, which rely on global similarity and fail to capture fine-grained semantic failures. It enables more interpretable and scalable analysis of TTA systems, potentially guiding future model improvements and standardization in the field. The benchmark characterizes generation complexity using event density and structural complexity, and its evaluation framework uses fine-grained semantic criteria. Experiments on 13 open-source TTA models reveal persistent limitations in attribute control, speech-content preservation, and compositional generation; human validation shows stronger alignment with human judgments than global similarity metrics.

## [AFD-Ledger: Analytical Provisioning for Attention-FFN Disaggregation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04502) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AFD-Ledger is an offline analytical provisioning system that compares Attention-FFN Disaggregation (AFD) with collocated deployment for serving Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models. It optimizes hardware assignment and deployment organization to determine which architecture yields higher throughput under given SLOs and hardware constraints. This work addresses a critical deployment question for MoE-based LLM serving, providing a systematic method to decide between AFD and collocated architectures. It can significantly reduce the cost and complexity of provisioning decisions, impacting system designers and operators in the AI infrastructure ecosystem. AFD-Ledger reduces complete deployment evaluations by 68.8%–83.5% while still recovering the globally optimal deployment in spaces where exhaustive provisioning is feasible. On physical LongCat 2.0 deployments, it preserves the correct architecture decision and predicts AFD-to-collocated throughput within 6.6%–9.6% of measurement.

**Background**: Attention-FFN Disaggregation (AFD) is an emerging architecture for serving Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) language models, where attention and feed-forward network (FFN) components are decoupled to better utilize compute and memory resources. MoE models use multiple specialized sub-models (experts) and a gating mechanism to route inputs, improving efficiency. Time-per-output-token (TPOT) is a key service-level objective (SLO) for streaming AI applications, measuring the latency of generating each subsequent token. Provisioning involves selecting hardware and organizing deployment to meet SLOs while maximizing throughput.

## [GeoReward: Mitigating Contextual Variable Overestimation in VLMs for Cross-Market Ad Preference Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04504) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel failure mode in vision-language models (VLMs) called Contextual Variable Overestimation (CVE), where models over-rely on dominant visual-textual cues and ignore sparse but critical contextual variables. To address this, the authors propose GeoReward, a reward model with three mechanisms, and introduce the Multi-Country Ad Click Preference (MACP) dataset for cross-market ad preference prediction. This work addresses a systematic bias in VLMs that can lead to poor performance in real-world applications like advertising, where market-specific context is crucial. By providing a diagnostic framework and a targeted solution, it could improve the effectiveness of cross-market advertising and inform future research on mitigating similar biases in multimodal models. GeoReward integrates three mechanisms: Market-Aware Retrieval Augmentation, Context-Guided Visual Modulation, and Selective Sensitivity Loss. Experiments show that GeoReward significantly alleviates CVE and improves prediction accuracy by over 15.75% on the MACP dataset compared to baselines.

Contextual Variable Overestimation (CVE) refers to the tendency of VLMs to overemphasize dominant features while ignoring sparse but decision-critical contextual information, such as market-specific cues.

## [CARVE: Training-Free Token Compression for Efficient 3D Medical Volume Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04515) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CARVE is a training-free framework that compresses visual tokens in slice-based multimodal LLMs for 3D medical volumes, casting token reduction as budget-constrained 2.5D allocation. It removes about 80% of visual tokens on Hulu-Med-7B while preserving 98.1% of full-token performance across three VQA benchmarks. This addresses the inefficiency of slice-based MLLMs for 3D medical volumes, where thousands of redundant tokens burden the LLM. By enabling selective token allocation, CARVE can reduce computational costs and improve scalability, benefiting medical imaging AI applications. CARVE partitions the depth axis into coherent windows and allocates tokens non-uniformly based on normalized cross-slice evidence. It builds spatial anchors on representative slices and merges eligible tokens into nearby anchors, leading all compression baselines on AMOS-MM report-generation metrics with a 6.2-point higher retention of full-token quality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Slice-based multimodal large language models (MLLMs) represent 3D volumes as sequences of 2D slices, leveraging mature 2D encoders. However, this produces thousands of visual tokens with overlapping evidence, leading to diminishing returns as token budgets grow. Existing token compression methods are designed for 2D images or videos, not for the near-duplicate content along the depth axis in 3D medical volumes.

## [MSRT: Resource-Aware Mixture of Speech Encoders Breaks Curse of Multilinguality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04586) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MSRT, a framework with a resource-aware Mixture of Speech Encoders (MoSE) and a five-stage curriculum learning strategy, achieving state-of-the-art performance on 45 languages across all 45x44 translation directions with only 10 hours of paired data per language. This work addresses the curse of multilinguality in speech-to-text translation, where low-resource languages suffer performance degradation. By improving low-resource performance without compromising high-resource languages, it could enable more inclusive multilingual translation systems and benefit languages with limited data. The MoSE uses an explicit language router to assign each utterance to a frozen expert (for high-resource languages) or a trainable expert (for medium/low-resource languages). The 4B-parameter model outperforms larger baselines, and the curriculum learning reduces data dependence to 10 hours per language.

## [CROWN-QA Benchmark Reveals LLMs' Unstable Negative Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04591) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces CROWN-QA, a new benchmark for evaluating completeness-sensitive negative reasoning in LLMs, comprising CROWN-Synth and CROWN-Real. Across three LLM families, models show unstable closure judgments and substantial over-closure, failing to reliably distinguish justified negative answers from insufficient evidence. This work highlights a critical limitation in LLM reasoning that could lead to incorrect answers in real-world applications where absence of evidence is used to draw conclusions. It provides a benchmark for future improvements and raises awareness of the need for completeness-aware reasoning in AI systems. The dominant failure in CROWN-Synth is asymmetric: models often recognize implicitly complete evidence but treat implicitly partial evidence as query-covering. Prompting redistributes errors between over- and under-closure rather than consistently resolving them, and structured certificate elicitation traces many errors to evidence-coverage mischaracterization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Completeness-sensitive negative reasoning refers to the ability to determine whether a negative answer is justified based on whether the evidence completely covers the query scope. LLMs are often asked whether something is absent from a record or context, but non-observation only licenses a negative answer when evidence is complete; otherwise, the answer should be unknown. This paper introduces CROWN-QA to systematically evaluate this capability.

## [Dynamical Mode Pruning Improves Echo State Network Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04593) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Dynamical Mode Pruning (DMP), a novel reservoir pruning method for Echo State Networks (ESNs) that ranks neurons by their contribution to dominant transition modes derived from a trajectory-averaged Jacobian Gramian. DMP removes low-impact units and retrains only the readout, improving or preserving forecasting accuracy on chaotic and real-world time-series benchmarks. This work addresses a key limitation of existing reservoir pruning methods, which rely on static connectivity or activation statistics and may overlook neurons that shape input-driven state transitions. By introducing a dynamical perspective, DMP offers a more principled criterion for reservoir refinement, potentially leading to more compact and accurate ESNs for time-series prediction, benefiting applications in forecasting and dynamical systems modeling. DMP computes a trajectory-averaged Jacobian Gramian to identify dominant transition modes, then ranks neurons based on their contribution to these modes. The method is evaluated on chaotic and real-world time-series benchmarks, showing that it improves or preserves forecasting accuracy while reducing redundant reservoir components.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Echo State Networks (ESNs) are a type of reservoir computing model that uses a randomly initialized, sparsely connected recurrent neural network as a fixed reservoir, with only the output weights trained. The reservoir is often over-parameterized and dynamically redundant, leading to inefficiency. Pruning methods aim to reduce this redundancy by removing unimportant neurons, but existing approaches often rely on static measures. The Jacobian matrix describes how a system's state changes with respect to inputs, and the Gramian of the Jacobian provides a measure of the system's observability or controllability, which can be used to assess dynamical importance.

## [DELSCOUT: Verifier-Budgeted Code Deletion with Static-First Learned Proposals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04611) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces DELSCOUT, a system that schedules redundant-code deletion under a finite verifier budget, using static-first learned proposals to order candidate deletions. It shows that candidate order, not model confidence, is the key control surface, improving verified-deletion coverage by 9.5% relative on MBPP replications. This work addresses the growing problem of code bloat from AI-assisted 'vibe coding', where additive changes accumulate without removal. By formalizing code deletion as a scheduling problem, it provides a principled approach that can improve maintainability in AI-generated software, impacting developers and AI coding tools. DELSCOUT uses a five-slot budget: three slots for deterministic shortest-first candidates and two for learned candidates, tested across 0.5B, 0.6B, and 8B rankers. Without validation, it evaluates the static prefix first, ensuring non-decreasing coverage and character reduction, but with a 4.8-62.5% increase in verifier calls.

The approach separates model search from execution authority, ensuring safe deletions.

## [DisMix: Order-Aware Mixup for Ordinal Medical Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04652) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DisMix is a novel order-aware mixup framework that disentangles ordinal and non-ordinal features using a dual-codebook VQ-VAE, enabling independent mixing of each subspace. It demonstrates the best aggregate performance across four medical imaging datasets compared to six mixup baselines paired with six ordinal classifiers. This work addresses a critical limitation of standard mixup in ordinal classification tasks, such as medical disease grading, where labels encode severity progression. By preserving ordinal structure during augmentation, DisMix could improve the reliability and accuracy of automated medical imaging diagnostics, especially in data-scarce scenarios. DisMix uses a dual-codebook VQ-VAE to separate ordinal and non-ordinal features; ordinal codes are interpolated to create intermediate ranks, while non-ordinal codes are varied for appearance diversity. It remains effective under data scarcity and clinical grading variability, as shown across four medical imaging datasets.

However, in ordinal classification tasks like medical disease grading, where labels represent a progression of severity, standard mixup can distort the ordinal structure. DisMix addresses this by disentangling ordinal and non-ordinal features using a dual-codebook VQ-VAE, allowing each to be mixed appropriately.

## [CSGen: Hierarchical Multimodal Diffusion for Curvilinear Structure Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04655) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CSGen introduces a hierarchical multimodal diffusion model that generates high-fidelity images with precise curvilinear structures, using a new multi-domain dataset of over 24K samples from 5 domains and 7 annotation types. It employs a hierarchical progressive control strategy and a sparsity-aware loss re-weighting mechanism to improve structure accuracy and visual realism. This work addresses the challenging problem of controllable generation of curvilinear structures, which is important for applications like medical imaging, remote sensing, and autonomous driving. By improving downstream segmentation performance, CSGen could enhance automated analysis in these fields and offer a scalable data-centric paradigm for complex structure generation. The model decouples topology clues from visual context via phased signal injection to mitigate semantic drift, and uses sparsity-aware loss re-weighting to focus on thin structures. Extensive experiments show superior structure accuracy and visual realism, with code and dataset available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Curvilinear structures, such as blood vessels, roads, and cracks, are common in many domains, and their analysis is crucial for tasks like segmentation and diagnosis. Diffusion models are a class of generative models that progressively denoise data to create realistic images, and multimodal diffusion extends this to handle multiple input conditions. Controllable generation of such structures is challenging due to their sparsity and complex topology, which CSGen aims to address.

## [Personalized Federated Sparse Adaptation for Time-Series Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04695) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a personalized federated sparse adaptation framework with a heterogeneous temporal mixture-of-experts (MoE) adapter for time-series foundation models, targeting non-IID building energy data. It compares global FL, local training, and personalized FL variants across 50 buildings and three TSFM backbones. This work addresses a critical challenge in federated learning: adapting large pre-trained time-series models to heterogeneous, private data. By demonstrating that personalization and sparse adaptation can outperform both fully shared and fully local approaches, it offers a promising direction for energy forecasting and other privacy-sensitive applications. The proposed MoE adapter uses a sequence-level router that maps each 168-hour context window to a top-k subset of experts specialized for different temporal patterns. The best sparse-adaptation strategy varies by backbone and metric, and routing behavior reveals client-level expert specialization and near-uniform routing across backbones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Time-series foundation models (TSFMs) are pre-trained on large-scale time-series data and can be fine-tuned for downstream tasks like building energy forecasting. Federated learning enables training on distributed private data without centralizing it, but non-IID data distributions across clients pose challenges. Mixture-of-experts (MoE) adapters introduce multiple specialized sub-networks that can be selectively activated, offering a flexible way to adapt models to diverse data patterns.

## [Functional ANOVA Reveals Design Choice Impacts in Remote Sensing MLC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04702) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper applies functional ANOVA (fANOVA) to systematically quantify the contributions of individual design choices and their interactions to performance variability in multi-label classification (MLC) of remote sensing images, analyzing 48 and 20 deep learning models across seven datasets. It constructs dataset meta-representations that capture design-choice sensitivity profiles, revealing that datasets group based on how they respond to design decisions. This work moves beyond simple rankings to provide systematic insights into how dataset properties interact with model design choices, which can guide practitioners in selecting appropriate architectures and training strategies for specific remote sensing datasets. The findings that fine-tuning and architecture dominate for large-scale datasets, while initialization is decisive in data-limited regimes, offer actionable guidance for model development. The empirical analyses span design choices such as network architecture, fine-tuning strategy, learning strategy, and initialization, applied across seven MLC RSI datasets. Hierarchical clustering of the meta-representations shows that dataset groupings are strongly linked to intrinsic properties like scale, spatial resolution, and label space complexity, with intermediate regimes governed by architecture-learning strategy interactions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Functional ANOVA (fANOVA) is a statistical framework that decomposes the variance of a function into contributions from individual input variables and their interactions, commonly used in interpretable machine learning to quantify feature importance. Multi-label classification in remote sensing involves assigning multiple land-cover or scene labels to an image, and benchmarking studies often produce rankings that do not generalize across datasets. This paper leverages fANOVA to analyze design choices in deep learning models for this task.

This paper proposes a 6G Integrated Sensing and Communication (ISAC) framework that uses Channel State Information (CSI) and a 3D CNN-BiLSTM model to detect railway intrusions and predict collisions. This paper proposes an integrated architecture that combines adaptive experience replay, online uncertainty estimation, and control barrier functions for safe actor-critic control. It demonstrates improved performance in a 2D robot navigation task with corrupted sensor data, achieving zero contacts in extreme stress tests. This work addresses a known gap in safe reinforcement learning by integrating modules that are typically treated separately, potentially leading to more robust and safer learning in real-world applications. The results suggest that coupling estimation, safety filtering, and replay can improve performance under corrupted sensor data, which is relevant for robotics and autonomous systems. The integrated configuration recorded no contacts and reached the goal in all five evaluation seeds in the extreme stress test, with mean cost 7.63±0.44 and obstacle-belief RMSE 3.52±0.55 cm. ## [InsightEmb: Progress-Oriented Embeddings for Agentic Insight Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04761) ⭐️ 7.0/10

InsightEmb introduces a contrastive embedding framework that learns progress-oriented retrieval geometry, trained solely on mathematical reasoning data, and demonstrates improved performance on dynamic agent tasks and a static skill-retrieval benchmark without environment-specific training. This work addresses a critical gap in agentic systems by shifting retrieval from semantic similarity to progress-oriented matching, which can enhance decision-making in self-improving agents. It suggests that transferable retrieval geometry can be learned from public reasoning data, reducing the need for expensive environment-specific supervision. InsightEmb jointly aligns concrete situations with abstract heuristic rules and clusters reasoning trajectories with similar progress structures. The framework is evaluated on dynamic agent tasks and a static skill-retrieval benchmark, outperforming existing reasoning embedding models without any environment-specific training.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Self-improving agents accumulate reusable insights from past trajectories, and retrieving the right insight at each decision step is crucial for progress. Traditional retrieval methods focus on semantic similarity, but they may overlook whether an insight resolves the agent's current decision bottleneck. Contrastive learning is a technique that trains embeddings by pulling similar pairs together and pushing dissimilar pairs apart, which can be adapted to learn progress-oriented geometry.

## [Explicit Language Memory Enhances Long-Horizon VLA Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04765) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a hierarchical Vision-Language-Action (VLA) architecture with an explicit language-memory module that converts temporal observations into coherent textual memory sequences, decoupling high-level semantic reasoning from low-level continuous control. The method is evaluated in simulation and sim-to-real experiments, showing improved success rates and robustness on long-horizon tasks. Long-horizon robotic control remains a major challenge for VLA models due to sparse demonstrations, non-Markovian dynamics, and error accumulation. This work introduces a novel mechanism to maintain temporal consistency and interpretability, potentially advancing the deployment of VLA models in complex real-world tasks. The architecture splits into a high-level VLM trained via visual question answering and a low-level VLA for precise control, with the high-level module recursively updating language memory and subtask instructions. The approach addresses four identified issues: sparse demonstrations, non-Markovian tasks, limited error correction, and weakened semantic representations from end-to-end fine-tuning.

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models unify visual perception, language understanding, and robotic control, but they struggle with long-horizon tasks that require temporal consistency and error recovery. Non-Markovian tasks, where rewards depend on history rather than just the current state, are common in real-world scenarios. Hierarchical planning and explicit memory mechanisms are emerging strategies to address these challenges.

## [Guideline-as-Oracle: Zero-Annotation Training for Ophthalmic Triage Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04772) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Guideline-as-Oracle (GAO), a zero-annotation training method that compiles American Academy of Ophthalmology guidance into a 70-row rule table to supervise 3,000 training dialogues for an ophthalmic telephone triage agent. Fine-tuning a 9B backbone yields GAO-Triage, improving agreement with a reference set from 61.7% to 74.1% (exact McNemar p=0.0046) and emergent-case recall from 9.5% to 69.0%. This work addresses the high cost and privacy constraints of expert annotation in medical dialogue systems, offering a scalable alternative that improves triage accuracy without requiring frontier models at inference. It could accelerate the deployment of AI triage agents in clinical settings, potentially improving patient outcomes and reducing clinician workload. The paper catalogs eight construction strategies for converting rules into dialogues, including cited-row tier assignment, one-fact boundary pairs, metadata-only repair, and label repair, and characterizes their evidential status. Permuting label-dialogue assignments collapses the model to a constant-routine predictor, indicating the signal lies in guideline-derived assignment rather than dialogue surface form; label repair coincides with the disappearance of a late-training safety degradation.

The American Academy of Ophthalmology provides clinical guidance that can be compiled into operational rules, serving as a source of supervision for training such agents.

## [Scoping Review Finds AI Audits Neglect System Integration Risks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04921) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A scoping review of 4,259 documents identified 58 AI audits that treat system integration as a core evaluation tenet, revealing fragmented practices and significant gaps in addressing integration-specific risks. The review categorizes integration across three sites—inter-component, system-environment, and multi-system—and highlights the need for audits to assess qualities like compatibility, completeness, and oversight. As AI systems are increasingly deployed in complex environments, model-centric audits are insufficient to capture risks arising from interactions among components and deployment contexts. This review calls on the AI community to prioritize system integration as a core strategy for addressing AI risk, which could influence future audit standards and practices. The review used reflexive thematic analysis to analyze the 58 audits, identifying elements, actors, enablers, and constraints. It found that few existing measures target integration-specific risks, and access to information and resources significantly influences audit design, with large gaps remaining in meeting traditional audit expectations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: A scoping review is a structured synthesis that maps the extent, range, and nature of evidence on a topic, often used to identify gaps in literature. Reflexive thematic analysis is a qualitative method for identifying and interpreting patterns of meaning within data, developed by Braun and Clarke. System integration audits have long been central in safety-critical domains like aerospace, but their role in AI auditing is underexplored.

## [SVI-DAG: Structured Variational Inference for Bayesian Causal Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04930) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SVI-DAG introduces a structured variational inference method that uses normalizing flows to model dependencies between edges in Bayesian causal discovery, enabling expressive and multimodal posterior learning over DAGs. It also employs Stein variational gradient descent to mitigate mode-seeking behavior and improve mode coverage. This work addresses key limitations of existing Bayesian DAG learning methods, which often fail to encode edge dependencies and lack principled ways to incorporate domain knowledge. By improving posterior approximation and uncertainty quantification, SVI-DAG could enhance causal inference in fields like epidemiology, economics, and machine learning. SVI-DAG uses normalizing flows to model edge dependencies and Stein variational gradient descent with a kernel in acyclicity space to update node potentials. It was evaluated against five state-of-the-art Bayesian DAG learning methods, showing superior uncertainty quantification while remaining competitive in structural accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Bayesian causal discovery aims to infer the posterior distribution over causal graphs (DAGs) that explain observed data, allowing reasoning about epistemic uncertainty. However, the space of possible DAGs is vast, making exact posterior inference intractable. Variational inference approximates this posterior, but traditional mean-field methods assume independence among variables, which is often unrealistic. Structured variational inference relaxes this assumption by modeling dependencies, and normalizing flows provide a flexible way to construct such expressive approximations.

## [ORACLE: RL-Based Multi-Objective Analog Circuit Optimizer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04999) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ORACLE is an open-source reinforcement learning framework for multi-objective analog circuit design that replaces scalar reward optimization with vector-valued learning and preference-aware conditioning, enabling a single trained model to generate designs across diverse trade-off settings without retraining. It also incorporates LLM-guided action selection to filter suboptimal actions, achieving significant runtime reductions and high specification satisfaction. This work addresses a key limitation in existing RL-based analog circuit design methods that often reduce multi-objective problems to a single scalar reward, failing to capture true Pareto trade-offs. By enabling preference-aware multi-objective optimization without retraining, ORACLE could significantly improve design efficiency and flexibility, benefiting hardware designers and advancing AI-driven electronic design automation. ORACLE uses a preference vector to specify relative weights of multiple objectives, and proposes two preference-guidance strategies: normalized-weight guidance and cosine-aligned guidance. On multiple circuit topologies with 2,000 test cases, it reduces runtime by 20.4x to 104.4x compared to state-of-the-art approaches, meets 99.9% of target specifications, and achieves 5.1x to 318.6x better figure of merit.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) is a machine learning paradigm where agents learn to make decisions by interacting with an environment to maximize cumulative rewards. In analog circuit design, RL-based automation aims to reduce manual effort, but many existing methods focus on single-objective optimization or simplify multi-objective problems into a single scalar reward, which limits the ability to capture Pareto trade-offs. Multi-objective optimization involves balancing competing objectives, and preference-aware conditioning allows models to adapt to different trade-off preferences without retraining.

OneDayAgent introduces a long-horizon harness that decomposes tasks into bounded subtasks, maintains execution memory under context pressure, and verifies/repairs final deliverables. It achieves a state-of-the-art score of 0.821 on the AgentIF-OneDay benchmark using the GLM-5.2 backend. This work addresses multiple failure modes of long-horizon agents jointly, which is a significant step toward more reliable autonomous agents for everyday tasks. The harness's ability to generalize across five backends from three model families without tuning suggests broad applicability and potential industry impact. The harness is evaluated on AgentIF-OneDay, a benchmark with 104 tasks covering work, study, and life scenarios. The same harness runs across five backend LLMs from three model families, indicating generalization without tuning, even though different models induce distinct execution styles.

ArtAnno introduces a closed-loop, multi-agent system for annotating implicit semantics in artworks, driven by a Bidirectional Human-AI Augmentation (BiHAA) framework. It includes a Proactive Agentic Support Module and an Interaction-Driven Evolution Module, evaluated through user and case studies. This work addresses a critical gap in computational art research by enabling efficient annotation of culturally grounded implicit semantics, which is often overlooked by current AI tools. It demonstrates a practical bidirectional collaboration model that could influence digital humanities and HCI, reducing the burden on annotators with limited domain expertise. The system was informed by a formative study with 20 artwork annotators from diverse backgrounds. The multi-agent architecture includes a Proactive Agentic Support Module for semantic mining and label suggestion, and an Interaction-Driven Evolution Module that distills annotation trajectories into reusable experience, improving efficiency and knowledge accumulation.

## [RepairFormer: Transformer-Based Automated Repair of Structured Inputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05060) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RepairFormer is a new transformer-based framework that automatically repairs corrupted structured input files (e.g., JSON, DOT, OBJ) by formulating repair as a supervised sequence generation task. It introduces format tags, oracle validation, and boundary-localized repair to preserve original content while generating valid outputs. This work addresses a practical problem in software testing and automation, where malformed configuration or data files can interrupt workflows. By improving repair accuracy and content preservation, RepairFormer could reduce manual intervention and enable more robust automated pipelines. In evaluation, RepairFormer achieves 88% repair and 94% recovery rates, and on their benchmark it repairs 97.57% and recovers 94.29% with 5x faster runtime compared to state of the art. The boundary-localized workflow focuses generation on the detected fault region, reducing input size and supporting repair of longer files.

## [Provable Limits and Certified Deferral for Verbalized Uncertainty in Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05064) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper provides theoretical limits and empirical evidence for using verbalized confidence in small language models (0.5B-14B parameters) for risk-controlled deferral, evaluating 11 models on ARC-Challenge and TruthfulQA with 25,168 predictions. It also identifies and repairs an answer-ordering artifact in TruthfulQA's multiple-choice format. This work addresses a critical deployment question for small models in private, offline, and cost-sensitive settings: when to defer to humans. The theoretical limits and certified deferral framework provide practical guidance for safe deployment, potentially impacting industries relying on small models for decision support. The paper presents three theoretical results: strictly monotone calibration preserves risk-coverage frontier and error-detection AUROC; temperature scaling cannot calibrate models whose confidence stays above 0.5 while accuracy falls below it; and a Clopper-Pearson procedure converts a 200-question calibration set into a finite-sample risk certificate under i.i.d. deployment. Empirically, eight of 22 model-task pairs hit the temperature-scaling infeasibility floor within one percentage point, and Platt scaling reduces ECE to as low as 0.02, yet certified autonomy at 20% risk budget is granted to only three model-task pairs and none at 10%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Verbalized uncertainty refers to a model expressing its confidence in natural language, such as 'I am 90% sure'. Calibration ensures that predicted confidence matches actual accuracy. Risk-controlled deferral is a framework where a model decides when to defer to a human based on a risk budget. The Clopper-Pearson interval is an exact binomial confidence interval method used for finite-sample guarantees.

## [VQ-VAD: Discrete Motion Codebook for Human-Centric Video Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05069) ⭐️ 7.0/10

VQ-VAD introduces a vector-quantized motion representation learning framework for human-centric video anomaly detection, adapting VQ-GAN to keypoint sequences and constructing a discrete motion codebook of normal behavior. It achieves 81.83% in-domain accuracy on HR-SHT and 76.69% cross-domain accuracy from CMU Panoptic without retraining. This work addresses a key limitation of existing pose-based VAD methods that rely on continuous latent spaces, which struggle to learn compact motion patterns. By introducing discrete representations, VQ-VAD could improve robustness and generalization in surveillance applications, offering a more privacy-preserving and efficient alternative to pixel-based approaches. VQ-VAD is trained exclusively on normal motion sequences and detects anomalies via high reconstruction errors when an observed sequence cannot be mapped to the learned codebook. It is evaluated across in-domain, cross-domain, and cross-dataset settings on four benchmarks, with the code available on GitHub.

**Background**: Video anomaly detection (VAD) aims to identify unusual events in surveillance footage, but it is challenging due to the scarcity of anomalies and visual variability. Pose-based VAD extracts human keypoints to focus on motion dynamics, reducing visual noise and addressing privacy concerns. Vector quantization, as used in VQ-GAN, discretizes continuous representations into a codebook, enabling compact and discrete motion modeling.

## [MultiPathFormer: A Foundation Model for Multipath Wireless Propagation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05076) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MultiPathFormer is introduced as the first wireless foundation model that operates at the multipath level, representing each transmitter-receiver link as an ordered sequence of continuous-valued path tokens and pretraining with next-path prediction. It incorporates an Environmental RAG mechanism and a first-path codebook, improving path statistics estimation like delay and power by up to 59%. This work shifts the pretraining paradigm for wireless foundation models from channel tensors to physical multipath propagation, enabling more physically meaningful representations. It demonstrates strong performance across downstream tasks like localization, beam prediction, and channel estimation, potentially advancing AI-driven wireless communication systems. MultiPathFormer is pretrained on 27 environments and transfers to unseen users; after scenario-specific fine-tuning, it outperforms training from scratch. It achieves 5.57 m mean localization error, 0.914 top-3 beam accuracy, 0.994 line-of-sight classification accuracy, and 0.561 channel estimation NMSE, outperforming SOTA channel-based foundation models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Wireless foundation models are trained on large datasets to support tasks like channel estimation, beam prediction, and localization. Traditional models pretrain on channel tensors using masked reconstruction over subcarriers, antennas, or time, but ignore physical propagation characteristics. Multipath propagation refers to the phenomenon where signals travel via multiple paths, each with distinct delay, power, and angles, which MultiPathFormer models as tokens. The Environmental RAG mechanism retrieves relevant environment knowledge to enhance path statistics estimation, and the first-path codebook helps capture the direct path's characteristics.

## [Shared Classical Randomness Boosts Shallow Quantum Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05110) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proves that augmenting shallow unitary Born models with shared classical randomness, implemented via spatially separated local Pauli operations controlled by a single random bit, yields a strictly larger class of output distributions at fixed shallow depth for arbitrarily large systems. This resolves an open question about whether such randomness provides a scalable representational separation. This result establishes a fundamental advantage of channel models over unitary models in quantum generative modeling, with implications for near-term quantum hardware that is limited in depth and connectivity. It suggests that incorporating classical randomness can enhance the expressive power of quantum generative models without requiring deeper circuits, which is valuable for practical quantum machine learning. For one-dimensional nearest-neighbor architectures, reproducing the distributions generated by the channel model with a purely unitary model can require depth Ω(N) in the worst case. The paper also shows that measurement-based quantum computation (MBQC) provides a natural implementation of the required shared classical randomness, and numerical experiments on MBQC-based generative models support the analytical results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum generative models, such as Born machines, use quantum circuits to sample from probability distributions. Near-term hardware restricts circuit depth and connectivity, limiting the distributions accessible to shallow unitary Born models. Introducing stochasticity, e.g., via quantum channels, can improve performance, but whether it provides a provable separation at fixed depth for large systems was open. Shared classical randomness is a weaker resource than entanglement, making this separation notable.

## [Chained Recursive Language Models for Multi-Iteration Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05124) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Chained Recursive Language Models (Chained RLM), an inference-time architecture that repeatedly calls the same LLM with compact summaries and blackboards to improve multi-iteration reasoning over long contexts. This architecture addresses a known limitation of LLMs in long-context reasoning by decomposing complex tasks into staged computations, potentially improving accuracy in tasks requiring extraction, counting, ordering, or multi-hop reasoning. It offers a practical approach that could be integrated into existing systems without retraining. Each recursive call receives the original problem and context, but not the full conversational history; instead, it gets a compact plain-text summary, a blackboard, and durable task-specific artifacts. The handoff mechanism allows intermediate artifacts to be inspected, corrected, and extended by later fresh inferences.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Long context reasoning in LLMs is often constrained by a single inference trajectory having to simultaneously explore context, store intermediate state, verify evidence, and produce the final answer. The blackboard pattern, a classical AI architecture, decomposes complex problems into stages where a shared workspace (blackboard) is updated by specialized agents. Chained RLM applies this idea to LLMs, using the same model as a sequence of fresh reasoning roots.

## [SSTQ: Privacy-Preserving Vector Quantization via Subsampled Stochastic TurboQuant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05127) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Subsampled Stochastic TurboQuant (SSTQ), a framework that combines overcomplete equal-norm tight frames, coordinate subsampling, and privacy-aware one-dimensional quantization for differentially private distributed optimization. SSTQ achieves optimal mean squared error (MSE) scaling while using only ceil(log2 N) + b bits per client, and it reduces the codebook-dependent MSE scaling from O(4^b) to O(2^b). SSTQ addresses the critical trade-off between local differential privacy and communication cost in distributed optimization, offering a clear improvement over existing methods like vqSGD. This is significant for federated learning and other distributed ML systems where communication efficiency and privacy are paramount. SSTQ includes two variants: a Flat Randomized Response version and a Metric-Aware Laplace version, with the latter better suited for higher codebook bit-width regimes. The framework is empirically evaluated on CIFAR-10 and Fashion-MNIST federated learning tasks, demonstrating favorable utility and communication efficiency compared to established baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Vector quantization methods like vqSGD use high-dimensional geometric constructions to compress gradients but suffer from dimension-dependent variance. Tight frames provide overcomplete representations that can improve robustness and sparsity, while subsampling reduces computational complexity. Differential privacy is achieved by adding noise, but this often increases communication cost; SSTQ aims to optimize this trade-off.

## [OPD-V: Visual On-Policy Self-Distillation with Modality Balance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05131) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces OPD-V, a visual on-policy self-distillation method that uses modality balance as privileged information to improve visual reasoning in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). It constructs Positive and Negative Teachers to define a Modality-Balance Trust Region for selecting on-policy tokens. This work addresses the overlooked issue of modality imbalance in OPSD, where text dominance limits the use of privileged information. By leveraging modality balance as a new form of privileged information, OPD-V can improve reasoning performance across multiple benchmarks and backbones, potentially advancing MLLM post-training efficiency. OPD-V uses a Positive Teacher with Zoom-In Image and a Negative Teacher with Mask Image to create Modality-Balance Logits Margins, defining a trust region for token selection. Experiments across 6 benchmarks, 4 MLLM backbones, and 5 post-training methods show consistent improvements in reasoning while reducing training cost.

This paper presents an end-to-end adaptation of NVIDIA's Nemotron retrieval stack for Modern Greek, including corpus mining, synthetic supervision, model training, reranker adaptation, and reader fine-tuning. They introduce HERA, the first large-scale Greek benchmark for retrieval-augmented generation, and show that fine-tuning a Nemotron 1B embedder on 65,773 Greek pairs improves nDCG@10 from 0.362 to 0.835. Modern Greek is absent from major multilingual retrieval benchmarks and NVIDIA's Nemotron models, yet it is crucial for RAG in legal, energy, financial, and medical applications. This work fills that gap, demonstrating significant retrieval and generation improvements, and provides a benchmark and models to support future Greek-language RAG research. A parameter-free BM25 baseline outperforms several off-the-shelf multilingual dense retrieval models on specialist Greek corpora, though the advantage over BM25 remains domain-dependent after fine-tuning. They also LoRA-tune a Nemotron 30B-A3B mixture-of-experts reader, increasing judged answer correctness from 29.4% to 66.9% while improving faithfulness and citation quality.

## [GRALS: GCN-Guided Local Search for Minimum Vertex Cover](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06396) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GRALS is a new local search framework for the minimum vertex cover problem that integrates vertex probability priors learned from a graph convolutional network with an expansion revelation elimination (ERE) operator. On 346 benchmark instances, it achieves the best-known solution for 335 instances, outperforming the strongest baseline which solved 302 instances. This work demonstrates a promising integration of graph neural networks with local search heuristics for a fundamental NP-hard problem, potentially improving solution quality on large-scale graphs. It could influence future heuristic design in combinatorial optimization and attract interest from both AI and operations research communities. The ERE operator dynamically identifies and eliminates redundant vertices to enhance solution quality, while the learned priors guide the search toward promising regions. The advantage of GRALS is maintained across different time limits and is particularly evident on large-scale graphs with millions of vertices.

**Background**: The minimum vertex cover (MVC) problem asks for the smallest set of vertices that covers all edges in an undirected graph, and it is a fundamental NP-hard combinatorial optimization problem with applications in network analysis and system design. Local search heuristics are among the most effective approaches for large-scale instances, often using a break-and-repair framework where the repair process mainly restores feasibility but offers limited structural improvement. Graph convolutional networks (GCNs) are a type of graph neural network that can learn node-level features, which can be used to provide probability priors for guiding search algorithms.

This paper introduces xPeer, a peer-review simulation engine benchmark with two components: an operational component analyzing 352 simulation records and a human-reference component releasing 1,108 F1000Research manuscript records with linked human reports. It applies a prespecified two-human/two-xPeer comparison to evaluate zero-shot reasoning in scholarly peer review. This benchmark provides a transparent, reproducible methodology for evaluating AI-generated peer reviews, addressing the scalability and auditability challenges in scholarly publishing. It could influence how AI tools are integrated into the peer-review process and set a standard for future comparisons. Among 802 records with exactly two human reports, only 271 had two usable xPeer reviewer fields, yielding a complete-pair availability rate of 33.8%. xPeer reports were longer (median 1,889 words vs. 763 for humans) and had more concerns (median 41 vs. 13), but human reports showed higher explicit-reasoning language and lower redundancy.

## [AttCal: Self-Supervised Attention Calibration Boosts Transformer Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.20661) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces AttCal, a self-supervised, annotation-free framework that calibrates attention in transformers using task-space sensitivity feedback. It treats attention distributions as stochastic policies to guide token deletion perturbations and optimizes attention parameters via policy gradient, achieving up to a 15.8% accuracy lift for Llama3-8B on text classification benchmarks. This work addresses the attention dilution and attention sink effects that cause transformers to over-focus on irrelevant tokens, a common issue in pre-trained language models. By providing a scalable, annotation-free calibration method, AttCal could improve the performance and interpretability of large language models across various NLP tasks without requiring human-labeled data. AttCal freezes the PLM backbone and updates only attention projection layers, ensuring training efficiency. It outperforms state-of-the-art supervised baselines in both classification accuracy and interpretability across five datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Transformer-based pre-trained language models (PLMs) often suffer from attention dilution, where attention is spread too thinly across tokens, and attention sink, where excessive focus is placed on certain tokens like special tokens. These issues degrade performance on tasks like text classification. Existing attention supervision methods require costly token-level human annotations or static heuristics, which are not scalable or context-dependent. AttCal addresses this by using a self-supervised approach that infers token importance from semantic shifts caused by token deletion.

## [MemFly: On-the-Fly Memory Optimization via Information Bottleneck](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07885) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MemFly is a new framework that applies information bottleneck principles to optimize long-term memory in LLM agents, using a gradient-free optimizer to balance compression and relevance. It introduces a hybrid retrieval mechanism combining semantic, symbolic, and topological pathways with iterative refinement for multi-hop queries. This addresses a critical trade-off in LLM memory systems between efficient compression and precise retrieval, which is essential for agents handling complex, long-horizon tasks. By outperforming state-of-the-art baselines in coherence, fidelity, and accuracy, MemFly could advance the deployment of more capable and reliable LLM agents in real-world applications. The framework minimizes compression entropy while maximizing relevance entropy via a gradient-free optimizer, constructing a stratified memory structure. The hybrid retrieval mechanism integrates semantic, symbolic, and topological pathways, with iterative refinement to handle complex multi-hop queries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Long-term memory in LLM agents stores historical interactions to support complex tasks, but existing frameworks struggle to compress redundant information while maintaining precise retrieval. The information bottleneck principle is an information-theoretic method for extracting task-relevant features by learning maximally compressed representations that preserve target information. Gradient-free optimizers, such as zeroth-order methods, are used to reduce memory overhead during LLM optimization. Hybrid retrieval combines multiple retrieval pathways (e.g., semantic, symbolic, topological) to improve accuracy and handle diverse query types.

## [Measuring LLM Persuadability in Legal Decision-Making](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.26233) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel metric to measure the persuadability of large language models (LLMs) in legal decision-making, specifically in a trilateral setting where two advocate models compete to persuade a judge model. The authors evaluate frontier open- and closed-weight LLMs, examining how argument quality, rhetorical form, and positional bias affect model decisions. As LLMs are increasingly proposed as legal decision assistants or even first-instance decision-makers, understanding their persuadability is critical to ensure fair and merit-based judgments. This research provides a framework to evaluate and potentially mitigate undue influence from advocate skills, which is essential for the safe adoption of AI in high-stakes legal and administrative settings. The study uses parallel bilateral trials to compare trilateral and bilateral settings, revealing how the trilateral setting changes demands on judge models and their apparent persuadability. It also analyzes the relative contribution of legal content versus rhetorical form, and whether model persuasion aligns with human expert judgments of argument quality.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are being explored for legal reasoning tasks, but their susceptibility to persuasive arguments raises concerns about fairness. The trilateral setting mimics real courtrooms where opposing advocates present arguments to a judge, making it a realistic testbed for evaluating LLM decision-making. This paper contributes to the growing field of legal alignment and AI safety by proposing a systematic method to measure persuadability.

This paper introduces a novel online goal recognition method that combines path signatures and dynamic time warping (DTW), demonstrating consistent improvements over state-of-the-art approaches in both predictive accuracy and online planning efficiency. This work addresses key challenges in online goal recognition for continuous domains, offering a more efficient and accurate solution that could benefit AI planning and robotics applications. By leveraging established encoding techniques, it opens new avenues for improving real-time decision-making systems. Experiments show it outperforms the state of the art in predictive accuracy and online planning efficiency, while remaining competitive in offline settings.

## [Theory-Level Autoformalization: From Isolated Statements to Unified Formal Knowledge Bases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13292) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This position paper advocates for a paradigm shift in autoformalization from formalizing isolated statements to formalizing entire theories, including all their inter-dependencies, as structured libraries. It outlines the significance of this shift, addresses alternative views, identifies open challenges, and proposes three promising research directions. This shift is crucial for practical formal verification, as real-world formalization efforts require a web of axioms, definitions, and lemmas before target theorems can be stated. By focusing on theory-level formalization, the field can move towards building unified formal knowledge bases, which would significantly enhance the applicability of AI for mathematics and theorem proving. The paper provides a survey of autoformalization, available at https://github.com/marcusm117/Awesome-Autoformalization. It also addresses alternative views and identifies open challenges, such as handling the complexity of inter-dependencies and ensuring consistency across a formal library.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Autoformalization is the process of automatically translating informal natural language mathematics into formal, machine-verifiable languages, such as those used by proof assistants. Most existing work focuses on individual statements, but real formalization efforts are inherently theory-level, requiring a comprehensive web of axioms, definitions, and lemmas. Formal verification, which relies on such formalizations, aims to prove the correctness of systems using mathematical methods. This paper argues that to make autoformalization practical, it must be extended to the theory level.

## [Monotonic Framework for Evaluating Wildfire Risk Signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21597) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel monotonic evaluation framework for wildfire risk systems, measuring whether increases in predicted risk scores correspond to increases in observed operational load. It compares three approaches on the French Alpes-Maritimes department: the expert-based DFE index, GRU-based models, and FARS, a hybrid multi-agent system. This work challenges the reliance on standard ML metrics like F1-score for evaluating risk systems, proposing a paradigm shift toward operational coherence. It could influence how environmental monitoring and risk management systems are designed and assessed, benefiting practitioners in applied ML and disaster response. The DFE index, despite poor classification metrics, exhibited the most balanced monotonic behavior across the full risk scale, while GRU models showed strong local monotonicity but poorly distributed risk levels. FARS inherited structural limitations from upstream signals rather than correcting them. The code for the monotonic framework is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Wildfire risk systems typically output a continuous risk score, but standard ML metrics like F1-score or IoU evaluate event prediction accuracy, not the operational coherence of the signal. The proposed monotonic framework addresses this gap by checking if higher risk scores consistently correspond to higher operational load. The study compares expert-based indices, deep learning models, and hybrid AI systems to highlight the importance of operational evaluation.

## [MemSIF: Dual-Track Fact Memory for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01742) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MemSIF introduces a structured interaction-to-fact memory framework that mitigates Temporal-Structural Misalignment (TSM) and Delayed Utility Manifestation (DUM) in long-term LLM agent memory. It achieves state-of-the-art results on LoCoMo and LongMemEval-S benchmarks, outperforming the strongest baseline by 2.29%-8.79% and 2.87%-6.15% respectively. This work addresses fundamental limitations in LLM agent memory systems, which are critical for long-horizon tasks. By improving memory alignment and utility, MemSIF could enhance the reliability and performance of LLM agents in real-world applications, such as personal assistants and autonomous systems. MemSIF comprises Structured Interaction Memory, which organizes interactions into Topical Segments and Event Trajectories, and Dual-Track Fact Memory, which uses CoreFact for stable schema-guided facts and ActiveFact for on-demand facts promoted by recurring demand. The framework was evaluated across five backbone LLMs, demonstrating consistent improvements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Long-term memory in LLM agents is essential for maintaining context over extended interactions, but existing systems often suffer from misalignments between temporal proximity and topical relatedness (TSM) and between write-time salience and future query utility (DUM). MemSIF addresses these by jointly organizing interactions and constructing facts within a unified framework, coupling contiguous topical segments with non-contiguous event trajectories.
