## [HALT: Verification-Aware Stopping for Efficient RAG Search Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02009) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces HALT, a lightweight verification-aware stopping policy for retrieval-augmented search agents that stops retrieval only when cumulative evidence covers all expected hop claims, reducing redundant search while preserving answer accuracy across three multi-hop QA benchmarks. This matters because it addresses the practical stopping problem in retrieval-augmented agents, offering a training-free and host-agent-agnostic method to improve efficiency and reduce latency, which could be widely adopted in RAG systems and multi-hop QA applications. HALT frames stopping as evidence coverage rather than generator confidence, and separates a deployable setting (generated hop claims) from a diagnostic upper bound (gold supporting-fact annotations). Experiments show that generated claims yield smaller but exact-match-preserving savings, while gold claims show larger savings, and ablations confirm the behavior is driven by claim-evidence alignment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) combines a language model with an external retrieval system to ground answers in retrieved evidence. Multi-hop QA agents iteratively issue search queries and accumulate evidence, but often continue retrieving after sufficient evidence has been gathered, adding cost and latency. HALT addresses this by using evidence coverage as a stopping signal, without modifying the underlying search agent.

This work addresses the significant challenge of handling data deletions in incremental Datalog materialization, which is crucial for knowledge representation and reasoning systems operating in dynamic cloud environments. The findings provide a roadmap for enhancing Datalog-driven computations, potentially improving performance and scalability of such systems. The paper highlights that differential dataflow ensures consistent performance for both data additions and deletions, addressing the cascading effects of deletions. The comparison includes a streamlined relational engine and two implementations of the same algorithm, one using differential dataflow and one not, providing insights into the trade-offs.

## [G-Boost: Adaptive Edge-Cloud Framework Boosts Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10367) ⭐️ 7.0/10

G-Boost is a novel adaptive edge-cloud framework that enhances edge-deployed small language models (SLMs) by selectively fusing their logits with cloud LLMs, guided by a process reward model and Monte Carlo tree search. Evaluated on GSM8K and MATH-500, it achieves up to 8.6 and 10.7 percentage point improvements over MCTS and Proxy-Tuning baselines, respectively. This framework addresses the critical challenge of deploying capable AI on resource-constrained edge devices without compromising privacy or requiring parameter updates. It offers a practical solution for improving SLM performance in real-world applications, potentially reducing reliance on cloud-only inference and enabling more efficient edge-cloud collaboration. The framework operates by formulating reasoning as a tree search, where each step chooses between SLM-only inference and SLM-LLM logit fusion, transferring domain knowledge without exposing private data. The edge runs the SLM and search controller, while the cloud hosts the LLM and reward model, exchanging only the current context. Experiments used Qwen2.5 and LLaMA2 models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Edge devices often host small language models (SLMs) due to resource constraints, but these models may underperform compared to larger cloud LLMs. Logit fusion is a technique that combines the output probabilities of multiple models to improve performance. Monte Carlo tree search (MCTS) is a search algorithm used to explore decision spaces, often applied in AI for planning and reasoning. G-Boost combines these concepts to enable dynamic, step-level collaboration between edge SLMs and cloud LLMs.

## [Curiosity-Diffuser: Guiding Diffusion Models for Reliable Robot Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14833) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Curiosity-Diffuser, a method that uses Random Network Distillation (RND)-based curiosity to guide conditional diffusion models, reducing overgeneralization and improving the reliability of imitation policies in robotics. It also proposes a new metric for evaluating policy reliability based on similarity to training data. This addresses a critical safety issue in robotic imitation learning, where neural network policies can hallucinate and produce unsafe behaviors. By improving reliability, it enhances the applicability of diffusion-based policies in real-world scenarios, potentially accelerating safe deployment of learned robot policies. The method uses classifier guidance to minimize curiosity during diffusion sampling, aligning generated trajectories with training data. The proposed reliability metric is computationally efficient, and experiments in simulation and real-world settings show significant improvements in task performance and behavioral similarity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are generative models that can produce trajectories for robotic policies, but they may overgeneralize and generate unreliable behaviors. Random Network Distillation (RND) is a curiosity mechanism that measures state novelty by predicting the output of a fixed random network, and classifier guidance is a technique to steer diffusion generation toward desired properties.

This paper introduces EPOPR, a predict-then-optimize framework that combines Equity-Conformalized Quantile Regression and Spatial-Temporal Attentional RL to address uncertainty in repair durations and biases in request submission for post-disaster power restoration. Experiments show it reduces average outage duration by 3.60% and decreases inequity by 14.19% compared to state-of-the-art baselines. This work addresses a critical societal issue by ensuring equitable power restoration after disasters, which is often overlooked in favor of efficiency. It provides a data-driven approach to correct disparities in request submission, potentially improving resilience for disadvantaged communities. The framework uses Equity-Conformalized Quantile Regression to predict repair durations under heteroscedasticity, and a Spatial-Temporal Attentional RL that adapts to varying uncertainty levels across regions. The paper highlights that disadvantaged communities submit fewer restoration requests, which biases traditional volume-based restoration strategies.

Heteroscedasticity refers to non-constant variance in data, which complicates prediction. This paper integrates equity considerations into the predict-then-optimize paradigm.

This paper introduces a novel framework for detecting data contamination in tabular datasets by generating controlled multiple-choice queries and comparing LLM performance against non-neural baselines. The framework includes a statistical testing procedure to formally identify significant deviations indicative of contamination. Data contamination can inflate LLM performance on downstream tasks, undermining the reliability of evaluation practices. This framework addresses an underexplored area—tabular data—and provides a method to quantify contamination, which is crucial for the machine learning community to trust benchmark results. The framework was applied to eight widely used tabular datasets, revealing clear evidence of contamination in four cases. The approach uses systematic transformations of underlying data to selectively disrupt dataset information while preserving partial knowledge, enabling isolation of contamination effects.

This framework provides a more nuanced approach by generating controlled queries and using statistical testing to detect contamination.

## [Reinforcement Learning Explains Consumption-Savings Puzzles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.20748) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a model where agents use Q-learning with neural network approximation to make consumption-savings decisions under income uncertainty, departing from standard rational expectations. It replicates two empirical patterns: higher marginal propensities to consume for low-asset unemployed households (0.50 vs 0.34) and consumption scarring from past unemployment. This work bridges AI and economics by showing that reinforcement learning can unify explanations for puzzling household behaviors, offering a novel alternative to belief-updating or heterogeneity-based theories. It could influence how economists model adaptive behavior and inform policy design for stimulus transfers. The model generates both higher MPCs and lower consumption levels simultaneously through value function approximation errors that evolve with experience, without relying on borrowing constraints. Simulation results closely match empirical estimates from Ganong et al. (2024) and Malmendier and Shen (2024).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Q-learning is a reinforcement learning algorithm where an agent learns to estimate the value of actions in states, often using neural networks for function approximation in complex environments. Marginal propensity to consume (MPC) measures how much of an extra dollar of income is spent, and consumption scarring refers to persistently lower consumption after past unemployment experiences. This paper applies these concepts to economic modeling, challenging traditional rational expectations frameworks.

## [MediRec: Explainable Chinese Medication Recommendation via LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.21084) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MediRec is a novel two-stage framework that combines clinically grounded reasoning-chain distillation with reinforcement learning to improve both accuracy and interpretability of Chinese medication recommendation from electronic health records. It achieves an F1 score of 0.5813 and a Jaccard score of 0.4626 on a Chinese benchmark. This work addresses the underexplored area of Chinese medication recommendation, where existing models are mostly developed on English datasets and lack interpretability. By providing transparent reasoning, MediRec could enhance trust and adoption of AI in Chinese clinical settings, potentially improving patient safety and decision support. MediRec uses reasoning-chain distillation to transfer clinical reasoning from a teacher LLM to a student model, and then applies reinforcement learning to optimize recommendation policies. The framework is evaluated on a Chinese medication recommendation benchmark, showing strong performance and clinically plausible recommendations with transparent reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown promise in clinical decision support, but their application to Chinese medication recommendation is limited. Existing approaches often focus on coarse-grained medication code prediction on English EHR datasets, lacking interpretability. Reasoning-chain distillation is a technique to transfer LLM reasoning abilities to smaller models, and reinforcement learning can optimize sequential decision-making. This paper combines these techniques to create an explainable recommendation system for Chinese healthcare.

## [FinRpt: Benchmark and Multi-Agent Framework for Equity Research Report Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.07322) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces FinRpt, the first open-source benchmark for Equity Research Report (ERR) generation, along with a comprehensive evaluation system of 11 metrics. It also proposes FinRpt-Gen, a multi-agent LLM framework trained with supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning to automate ERR generation. This work addresses the data scarcity and lack of evaluation metrics in the emerging field of automated equity research report generation, providing a standardized benchmark and a strong baseline framework. It could accelerate research and practical adoption of LLMs in financial analysis, potentially transforming how equity research is produced. The dataset construction pipeline integrates 7 financial data types to automatically produce a high-quality ERR dataset. The multi-agent framework FinRpt-Gen is trained using supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning, and experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the benchmark and the strong performance of the framework.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Equity research reports are detailed documents that analyze a company's financials, industry, and prospects, typically including a recommendation (e.g., buy, hold, sell). They are crucial for institutional investors but are time-consuming to produce. Large language models (LLMs) have shown promise in various financial tasks, but generating complete research reports remains challenging due to data scarcity and lack of standardized evaluation. Multi-agent frameworks, where multiple LLM agents collaborate on complex tasks, have emerged as a powerful approach in other domains, and FinRpt-Gen applies this to ERR generation.

## [CAS: Bandit-Based Stabilization for Multi-Attack Adversarial Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.12265) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Calibrated Adversarial Sampling (CAS), a framework that reformulates multi-attack adversarial training as a multi-armed bandit problem, dynamically sampling a single attack per iteration to balance exploration and exploitation. This reduces training cost and mitigates parameter drift while achieving superior overall robustness. This work addresses key inefficiencies in multi-attack adversarial training, offering a scalable and principled approach that could improve the robustness of deep neural networks against diverse adversarial perturbations. It is relevant to researchers and practitioners in adversarial robustness and could influence future training strategies. CAS samples a single attack per iteration using a bandit algorithm, significantly reducing computational overhead compared to computing all attacks. The method dynamically balances exploration and exploitation to control excessive parameter drift, and the code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial training is a robust training method where models are trained on adversarially perturbed examples to improve resilience against input perturbations. Multi-attack adversarial training extends this to handle diverse attack types, but existing methods either compute all attacks (costly) or use stochastic sampling (causing parameter drift). The multi-armed bandit problem is a classic reinforcement learning problem that exemplifies the exploration-exploitation tradeoff, which CAS leverages to optimize attack selection.

## [GFlowNet Interpretability Study Reveals Architecture, Not Training, Drives Decodability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.19264) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a control-validated interpretability study of SynFlowNet, a synthesis-aware GFlowNet trained with a drug-likeness reward, using gradient saliency, counterfactual edits, and sparse autoencoders. It finds that an untrained network with the same architecture decodes physicochemical properties nearly as well as the trained policy, indicating that decodability largely reflects graph architecture and atom featurization rather than learned representations. This study addresses a critical need for transparency in AI-driven molecular design, showing that high probe scores alone should not be treated as evidence of learned chemistry. It provides a reusable control protocol for molecular-model interpretability, which could influence how interpretability is evaluated in drug discovery and other scientific applications. The study uses multiple controls including shuffled-label controls, RDKit-descriptor baselines, a scaffold-disjoint split, cross-seed stability, and an architecture-matched untrained network. Surviving conclusions include that the overcomplete autoencoder reconstructs embeddings better than a matched undercomplete baseline, chemically enriched substructure detectors emerge in individual runs, and zeroing individual features produces property-specific effects on probe decoding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: GFlowNets are a class of generative models that sample compositional objects, such as molecules, proportionally to a reward function. SynFlowNet is a GFlowNet variant that uses chemical reactions and purchasable reactants to build molecules, ensuring synthetic accessibility. Interpretability methods like sparse autoencoders are used to understand what these models learn, but this study shows that careful controls are necessary to avoid overinterpreting probe results.

## [MODEST: First Ultra-High-Resolution Multi-Optics Stereo Dataset for DoF and Defocus Deblurring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.20853) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MODEST is introduced as the first ultra-high-resolution (5472×3648px, 20MP) stereo DSLR dataset, containing 20,000 images across 50 optical configurations, with focal lengths from 28-70mm and apertures from f/22 to f/2.8, captured from multiple stereo viewpoints across 10 complex real-world scenes. The dataset also provides intrinsic and extrinsic calibration images to support calibration research. This dataset addresses a critical gap in computer vision by providing large-scale, high-resolution, real-world stereo data with systematic optical variations, enabling more robust evaluation and training of depth-of-field rendering and defocus deblurring models. It bridges the realism gap between synthetic low-resolution training data and real-camera high-resolution inference, benefiting applications in AR, VR, smartphones, and robotics. Each scene in MODEST is curated to include challenging visual elements such as reflective surfaces, transparent glass, fine-grained details, point lights, and multi-scale depth illusions. The paper evaluates several state-of-the-art DoF and defocus deblurring methods, demonstrating failure cases and limitations, and provides a comprehensive tuning analysis showing sensitivity to focal configuration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Depth-of-field (DoF) rendering and defocus deblurring are important computer vision tasks, but existing datasets often lack high resolution, real-world imagery, and systematic variation of optical parameters like focal length and aperture. Stereo imaging is increasingly common in modern applications, yet most datasets are single-view or low-resolution. MODEST aims to fill this gap by providing a comprehensive stereo dataset with professional camera optics, enabling controlled analysis of geometric and optical effects.

## [DRIFT: A Framework to Mitigate Diversity Collapse in RL Fine-Tuning for Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.12401) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces DRIFT, a framework that systematically incentivizes output diversity during reinforcement fine-tuning of generative models, addressing the curse of diversity collapse. It proposes three strategies: reward-concentrated sampling, stochastic prompt variations, and potential-based reward shaping for intra-group diversity. This work addresses a critical limitation in RL fine-tuning of generative models, where policies often collapse to a single mode, reducing output diversity. By balancing task alignment and diversity, DRIFT could enhance the versatility of image generation models, benefiting applications that require diverse candidate outputs. DRIFT achieves a 9.08% to 43.46% increase in diversity at equivalent alignment levels and a 59.65% to 65.86% increase in alignment at equivalent diversity levels, demonstrating superior Pareto dominance. The framework is designed for diffusion and flow models, which are large-scale generative models commonly used in image generation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) is used to fine-tune generative models like diffusion models to align with human preferences. However, the objective formulation often leads to diversity collapse, where the model outputs become overly concentrated, similar to mode collapse in GANs. DRIFT aims to mitigate this by incentivizing diversity during fine-tuning.

## [RooflineBench: New Benchmarking Framework for On-Device LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11506) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RooflineBench introduces a Roofline-based framework for benchmarking on-device LLMs, proposing a new metric called Relative Inference Potential to compare efficiency across hardware. The paper also reveals that sequence length significantly impacts operational intensity and identifies an efficiency trap caused by hardware heterogeneity. This framework addresses the challenge of objectively measuring theoretical performance ceilings of diverse LLM architectures on heterogeneous edge hardware, which is crucial for the growing trend of on-device AI. The insights can guide hardware-software co-design to better align neural structures with physical constraints, potentially improving efficiency and adoption of on-device LLMs. The framework unifies architectural primitives and hardware constraints through operational intensity (OI) and defines an inference-potential region. Empirical analysis across diverse compute tiers shows that OI regresses as model depth increases, and that structural refinements like Multi-head Latent Attention (MLA) can unlock latent inference potential.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: The Roofline model is a visual performance model that estimates achievable performance based on hardware limits like peak compute and memory bandwidth, using operational intensity (ops/byte) as a key metric. On-device LLMs, often small language models (SLMs), run on resource-constrained hardware, and benchmarking them is challenging due to diverse architectures and platforms. Existing benchmarks like MobileAIBench focus on task-specific testing, but RooflineBench aims to provide a more theoretical performance ceiling analysis.

## [AdaCorrection: Adaptive Offset Cache Correction for Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.13357) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AdaCorrection introduces an adaptive offset cache correction framework for Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) that estimates cache validity with lightweight spatio-temporal signals and adaptively blends cached and fresh activations at each timestep. This training-free method improves inference efficiency while maintaining generation quality, and its code has been integrated into FastCache-xDiT. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in DiT inference—the expensive iterative denoising process—by enabling efficient cache reuse without sacrificing fidelity. It offers a practical, training-free solution that can accelerate image and video generation models, benefiting the broader generative AI community. AdaCorrection corrects cache misalignment by adaptively blending cached and fresh activations based on lightweight spatio-temporal signals, computed on-the-fly without additional supervision or retraining. Experiments on image and video diffusion benchmarks show consistent improvements in generation performance with minimal computational overhead, maintaining near-original FID.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) are state-of-the-art generative models for high-fidelity image and video synthesis, but their iterative denoising process makes inference computationally expensive. Prior acceleration methods cache intermediate features across timesteps, but they rely on static reuse schedules or coarse heuristics, leading to temporal drift and cache misalignment that degrade quality. AdaCorrection addresses this by adaptively correcting cached activations, building on prior work like SmoothCache and OmniCache that explore cache reuse in diffusion models.

## [Place-it-R1: Environment-Aware MLLM Reasoning for Video Object Insertion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06140) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Place-it-R1 is an end-to-end framework that uses environment-aware MLLM reasoning to guide video diffusion for physically plausible object insertion. It introduces semantic and spatial guidance, along with Spatial Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), to improve local realism. This work addresses a key challenge in video editing: producing physically consistent object insertions, which existing diffusion methods often fail to achieve. By integrating MLLM reasoning with video diffusion, it could enhance the capabilities of video editing tools and advance the intersection of multimodal LLMs and video generation. The framework translates MLLM reasoning into two forms of guidance: semantic guidance describing the intended physical interaction, and spatial guidance providing a valid insertion region per frame. Spatial DPO uses an MLLM to rank generated candidates and introduces a region-aware preference objective that localizes physical-violation penalties to the inserted-object region. It also offers flexible and standard modes to balance environment adaptation and scene preservation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Video object insertion is a fundamental task in video editing, but existing diffusion-based methods often produce visually plausible yet physically inconsistent results. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can analyze environments and infer object-scene interactions, which can be leveraged to guide generation. Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a technique for aligning models with human preferences, and spatial variants like VistaDPO extend it to video with spatial-temporal awareness.

## [Monitoring Alone Insufficient Against Stealthy Attacks in Multi-Agent Discussions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21194) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel stealthy attack method specifically designed for discussion-monitored scenarios in multi-agent systems, demonstrating that continuous monitoring by anomaly detectors is insufficient to prevent adversarial attacks. The proposed method successfully evades detection while remaining effective, as shown through extensive experiments. This work challenges the assumption that monitoring alone can secure multi-agent discussions, a common defense mechanism in AI safety. It highlights the need for more robust security measures in multi-agent systems, which are increasingly used for complex reasoning tasks, and provides a new attack vector for the AI safety community to consider. The attack method is tailored to the discussion-monitored scenario, where anomaly detectors continuously monitor inter-agent communications and block detected adversarial messages. The paper shows that existing attacks exhibit detectable patterns under such monitoring, but the proposed method overcomes this limitation, indicating that monitoring alone does not eliminate adversarial risks.

Anomaly detectors are used to monitor these discussions for security, but this paper reveals that they are not foolproof. The research builds on prior work on adversarial attacks in multi-agent systems, which often assume no monitoring, and extends it to a more realistic monitored scenario.

SleepVLM, a vision-language model, performs sleep staging by reasoning over rendered polysomnography waveform images, outputting the sleep stage along with applicable AASM rules and an auditable rationale. This addresses the critical issue of interpretability in clinical AI, providing a direct basis for auditing individual predictions, which could enhance trust and facilitate clinical adoption of automatic sleep staging systems. ## [MOON3.0: Reasoning-Aware Multimodal Learning for E-commerce](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00513) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MOON3.0, the first reasoning-aware MLLM-based model for product representation learning, which explicitly models fine-grained product attributes using MLLM reasoning capabilities. It also releases a large-scale multimodal e-commerce benchmark called MBE3.0. This work addresses a key limitation of current MLLMs in e-commerce, which often encode product information into global embeddings and miss fine-grained attributes. By explicitly modeling these attributes, MOON3.0 could improve downstream tasks like product search and recommendation, benefiting the e-commerce ecosystem. MOON3.0 employs a multi-head modality fusion module, a joint contrastive and reinforcement learning framework, and a fine-grained residual enhancement module to address challenges like long-context reasoning dilution and attention attenuation. The model demonstrates state-of-the-art zero-shot performance on both the new benchmark and public datasets.

Supervised fine-tuning (SFT) is a common training step for LLMs, but it can lead to rigid imitation rather than effective reasoning strategies.

## [Study Questions SNIP's Fine-Grained Cross-Modal Alignment in Symbolic Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08324) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper empirically investigates whether SNIP, a contrastive pre-training model for latent space optimization in symbolic regression, achieves fine-grained cross-modal alignment. The authors find that cross-modal alignment does not improve during optimization and remains too coarse for effective symbolic space search. This work highlights a critical gap between the promise and practice of multi-modal latent space optimization for symbolic regression, potentially influencing future LSO designs. It underscores the need for fine-grained alignment, which could improve the efficiency and effectiveness of symbolic regression methods. The experiments show that (1) cross-modal alignment does not improve during optimization even as fitness increases, and (2) the alignment learned by SNIP is too coarse to conduct principled search in the symbolic space. The paper draws parallels to CLIP, which is known to have coarse-grained alignment.

**Background**: Symbolic regression (SR) aims to discover mathematical expressions from data, traditionally using genetic programming (GP) for combinatorial search. Latent Space Optimization (LSO) methods use neural encoders to map symbolic expressions into continuous spaces, enabling continuous optimization. SNIP, inspired by CLIP, introduces a multi-modal approach aligning symbolic and numeric encoders in a shared latent space, but this relies on fine-grained alignment, which CLIP literature suggests is typically coarse.

## [Graph-Based Soft Prompting Boosts LLM Reasoning on Incomplete Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12503) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces GraSP, a graph-based soft prompting framework that uses a GNN to encode subgraphs into soft prompts, enabling LLMs to reason over subgraph structures rather than explicit edge paths. This approach achieves state-of-the-art performance on three of four multi-hop KBQA benchmarks. This work addresses a critical limitation of existing multi-hop KBQA methods that rely on explicit edge traversal, which fails when knowledge graphs are incomplete. By shifting to subgraph-level reasoning, it improves robustness and opens new possibilities for combining LLMs with structured knowledge, potentially benefiting applications like question answering and fact verification. The framework employs a two-stage paradigm: a lightweight LLM first uses soft prompts to identify question-relevant entities and relations, then a more powerful LLM generates evidence-aware answers. This reduces computational cost while preserving performance. The code is available at https://github.com/Wangshuaiia/GraSP.

**Background**: Knowledge Base Question Answering (KBQA) grounds LLM generation in knowledge graphs to reduce hallucinations. Multi-hop KBQA typically requires traversing explicit reasoning paths, which is fragile to missing edges. Graph-based soft prompting encodes subgraph structures into prompts, allowing LLMs to leverage richer structural context without explicit path traversal.

## [Formalizing Vibe-Testing: A Structured Approach to LLM Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14137) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper studies how users informally evaluate LLMs through 'vibe-testing' and formalizes it into a two-part process: personalizing what to test and how to judge responses. It introduces a proof-of-concept pipeline that generates personalized prompts and uses user-aware subjective criteria, showing that this approach can change which model is preferred in coding benchmarks. This work addresses a gap between benchmark scores and real-world usefulness by providing a systematic way to analyze the widely used but understudied practice of vibe-testing. It offers a bridge between informal user experience and formal evaluation, potentially influencing future LLM evaluation methodologies and helping developers better align models with user needs. The paper analyzes two empirical resources: a survey of user evaluation practices and a collection of in-the-wild model comparison reports from blogs and social media. Experiments on coding benchmarks show that combining personalized prompts and user-aware evaluation can change which model is preferred, highlighting the role of vibe-testing in practice.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: LLM evaluation traditionally relies on benchmarks like accuracy or human preference, but these often fail to capture real-world usefulness. Vibe-testing refers to informal, experience-based evaluation where users compare models on tasks relevant to their own workflows. This paper aims to formalize this practice to enable systematic analysis and reproducibility.

## [IConFace: Fine-Grained Identity Conditioning for Reference-Aware Face Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.02814) ⭐️ 7.0/10

IConFace introduces a unified framework that conditions face restoration on up to three same-identity reference images, using a hybrid concat backbone and an identity pathway to preserve localized identity details. The paper also presents a human-audited benchmark to measure the preservation of persistent localized identity features. This work addresses the underdetermined nature of severe face degradation by leveraging reference images, improving identity consistency and detail recovery. It offers a unified model that works both with and without references, potentially advancing practical face restoration applications in security, forensics, and media. The framework uses a hybrid concat backbone that retains degraded and reference observations as dense visual tokens, an identity pathway for compact multi-reference guidance, and a degraded-structure pathway for full-field and local-residual memories. IConFace achieves leading reference compatibility under severe degradation and the highest preservation rate on the new benchmark, while also performing well on five blind-restoration benchmarks without references.

Traditional methods rely on generative priors but may miss persistent localized traits. Reference-aware restoration uses additional same-identity images to supply missing evidence, while the degraded image anchors the target structure. This paper builds on that idea with asymmetric identity-structure conditioning.

## [HARECast: Stabilizing Attention Response for Reliable Precipitation Nowcasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13181) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper identifies cross-sample instability of attention-response energy as a previously underexplored source of forecasting unreliability in precipitation nowcasting, and proposes HARECast, a head-wise attention response energy-regulated framework that stabilizes attention responses through group-wise regularization. HARECast achieves state-of-the-art performance on SEVIR and MeteoNet benchmarks. This work addresses a critical yet overlooked aspect of attention-based models in weather forecasting, potentially improving the reliability of short-term precipitation predictions. By providing both empirical evidence and theoretical analysis, it offers a generic framework applicable to both unimodal and multimodal nowcasting architectures, which could benefit operational meteorology and disaster preparedness. HARECast explicitly models head-wise attention-response energy and stabilizes it via a group-wise regularization objective that reduces cross-sample fluctuations. The framework is instantiated in a standard forecasting pipeline with reconstruction branches and a diffusion-based predictor, and evaluated on SEVIR and MeteoNet benchmarks, achieving state-of-the-art results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Precipitation nowcasting is the short-term forecasting of precipitation (up to a few hours) with high spatial and temporal resolution, which is challenging due to the localized and rapidly evolving nature of atmospheric dynamics. Attention mechanisms, especially self-attention and multi-head attention, have been widely adopted in deep learning models for nowcasting to capture complex spatiotemporal dependencies. However, the stability of attention responses across samples has been largely ignored, and this paper shows that cross-sample instability of attention-response energy can lead to larger prediction errors.

## [Quantization Breaks Recourse: New Metrics and Mitigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17160) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces counterfactual sensitivity under quantization, proposing two metrics—Validity Drop (VD) and Counterfactual Recourse Gap (CRG)—to measure how quantization degrades algorithmic recourse, and a method called Counterfactual-Faithful Quantization (CFQ) to mitigate recourse failures. This work highlights a critical but overlooked issue: model quantization, while preserving accuracy, can silently break algorithmic recourse, undermining fairness and transparency in decision systems. It provides both metrics and a practical solution, benefiting researchers and practitioners in model compression and explainable AI. CFQ is compatible with standard LSQ/PACT-style quantizers and mixed-precision policies, and can be instantiated as a training-free calibration procedure for post-training quantization. Experiments on Adult, German Credit, and COMPAS show that CFQ reduces VD/CRG from 0.121/0.162 to 0.061/0.071 on Adult at matched accuracy and bit budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Model quantization reduces memory and latency by lowering numerical precision, but is typically evaluated only on predictive accuracy. Algorithmic recourse provides actionable steps for individuals to reverse unfavorable automated decisions, relying on counterfactual explanations. This paper bridges these fields, showing that quantization can invalidate recourse actions even when accuracy is preserved.

## [Spectral Integrated Gradients Improve Feature Attribution via SVD Paths](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19607) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Spectral Integrated Gradients (SIG), a novel method that constructs integration paths using singular value decomposition (SVD) of the baseline-to-input difference. By activating singular components from largest to smallest, SIG achieves a coarse-to-fine feature attribution, producing cleaner and less noisy attribution maps. SIG addresses a known limitation of standard Integrated Gradients, which introduces all features simultaneously and accumulates noisy gradients. This improvement can enhance the reliability of explainable AI (XAI) in critical applications like image classification, potentially leading to better model debugging and trust. The method is evaluated across diverse image classification datasets, showing improved quantitative performance over existing path-based attribution methods. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/leekwoon/sig/.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Integrated Gradients (IG) is a widely used feature attribution method that satisfies axioms like sensitivity and implementation invariance. However, the choice of integration path significantly affects attribution quality; the standard straight-line path can accumulate noise. SVD is a matrix factorization technique that reveals the most important directions of variation, which SIG leverages to order the introduction of features from global to fine.

The framework employs a two-stream fusion method and a joint loss function to address challenges like high-dimensional heterogeneous data and inter-class spectral similarity. This work advances land-cover classification in remote sensing by improving the representation of spatial-spectral features from multispectral point clouds. The proposed method and datasets could benefit applications in environmental monitoring, urban planning, and autonomous navigation, setting a new benchmark for future research. The framework consists of two parallel streams: one extracts position-encoded global spectral features using fusion self-attention, while the other uses multikernel point convolution and feature aggregation attention for spectral-guided geometric features. A residual attention fusion block integrates the most informative features, and a joint loss function improves learning on unbalanced and inter-class similar samples. The codes and datasets will be released on GitHub.

The AudioDER dataset spans sound, speech, and music, and is constructed using a three-stage pipeline: acoustic redundancy filtering, unified multiple-choice QA conversion with chain-of-thought generation, and quality verification. ## [Delta-Diffusion: Longitudinal PET Synthesis via Poisson Diffusion Bridge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22216) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Delta-Diffusion introduces a conditional Poisson Diffusion Bridge (PDB) to model longitudinal brain amyloid-PET trajectories, anchoring generation to baseline PET scans and using a Diffusion Transformer with adaptive modulation. Validated on 542 subjects across two cohorts, it outperforms state-of-the-art methods in capturing amyloid progression. This work addresses a critical challenge in medical imaging: accurately synthesizing longitudinal PET scans to track disease progression without repeated costly and radiation-heavy scans. It could enable more accessible and safer monitoring of Alzheimer's disease progression, benefiting clinical research and drug development. The PDB formulation replaces Gaussian noise with a physically-grounded Poisson perturbation, and a volume-of-interest balanced objective emphasizes sparse, high-risk amyloid regions. The model uses adaptive scale-shift modulation to calibrate synthesis with elapsed clinical interval and structural MRI context.

Diffusion bridges offer a way to condition generation on a starting point, and Poisson perturbations align with the heteroscedastic noise characteristics of PET imaging.

A new study analyzing 21.4 million scientific abstracts from 2010-2025 finds a 48% rise in militaristic language in OpenAlex and 32% in PubMed, with acceleration after 2019. A within-subject experiment (N=801) shows war framing reduces credibility, funding willingness, and policy support. This trend may erode public trust in science and reduce funding and policy support, affecting how scientific findings are communicated and received. The rise in militaristic language is conflict-aligned at country and annual scales (r=0.77-0.84), with the Global South showing the fastest increase. Social sciences lead in level, while engineering and computer science lead in growth; COVID and post-2022 LLM eras accelerated the rise and narrowed the language gap between native and non-native English-speaking countries.

The paper reveals that Best-of-N (BoN) TTS evaluation is confounded by ASR family alignment, where verifier rankings vary across Whisper, wav2vec 2.0, and HuBERT evaluators. It proposes cross-family rank ensembles (rank averaging and conjunctive max-rank) that reduce WER by 12% relative over F5-TTS at N=10. The proposed cross-evaluator triangulation offers a more reliable default for reporting BoN TTS performance, potentially influencing future evaluation standards in the speech community. The study uses LibriSpeech-PC with F5-TTS and finds that same-family verifier-evaluator pairs recover more oracle headroom than cross-family pairs, suggesting lineage-level coupling. The best ensemble achieves a 12% relative WER reduction over F5-TTS at N=10 without degrading automatic similarity or quality metrics.

This paper addresses this confound by proposing cross-family rank ensembles and recommending cross-evaluator triangulation.

## [Amplitude Gating Improves Tool-Structured LLM Outputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11183) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Amplitude Gating (AG), an inference-time intervention that modulates activation magnitudes in feed-forward networks to improve structured outputs in tool-using LLMs, without retraining weights. It also proposes a paired evaluation protocol called Per-Sample Fix-Harm Evaluation (PFHE) to report fixes, harms, and preserved cases. This work addresses a practical problem in LLM tool use, where small format or function-call errors can invalidate responses. By offering a lightweight, inference-time method and a more nuanced evaluation protocol, it could help improve reliability of LLM agents without costly retraining, benefiting developers and users of tool-using LLMs. On a cross-position union passing source-alignment audit, an exploratory offline mixed selector raised the Qwen3.5-9B tool-route micro-average from 38.66% to 42.92% (+4.27 points), and two Hermes function-call endpoints improved by +7.64 and +7.62 points. However, the method showed model- and task-specific effects, with no universal improvement, and the authors caution against deployment without strict fallback.

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used as tool-using agents, where they must generate structured outputs like function calls. Feed-forward networks (FFNs) in transformers use activation functions like SwiGLU, and inference-time interventions modify activations to steer model behavior without retraining. Previous methods like Orthogonal Residual Projection (ORP) altered weight directions, causing regressions, whereas Amplitude Gating preserves directions and only adjusts magnitudes.

## [Lecture Notes Unify Uncertainty Representation for Optimal Decision-Making](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14407) ⭐️ 7.0/10

These lecture notes develop a unified decision-theoretic framework linking an agent's objective and knowledge to the necessary uncertainty representation for optimal action. They cover risk-neutral agents needing posterior distributions, risk-averse agents using prediction sets with worst-case rules, and three approaches for epistemic uncertainty. This work provides a principled foundation for uncertainty quantification in decision-making, which is crucial for AI/ML and signal processing systems that must act under uncertainty. By clarifying which uncertainty representation is sufficient for optimality, it can guide the design of more reliable and trustworthy autonomous systems. The notes show that a risk-neutral agent requires the posterior distribution over the state, while a risk-averse agent can rely on a prediction set and a worst-case decision rule without loss of optimality. For unknown environments, they identify calibration, credal sets with distributionally robust optimization, and Bayesian inference as complementary approaches to epistemic uncertainty.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Uncertainty quantification distinguishes between aleatory uncertainty (inherent randomness) and epistemic uncertainty (lack of knowledge that could be reduced with more data). Decision theory models rational choice under uncertainty using expected utility and probability. This work bridges these fields by showing how the decision objective and agent's knowledge determine the appropriate uncertainty representation.

## [Perturbation Grounded Selection Fails to Beat Matched Control in VLM Test-Time Scaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01207) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Perturbation Grounded Selection (Pgs), a training-free method that scores candidate answers by re-deriving them under label-preserving input perturbations, and evaluates it against a format-matched control (MatchedCtrl) across multiple benchmarks. The results show that Pgs does not significantly outperform MatchedCtrl, indicating that perturbation consistency is not a reliable selection signal once decoding format is controlled. This finding challenges the effectiveness of perturbation-based selection methods for vision-language models (VLMs) and highlights the importance of controlling for decoding format in test-time scaling evaluations. It suggests that previously reported gains over chain-of-thought majority voting may be overstated, guiding future research toward more robust selection mechanisms. The study uses Qwen and LLaVA-OneVision models across TextVQA, MATH-Vision, MMMU, and ViLP benchmarks. Pgs shows up to +31.8 points improvement over plain majority voting on TextVQA, but MatchedCtrl tracks or exceeds Pgs within noise on every benchmark, and no Qwen category shows a significant gain over this control.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time scaling improves reasoning by sampling multiple candidate solutions and selecting among them, but this approach transfers poorly to vision-language models (VLMs) because self-verification often fails to distinguish image-grounded answers from confident guesses based on language priors. Perturbation Grounded Selection (Pgs) attempts to address this by using input perturbations to ground verification in the image, but the paper's negative results suggest that the decoding format, rather than the perturbation, is the key factor in selection performance.

## [PICopilot: LLM Agentic Framework for Photonic IC Design Script Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01791) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PICopilot is introduced as the first LLM-based agentic framework that automatically generates design scripts for photonic integrated circuits (PICs) from natural language instructions. It employs a multi-agent architecture with a feedback mechanism and a tailored retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) pipeline, successfully completing all 48 benchmark tasks and outperforming other LLM-based approaches, including solving 21 more tasks than GPT-5 with a general RAG pipeline. This framework addresses the growing productivity gap in script-based PIC design, which is becoming more prevalent due to its flexibility and maintainability but requires significant programming and API expertise. By automating script generation, PICopilot could lower the barrier for designers and accelerate PIC development, potentially impacting the broader electronic design automation (EDA) ecosystem. PICopilot's multi-agent architecture includes specialized agents for task decomposition, script generation, and verification, with a feedback loop that iteratively refines outputs. The RAG pipeline is specifically designed for PIC design APIs and domain knowledge, and the framework achieves high success rates without substantial extra latency or cost compared to baseline LLMs.

**Background**: Photonic integrated circuits (PICs) are increasingly designed using script-based methods instead of graphical user interfaces (GUIs) for better flexibility, portability, and maintainability. However, this shift requires designers to master tool APIs and programming, which is time-consuming and less intuitive. Large language models (LLMs) have shown promise in code generation, and agentic frameworks that combine LLMs with tools and feedback loops are emerging in electronic design automation (EDA) to automate complex design tasks.

The Federal Communications Commission (FCC) voted 2-1 along party lines on August 6, 2026, to eliminate the 39% national ownership cap on broadcast TV stations, replacing it with a case-by-case review of transactions. This regulatory change could lead to increased media consolidation, allowing large companies to acquire more broadcast TV stations nationwide. It may also trigger legal challenges, as critics argue the FCC lacks authority to alter a congressionally mandated cap. The vote was split along party lines, with Republican commissioners in favor and the Democratic commissioner opposed. The decision does not affect local ownership limits within markets, and it does not resolve an ongoing antitrust lawsuit that paused the Nexstar-Tegna deal.

## [Auditable LLM Harness Improves Exact-Score Football Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05030) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces an auditable LLM harness that combines dynamic Poisson models with LLM contextual reasoning for exact-score football match prediction, documented across four iterative versions (V1-V4). On a replay of the first 150 matches of the 2025-26 English Premier League, V4 achieved 14.7% Top-1 and 30.7% Top-3 exact-score accuracy, improving over V1's 10.0% and 26.7%. This work addresses a key limitation of LLMs—their lack of calibrated probability outputs—by integrating them with statistical models in an auditable way. It offers a novel hybrid architecture that could improve sports analytics and other domains requiring both contextual understanding and probabilistic accuracy. The harness defines input semantics, supplies pre-match evidence, and constrains the LLM to an inspectable reasoning route. V4 added shared first-breakthrough and post-goal cascade judgments, time-aware stopping, and deterministic tail candidates, increasing candidate coverage from 77.3% to 84.7%, though no added tail candidate became a Top-3 exact hit.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Football score prediction often uses dynamic Poisson-family models like Dixon-Coles, which estimate team strength and expected goals but lack contextual understanding. LLMs can reason about tactical matchups and motivation but are not calibrated probability engines. This paper combines both through an auditable harness, documenting iterative improvements and negative findings.

## [FBID: Adaptive Personalized Federated Learning for Robust OOD Attack Detection in IoT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04073) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes FBID, a novel adaptive personalized federated learning framework that uses a contextual multi-armed bandit at the server to dynamically regulate each client's local training intensity and a trust-based blending mechanism to interpolate between global and local models. Experiments on the CICIoT2023 dataset show that FBID improves individual client OOD detection rate by up to 7.66% and F1-score by up to 5.08% relative to the strongest stable baseline. This work addresses a critical limitation of existing personalized federated learning methods—over-personalization leading to degraded out-of-distribution attack detection—which is vital for robust IoT security. By introducing server-side personalization control, FBID offers a more reliable approach for detecting novel attacks in heterogeneous IoT environments, potentially improving the practical deployment of federated intrusion detection systems. FBID employs a contextual multi-armed bandit at the server to adaptively adjust each client's local training intensity based on observed behavior and update quality. It also introduces a trust-based blending mechanism to compute client-specific interpolation coefficients between the global and local models, preserving global knowledge while allowing beneficial local specialization. The experiments were conducted on the CICIoT2023 dataset under heterogeneous client distributions and OOD stress-test settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning (FL) enables training a shared model across multiple parties without centralizing data, but traditional FL produces a single global model that may not adapt well to heterogeneous client data. Personalized federated learning (PFL) addresses this by adapting models to each client, yet existing methods often rely on client-side self-adjustment, which can lead to over-personalization and poor performance on out-of-distribution (OOD) samples—data that differ from the training distribution. Contextual multi-armed bandits are a decision-making framework where an agent chooses among options (arms) based on context to maximize reward, which FBID leverages for adaptive control.

## [Spatiotemporal Graph Transformer Enhances Edge Traffic Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04075) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a spatiotemporal graph Transformer framework that integrates graph neural networks for spatial correlations and Transformer-based self-attention for long-range temporal dependencies, specifically designed for traffic forecasting in edge computing. Experiments on a real-world cellular network dataset show it outperforms recurrent graph baselines like GCN-RNN, GCN-LSTM, and GCN-GRU across multiple forecasting horizons. Accurate traffic forecasting is critical for proactive resource management in edge computing, where service demand varies dynamically. This work demonstrates the potential of combining graph neural networks with attention mechanisms to build more intelligent and adaptive edge systems, potentially reducing overload risk and improving efficiency. The framework decouples spatial representation learning from temporal reasoning, which is an effective mechanism for large-scale spatiotemporal modeling. The experiments use a real-world cellular network dataset and compare against recurrent graph-based baselines, but the abstract does not provide specific performance metrics or details on the dataset size.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Traffic forecasting in edge computing involves predicting service demand across different regions and time steps, which is challenging due to spatial correlations and long-range temporal dependencies. Graph neural networks (GNNs) are commonly used to model spatial relationships, while recurrent neural networks (RNNs) like LSTM and GRU capture temporal dynamics. Transformers, based on self-attention, have recently shown promise in capturing long-range dependencies in sequential data. This paper combines these approaches to address the limitations of existing recurrent methods under non-stationary conditions.

<a id="item-295"></a>
## [Interpretable Fuzzy Inference for UAV Target Tracking Using Bounding-Box Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04121) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces an interpretable fuzzy-inference framework for continuous yaw control in UAVs, using low-dimensional features from YOLO bounding boxes (centroid, area, aspect ratio) without explicit geometric modeling. A Takagi-Sugeno model with 27 rules achieves a test-set mean absolute error of 0.140° and 100% accuracy within ±3° and ±5° thresholds. This work offers a transparent, data-efficient alternative to deep learning for UAV vision-based control, addressing resource constraints and interpretability needs in edge robotics. It could enable more reliable and understandable autonomous guidance in cooperative aerial-ground systems, potentially impacting applications like search-and-rescue or delivery. The framework uses a Mamdani baseline with shoulder-triangle-shoulder partitions, followed by a first-order Takagi-Sugeno model with parameters derived from training-set quantiles. Evaluation on 6,169 labeled VICON samples shows directional consistency of 90.254% between image-plane displacement and yaw sign.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Fuzzy inference systems map inputs to outputs using linguistic rules and fuzzy sets, with Mamdani systems using fuzzy outputs and Takagi-Sugeno systems using crisp functions, making them interpretable and suitable for expert knowledge. YOLO is a popular object detection algorithm that outputs bounding boxes, which can provide geometric features like centroid and area without complex 3D reconstruction.

## [TRNet: Topography-Guided Multimodal Paddy Rice Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04154) ⭐️ 6.0/10

TRNet introduces a topography-guided network that integrates RGB imagery, DEM, and slope data for paddy rice segmentation, achieving IoU of 85.10% and 80.68% on internal and held-out test sets, surpassing the Dual-Encoder U-Net by 9.15 and 18.83 percentage points respectively. This work addresses a critical challenge in remote sensing: accurately mapping paddy rice in mountainous regions where terrain causes spectral confusion. By leveraging coarse topography as a contextual prior, TRNet demonstrates a promising direction for improving agricultural monitoring in complex landscapes, potentially benefiting food security assessments and precision agriculture. TRNet comprises two main components: Topographic Energy-Spectral Rectification (TESR) and Topography-guided Paddy Structure Decoder (TPSD). TESR applies terrain-conditioned low-frequency modulation and asymmetric high-frequency regulation to suppress steep-slope clutter, while TPSD combines semantic, boundary, and interior cues with coarse terrain context. The method uses 0.5-m GaoJing-1 RGB imagery and 5-m TanDEM-X DEM data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Paddy rice segmentation from very-high-resolution imagery is challenging in mountainous regions because terrain alters optical appearance and increases confusion with similar vegetation. Traditional methods often rely solely on optical data, which is insufficient in such complex terrains. TRNet addresses this by integrating digital elevation models (DEM) and derived slope data, using topography as a guiding context to improve segmentation accuracy. The GaoJing-1 (SuperView-1) satellite constellation provides 0.5-m resolution imagery, making it suitable for detailed agricultural mapping.

MERaLiON-GR is a speech gender recognition model that fine-tunes the MERaLiON-SpeechEncoder-2 with Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) and appends a multi-scale ECAPA-TDNN with attention pooling. It outperforms state-of-the-art models like Vox-Profile and a large Audio-LLM across English and Southeast Asian languages. This work demonstrates the value of dedicated speech models for paralinguistic tasks like gender recognition, especially for under-resourced Southeast Asian languages. It highlights the effectiveness of parameter-efficient fine-tuning (LoRA) in adapting large speech encoders to specific tasks, potentially enabling more accessible and efficient deployment. The model performs binary classification (female/male) and was evaluated on Singaporean and Southeast Asian languages including English, Chinese, Malay, Tamil, Thai, Vietnamese, Indonesian, and Khmer. It surpasses baselines in both full-utterance and segment-level evaluation modes, underscoring strong cross-lingual generalization.

LoRA is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that reduces the number of trainable parameters while maintaining performance.

<a id="item-298"></a>
## [ProverbIT Benchmark Reveals LLMs' Weakness in Multiple-Choice Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04670) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces ProverbIT, a new benchmark of 100 Italian proverbs, and evaluates 13 frontier models, including Large Reasoning Models (LRMs), across three tasks: completion, multiple-choice with correct answers, and multiple-choice without correct answers. Results show that while models perform well on completion, their performance drops dramatically on multiple-choice without correct answers, even for state-of-the-art reasoning models. This benchmark highlights a significant limitation in current LLMs: they rely on memorized patterns rather than deep semantic understanding of culturally embedded expressions. The findings could influence future model training and evaluation, emphasizing the need for more robust reasoning capabilities in figurative language comprehension. The benchmark includes 100 multiple-choice questions, and the evaluation covers 13 models, including LRMs. Chain-of-Thought analysis of two LRMs reveals a bias toward selecting literal synonyms and a tendency to mention correct proverb endings during reasoning without recognizing their absence from the options.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown remarkable performance in many NLP tasks, but their understanding of culturally embedded expressions like proverbs is not well understood. Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) are a newer class of models designed to improve reasoning by planning and verifying steps, unlike traditional LLMs that generate text autoregressively. ProverbIT is part of the CALAMITA challenge, aiming to evaluate LLMs beyond simple pattern matching.

<a id="item-299"></a>
## [IMFACT: Counterfactual Explanations for Time Series via IMF Substitution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04777) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces IMFACT, a model-agnostic framework that generates counterfactual explanations for time series classifiers by substituting intrinsic mode functions (IMFs) obtained from empirical mode decomposition. It evaluates six IMF-selection strategies and a multi-NUN cycling extension on two UCR benchmarks, with the variance-based strategy using three NUNs outperforming two baseline methods on reliability and plausibility. This work addresses a critical limitation in counterfactual explanation for time series: perturbing raw features can destroy temporal structure and produce physically implausible results. By operating in the decomposition space, IMFACT offers a more principled approach that could improve trust and interpretability in applications like fault detection and biological signal analysis. The method uses Empirical Mode Decomposition (EMD) to split a signal into IMFs, then progressively substitutes selected IMFs with those from a Nearest Unlike Neighbour (NUN) until the classifier flips to the target class. The evaluation is limited to two UCR datasets (FaultDetectionA, FruitFlies), and the cycling across three NUNs yields the best proximity across both datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Counterfactual explanations answer 'what changes to the input would lead to a different outcome?' and are widely used for interpreting classification models. For time series, naive perturbations in raw feature space often break temporal dependencies. Empirical Mode Decomposition (EMD) is a data-driven technique that decomposes a signal into intrinsic mode functions (IMFs) that preserve local frequency characteristics, making it a suitable space for generating plausible perturbations.

## [Privacy-Aware Motion Reasoning for Classroom Incident Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05115) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This pilot study introduces a novel hybrid benchmark combining generative CCTV-style videos with real-world pose data, and a lightweight motion-reasoning framework that uses hierarchical kinematic representations and knowledge distillation to recognize classroom incidents efficiently and privacy-aware. This work addresses an underexplored application of computer vision in privacy-sensitive environments, offering a benchmark and method that could enable safer classrooms without compromising privacy. It demonstrates that lightweight models can outperform larger baselines, potentially reducing computational barriers for real-world deployment. The framework first constructs hierarchical kinematic representations of human actions, then distills multi-order kinematic reasoning from a large teacher model into a much smaller single-order student, enabling efficient per-person inference. Experiments show the model outperforms substantially larger baselines at less than one-tenth of their computational cost, with stronger out-of-domain motion reasoning and zero-shot synthetic-to-real generalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Classroom incident recognition from CCTV-style data is challenging due to privacy concerns and the need for efficient models. Hierarchical kinematic representations capture motion direction, speed, acceleration, and intensity, which are often more discriminative than pose alone. Knowledge distillation is a model compression technique where a small student model learns to mimic a large teacher model, enabling efficient inference. Synthetic data generation helps create privacy-safe training datasets for rare events.

## [Review Text Predicts Rating Changes on Rental Platforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14053) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new study using a two-wave panel of over 200,000 U.S. short-term rental listings across 34 markets finds that a prespecified sentiment index based on review text predicts small but precise upward movements in displayed ratings over the following year. The effect is robust to host fixed effects and not driven by any single market. This finding suggests that platform rating aggregation discards information contained in review text, which could inform the design of more informative reputation displays. It highlights the potential of text as a leading indicator for reputation dynamics, which may be relevant for other online platforms facing similar rating inflation issues. The sentiment index is a dictionary-based instrument that has not been validated against human judgment, a limitation the author explicitly acknowledges. The study locked the model and falsification checks in advance and reserved half of the markets for a single confirmatory estimation, enhancing methodological rigor.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: Rating systems on accommodation platforms often suffer from score inflation, where nearly all listings have near-perfect ratings, reducing their discriminative power. This study treats review text and ratings as parallel channels that aggregate guest experience at different speeds, using a two-wave panel to examine whether text predicts future rating changes. The methodology includes prespecified sentiment analysis and confirmatory estimation on held-out markets to ensure robustness.

## [Multi-Agent Pun Translation Wins CLEF JOKER 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.06506) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a three-stage multi-agent system for translating English puns into French, combining contrastive learning, phonetic-semantic embeddings, and a generator-discriminator framework. The system achieved first and second place in the CLEF JOKER 2025 Task 2 competition, evaluated manually by native French speakers. This work demonstrates a novel approach to a long-standing challenge in machine translation, showing that linguistically-informed techniques can outperform literal translation for humor. It bridges translation studies and computational linguistics, potentially influencing future MT systems for creative and culturally nuanced content. The methodology uses a three-stage pipeline: baseline with multiple LLMs and contrastive learning feedback, a guided chain-of-thought with combined phonetic-semantic embeddings, and a multi-agent generator-discriminator for iterative refinement. The system was evaluated manually by expert native French speakers, achieving top rankings in the CLEF JOKER 2025 Task 2.

**Background**: CLEF JOKER is a shared task series at the CLEF conference focused on non-literal language, with Task 2 specifically targeting the translation of English punning jokes into French. Pun translation is notoriously difficult because it requires preserving both meaning and phonetic similarity across languages. The proposed system leverages large language models and specialized embeddings to capture these nuances, moving beyond literal translation.

## [Human-in-the-Loop Atlas Segmentation for 3D Assets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17824) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new human-in-the-loop pipeline segments 3D assets into a 2D parameterized atlas using SAM 2 and Label Studio, with a greedy set cover view selection strategy. The method is demonstrated on eight cultural heritage objects, producing usable segmented atlases for downstream tasks. This pipeline addresses the challenge of application-dependent 3D segmentation by combining automated AI with user control, making it practical for game and XR content workflows. It enables efficient segment-wise material assignment, style transfer, and semantic labeling, potentially reducing manual effort in production. The pipeline uses a greedy set cover algorithm to select a compact set of rendered views, then back-projects SAM 2 masks onto the UV parameterization to create a unified atlas. Evaluation on eight objects revealed recurring manual corrections needed for fine structures, cavities, and weak appearance boundaries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 6, 04:00

**Background**: 3D asset segmentation is often application-dependent, requiring user control. SAM 2 is a foundation model for promptable segmentation in images and videos, while UV parameterization maps 3D surfaces to 2D planes. Greedy set cover algorithms efficiently select a minimal subset covering all elements.
